queued, waiting on someone, or still to verify follows.

Build 195 (released, below), chosen 2026-11-20 (the user: "1-3 ei muuta"): **12** follow-up (below), **109** POTA and WWFF spots on the display (Robin Bishop G5OUT; SOTA left out until the SOTA Management Team approves, its API terms), and the KPA1500's OPERATE readback in the log (Rich Yost N2RY's build 190 log, the ideas list). Promised: Sergio the power next build, Robin the spots "when it is in a build", Rich the log line. **109**: Controls › View › Park spots, **POTA** and **WWFF** (off by default, MobilePotaSpots / MobileWwffSpots): each list read once a minute while a radio is connected (MobileEnginePota.cpp, api.pota.app/spot/activator and spots.wwff.co/static/spots.json, the app's User-Agent; OKSASDR_POTA_SPOTS_URL / OKSASDR_WWFF_SPOTS_URL for stand-ins), a spot not seen before to the radio as the desktop's PotaClient does (source POTA / WWFF, "<ref> <park> <mode>" as the comment, POTA's expire / WWFF's 30 min from its time as the lifetime, PotaSpotColor #FFFF00 / WwffSpotColor #66FF66), through one queueRadioSpot shared with the cluster (its dedup: a call on the same frequency once a lifetime); an activator's park kept by call, so noteSpotCall (tuneToSpot) gives the Log tab lastSpotSig / lastSpotRef from a park spot or from any spot of a listed activator; the Log tab's spotted-call button fills Their park with the call. The line under the buttons: "Listed now (07:12 UTC): POTA 15, WWFF 2." or why a list was not read. Tested on the FLEX-6600M by the real buttons: the live lists (POTA 13-16, WWFF 1-2 at the time) on the radio; BA4SUA (POTA CN-1601, 14.0249 CW) tapped on 20 m: tuned, CW, the Log tab's call and Their park POTA; stand-in lists: a spot of OH0TEST from another source got POTA OH-0001 from the list, OH0WWF/P (WWFF OHFF-0001, FT8) tuned and filled; rendered at 393x852, 375x667, 1180x820 and --font-px 17; the test spots taken off the radio (spottest clearparks), the slice put back to 14.074 DIGU. Found with it: a spot tapped near the view's edge in DIGU retuned 2 kHz off (the spot's own tap tuned, the view followed the slice, and the display's tap no longer saw the tag under the finger): the display's tap now judges the overlays as they lay at the press. **The KPA1500's OPERATE readback**: a command not shown in the 3 s wait now logs what the readings say ("reads STANDBY, answering"), then "shows operate after 6.2 s, later than the wait" or "still does not show operate 33 s after the command"; tested against kpa1500-mock.py --operate-delay 5 / -1 (new). Docs: the manual (View › Park spots, the Log tab, the KPA500's and KAT500's Power, the experimental note), pictures park-spots (new), kpa500-controls (new), tab-kpa500, kat500-controls; update-manual-screenshots.sh no longer stops at "changes asked: 0" (grep -c under pipefail); README, privacy (the park lists), TestFlight and Android notes (beta 106).

**12** follow-up (found 2026-11-20 at night in Sergio Landoni LU7YS's log of build 194 on his real KPA500 and KAT500; ready in the working tree, not committed): *the KAT500 off* and *the KPA500's and KAT500's power*. The KPA500 worked on his devices (487 W, SWR 1.20, Standby / Operate, band following). The KAT500 answered its identity and firmware (02.16) but every reading stayed "–" and the controls dim: it was off (its own power, PS0), and off it answers only I, RV, SN and PS, which the model then asks every 5 s; the OFF badge showed only once a mode was read, so nothing said why. The log's "stopped answering" / "answers 02.16" every few seconds is the same: the 5 s reads land just past the model's 5 s freshness. Now: OFF in the badge and "The tuner is off" with what turns it on; with Control on a **Power On / Off** row (PS1 / PS0 through the model's setPower, confirmed, not while transmitting, the one command that works with the tuner off; the desktop applet has the same ON/OFF); readings from before it went off not shown (nor on the meters); 3 s more before "stopped answering". Tested against the stand-in (elecraft-mock.py: --kat-off, --delay-ms, kat mute / unmute) by the real buttons: 30-40 s off with 300-600 ms late answers without a flip, Power On → readings in 10 s, Power Off → the readings gone, a silent tuner still "stopped answering" and back; rendered at 1180x820, 393x852, 375x667. Sergio (mail 2026-11-20 00:54, away from the station): "the tuner was powered off! I managed to switch it on through AetherSDR, and that resolved the problem", and asks "Power ON/OFF buttons for both the KPA500 and KAT500 in OksaSDR Mobile, just as we have in AetherSDR on Windows" ("Operate/Standby is useful, of course, but it's not the same as actually powering the equipment on and off"). So also *the KPA500's power* (ready in the working tree the same night): with Control on a **Power On / Off** row on its Amp tab, as the desktop's Kpa500Applet power button and through the same Kpa500Connection::setPower: Off (^ON0;) while it answers as on, On (its bootloader's wake, a lone P; the rear power switch must be on) also while it is silent, as it is off, over the bridge's open link; each confirmed, none while transmitting (the connection's TX state brought up to date first, as it refuses then without a word). Off a KPA500 answers nothing and the connection then asks only ^ON; every 5 s, so silence alone proves nothing (it is "not answering" after 3 s whatever the amplifier did): On is done when a check gets ^ON1 (15 s at most: "the wake needs its rear power switch on and the bridge connected to it"), Off by ^ON0 or by the silence through two checks ("silent after Off, as the KPA500 is when it is off"), or "still answers as on" when a check gets ^ON1. The state line no longer says "Connecting to …" while the link is up and the amplifier silent: "Link to … open, but the amplifier does not answer: it is off (Power › On below wakes it), or the bridge is not connected to it". The stand-in's KPA500 is now silent off also to ^RVM (as the real one), with --kpa-off and kpa rear off / on. Tested by the real buttons against the stand-in (300 ms late answers) and the FLEX at 393x852: off → the link line, On → confirmed → "shows power on", STANDBY, 20m; Off → silent → "silent after Off"; the rear switch off → "has not answered in 15 s", on → On works; the question open, the radio keyed (USB 5 W, no audio, peak 0.0 W): the question closed, On dim, a tap sends nothing; put back DIGU, 100 W. Still to do for a build: the manual's KPA500 and KAT500 text and pictures (Power), the notes.

Build 180 / beta 95, released 2026-11-16 (the user, 2026-11-16, build 179 on the iPad: "Ei toimi vieläkään lennosta"): **84 (g)** the sign-in renewed on a new HTTPS connection after a network change. The devlink log of 179: the SmartLink connection was made again at once, but the Auth0 refresh went over a kept-alive connection from the old network and timed out after 15 s (twice, 19:37-19:38 and 19:38-19:39), so the session was back only after 40-50 s, the last try not at all within the test. Now SmartLinkClient::restart and every transient refresh or login failure clear the HTTPS connection cache (QNetworkAccessManager::clearConnectionCache). Tested on this PC against the FLEX with Auth0 too through a local CONNECT proxy that can leave its tunnels dead (OKSASDR_AUTH_PROXY, development builds): T6 (every connection dead, a network change and the radio dropping the session at once): signed in again 1.2 s after the change, the session back in 8.6 s; the same run without the cache clear timed out after 14.5 s as on the iPad; T1-T4 again. To verify on the iPad: Wi-Fi to the hotspot and back with the app running, both ways.

Build 178 / beta 93, released 2026-11-16 (TestFlight build numbers 175–177 went to the Android-only betas' skipped iOS runs) (the user, 2026-11-16, after build 174 on the iPhone ("ios testattu ei tunnista järkevästi") and beta 92 on the OnePlus ("Vosk tosiaan toimii paremmin"): "Eikös täältä löydy suoraan myös ipad tuki", then "62, Android symbolit, 54"): **81 (step 2)** Vosk on iOS too: Alphacephei's iOS libvosk as react-native-vosk publishes it (its xcframework's device slice at commit 1ebdd9e, SHA-256 pinned, arm64 taken out by lipo in CI; the Vosk C API with every function used, BLAS / LAPACK from Accelerate, built with iOS SDK 15.2, no bitcode), linked statically (VoskSpeech's AETHER_IOS_VOSK path, the same grammar and recognition as Android), the English model in the bundle (vosk/en-us, checked in the archive by CI); Apple's recogniser kept for a build without it (and for Finnish later). The static path tested on this PC against libvosk.so (10 synthesized calls, 10 right). A call cut by a word not understood is not filled (CallsignSpeech: cut, "Not sure of the call"; build 174's iPhone gave OH6OK for OH6OKSA), the recording up to 15 s (8 s cut the user's), one spelling per word in the grammar (oscar / oskar, juliet / juliette, alpha / alfa, whiskey / whisky, charlie / charly / charley: beta 92's five alternatives were the same hearing spelled two ways; synthesized calls at 10 dB SNR 18 of 20 either way, narrowband with noise 7 of 20 against 6); --voice-selftest 47 of 47. **62** SmartLink accounts and favourites ((0)+(a)+(c); MobileEngineSmartLink.cpp): the accounts (id, e-mail, label) in AppSettings, each one's refresh token in the secure store (the first keeps the single key of the builds before, so nobody signs in again), + Account (the signed-in one kept, back to it if the new sign-in fails), a row of account buttons, Sign out per account, Remove for an account whose sign-in is gone, the e-mail from the id token for a kept account that had none; ☆ / ★ beside a SmartLink radio, the favourites of every account at the top of the SmartLink box, a tap connects, making its account active first and waiting (up to 20 s) for its radio list. On the FLEX through SmartLink from this PC: the kept sign-in became account 1, its label from SmartLink ("OH6OKSA (Niko Oksa)"), a favourite added, a second account (no sign-in) switched to and back, + Account and Cancel, Remove, a favourite connected on the active account and from the other one (switch, sign-in, list, connect), disconnected each time. **54 (Android)** MIDI controllers on Android: MidiControlManager takes a platform's own ports (MidiPlatformPorts) where RtMidi has no backend; Platform.java lists USB / Bluetooth MIDI devices (MidiManager), opens one (openDevice / openBluetoothDevice), searches 15 s for Bluetooth LE MIDI (the MIDI service UUID; Nearby devices on 12+, Location before), keeps a used Bluetooth controller's address to open it again; the bytes framed into messages (MidiFramer: running status, SysEx and real time left out) and handed to the same code as iOS. On this PC a fake port (OKSASDR_MIDI_FAKE) sent CC 100 = 65 six times with sensing and a SysEx between: 6 steps of 1 kHz on the FLEX, the frequency put back. Not tried with a real controller on Android (no MIDI device here). **Android symbols**: already done in build 157 (item 26); every symbol the UI draws is in the bundled subsets; the stale Later entry removed. To verify on devices: voice entry on an iPhone, iPad and a Mac (the model loads from the bundle; the log's alternatives), the app size, two SmartLink accounts (a second real account), a favourite after a cold start, Android MIDI with a USB and a Bluetooth controller (Hanno's or Michael's CTR2 on Android, if one of them has an Android device).

Build 172 / beta 84, released 2026-11-15 (the user, 2026-11-15: "Buildiin ehdottomasti" for 76-78 and 79 this build or the next, then "Tehdään suoraan kaikille testaajille": no preview first): **76** a clear button in the call field; **77** a Name field; **78 (a)** a logged contact edited or deleted in the app's own log; **79** logging by voice, step 1. Build 171 / beta 83, released 2026-11-15 (the user, 2026-11-15: "Nämä kaikki seuraavaan buildiin" for 72 and 73, "Lisää 1, 2 ja 3" for 74, 67 (b) and 75, "Tehdään näin" widening 74, then "Ota mukaan" for two of Christoph Steinert DM5HF's points): **72** the KPA1500 tuned in STANDBY; **73** a MIDI Learn that takes another function's control asks; **74** the receive path to upstream's (audio put back in order, #6886, #6704); **75** Big buttons' Log; **67 (b)** a POTA self-spot; and the radio's voice keyer held off in RADE, a Mac's arrow keys in text fields. '**72**: kpaTune asks ^OS0 first when the amplifier reads OPERATE and keys the carrier only once the readback shows STANDBY (5 s, else "did not go to STANDBY", no carrier); ^OS is read every cycle with ^TP and ^FL, and an OPERATE reading stops the carrier; the result adds "Put it in OPERATE when you are ready." The other amplifiers checked (above). Tested on the FLEX at 10 W (14.345, put back to 14.154 USB) against kpa1500-mock.py --tune-seconds 2: the stand-in got ^OS0 then ^FT, two writes in all; "The amplifier tuned. Put it in OPERATE when you are ready." '**73**: MobileEngineMidi's learnCompleted undoes a learn whose source another function has (the desktop's overlap rule) and asks on the row; tested with ctr2-mock: Slice volume caught the VFO knob's CC 100 (asked, nothing changed), Learn again took CC 102 (mode 1), Replace moved CC 100 (the VFO knob's binding gone), each checked in the saved midi.settings, then put back. '**74**: #6886 and #6704 cherry-picked (clean but for two test-access lines); #6992's audio part by hand in PanadapterStream: VitaRxReorder.h as upstream's, the speaker streams (PCM and Opus) through it before decoding, a 30 ms precise flush timer, one Opus decoder per stream, a stream's window retired with its generation; a minute's line in the log when audio was out of order, concealed or too late. #6700 left out (the radio binds IPv4 only). Unit tests: vita_rx_reorder_test (upstream's), remote_audio_rx_reorder_test rewritten for the new behaviour (a late packet plays in its place; a gap waits 30 ms; later than that dropped), panadapter_late_packet_test and lan_udp_keepalive_test (upstream's) and the receive tests before: all pass. On this PC's path to the FLEX (site-to-site WireGuard over LTE): 3 min uncompressed, 3 min Opus and 2 min Opus played into a test sink: no sequence errors, no underruns, no reordering seen in those minutes (the minute's line stayed silent), so the gain is for paths that reorder (testers' VPNs, Wi-Fi). '**75**: Log beside Step (upright and on its side; in the top row on a low landscape phone, as Step), a log panel in the overlay (the call, the lookup, Sent / Rcvd with − / +, Log, Cancel, PTT taking the rest), "Logged CALL" over the frequency for 4 s; the call is Engine.workedCall, My park the engine's. Rendered at seven sizes (Log first in the top row ran past 360 px with the "Logged" line: moved beside Step, the line over the frequency); a QSO logged dry (W1AW 59 / 57 with the lookup), Esc cancels, the page has the keyboard after both; focus sweeps over the Log, Amp and View tabs, Big buttons and Radio Setup › MIDI: nothing keeps the keyboard. Manual: big-buttons.jpg, big-buttons-log.jpg. '**67 (b)**: MobileEnginePota.cpp, Self-spot by My reference; tested against a stand-in for the endpoint: one POST with the JSON above, User-Agent OksaSDR/<version>, no Origin or Referer; a second within 30 s refused ("Wait 24 s between spots."); the two-fer chooser. '**RADE and the voice keyer** (Christoph, build 170: "DVK does send all macros in SSB although RADE is on"): the radio's voice keyer plays through the radio's own analog chain, so F1-F12 now refuse it while RADE is on, with the reason; the app's own recordings take the microphone's place before the RADE encoder and go as RADE. '**A Mac's arrow keys** (Christoph: the arrows did not move the cursor in the FreeDV Reporter message field): unproven without a log; AppleControlKeys now also maps AppKit's function-key characters (0xF700-0xF703 arrows, Home, End, forward Delete, Page Up / Down) as it maps Backspace, and the keyboard diagnostics log a navigation key's code (never typed text), so his next log tells; on this PC a key with U+F702 moved the call field's cursor (W1AW to W1XAW). Tested on the FLEX for RADE: RADE on (the radio's DVK stood in by a licence status), F1 refused with the reason, RADE off put slice A back to USB 100-2800 Hz.

Build 166 / beta 78, a fix build (Kurt Hanisch DL5EAG, mail 2026-11-11: beta 77 dropped the network and hung, back on 76; the developer's two Android phones and the devlink log the same morning; build 165 was the branding commit's automatic build, the same app as 164): Big buttons' − / + took their size from their own text, which follows the size the layout gives, so a turn, a fold or any resize started a layout loop ("TuneButton: Binding loop detected for property implicitHeight", "GridLayout called polish() inside updatePolish()") in the overlay, hidden or not: the GUI thread stalled 1-8 s at a time, the ping showed seconds, the radio dropped the client and Android closed the app once. Reproduced on the desktop by resizing (stalls up to 8 s), gone with the fix: the buttons' starting size fixed, the overlay made only while on (a Loader). With it, as the user asked on the phones: a tap on the frequency opens frequency entry; on a phone on its side Step beside Exit, so PTT keeps its height (it was a sliver); Log's Developer link address on its own line on a phone (squeezed unreadable). And over SmartLink the slots-full question came back (the user: "Nythän ei edes tule sitä pyyntöä"): it was asked at once after the refusal while the connection was still closing, so its check for an idle app always failed; now asked when the app is idle, tried on the FLEX with the slots full (my session holding the second). And **58**, Big buttons with the slices, two meters and the main screen's slice features. Seen once and not reproduced since: the Connect page's ColumnLayout in a polish loop after a refused connection (slots full), on beta 77's binary only.

MobileEngineMidi.cpp registers the app's 48 functions under the desktop's ids (the .map table's: freq, tunestep, bands, modes, nr, mainmute as the app's mute, ptt, the paddles...); the VFO knob tunes by the step from its own target while it turns (the desktop's 250 ms); TX functions only with Transmit on, ended when the port goes (watched every 4 s: a Bluetooth controller switched off stays open but leaves the list). To test on devices: pairing and the CTR2 itself (Hanno), USB MIDI on an iPad. ''**52**: the SmartLink sign-in kept through network dropouts. SmartLinkClient tells a passing failure (no reply, a timeout, 408, 429, 5xx: lastAuthFailureTransient) from a refusal, and drops a login or refresh answer that comes after logout (a generation counter); the app keeps the sign-in on a passing failure ("SmartLink server not reachable. Retrying…"), tries again after 5 s doubling to 60 s, at once when the network or the app comes back, and when the server drops under a kept sign-in; a dropped remote session keeps its own 5 s retry; Android's secure store writes and removes with commit(). Tested on this PC: 45 s without a network (unshare -rn): kept, tries at 5, 10, 20 and 40 s, the stored sign-in unchanged; with the network it signs in from it; --smartlink-signout-test (Sign out while the refresh is under way): still signed out 10 s later (the kept sign-in put back from a copy afterwards). Manual: SmartLink paragraph. To test on a phone: airplane mode for a minute, and a sign-out then closing the app at once (Android).

Manual: FT8 step 8 and FTx's Hound and contest paragraphs, ft8-hound.jpg, tab-digi.jpg. On the air 2026-11-10 (receive): KH8A's multi-stream message decoded on 18.095, "IC8AIK RR73; SQ2BXI <...> -12" at 366 Hz, -10 dB; KH8A runs MSHV's multi-answer (its spot: "QSY MSHV for EU"), which callers work in the normal mode, and it went to SSB before the Hound run (15 min at 10 W, its signal not heard again). On the air 2026-11-10 (transmit, 10 W): C8K Mozambique, WSJT-X F/H on 18.096 (two or three streams at 813-941 Hz, "LA9GX RR73; HA8ZB <C8K> +00"): a tap on its combined message called "<C8K> OH6OKSA" at 1319 Hz; the Fox answered "<OH6OKSA> C8K -15" four times (934, 875, 875, 878 Hz), R-05, R-08 and R-13 went each time on its Hz, then 300 Hz off (578 Hz) after it stopped; no RR73. WSJT-X's Fox cannot read a nonstandard call's R report: it queues the call from "<C8K> OH6OKSA" (decoder.f90), but foxRxSequencer finds the Hound by DecodedText::deCallAndGrid, whose word2 is [A-Z0-9/]+, so "C8K <OH6OKSA> R-13" gives an empty call; WSJT-X sends the same R message with a nonstandard call (genStdMsgs, t0b), and a free text "C8K OH6OKSA R-13" is over 13 characters. So a nonstandard call cannot complete a QSO with a WSJT-X Fox, in WSJT-X or here; standard calls are not affected. Done 2026-11-10 (the user: "Tee 1-4"): Hound's status says it when turned on with a nonstandard call without "/" (a compound call is matched by the Fox's compound loop), the manual's FTx Hound list and step 8 and the notes say it (checked on the FLEX with --ft8-test hound, dry, and rendered at 440x956 and 1366x1024: four and three lines); reported to the WSJT-X developers: https://github.com/WSJTX/wsjtx/issues/164 (the same code in master, v3.0.2). The test tool: hound,live first took a Hound's call to the Fox for the Fox ("C8K OH3MMF KP21" at 775 Hz: Hounds on dial 18.095 land below 1000 Hz on 18.096; a tap in the app does the same, as a double-click in WSJT-X), one "<OH3MMF> OH6OKSA" went out before the run was stopped; it now taps only rows the Fox sent. --ft8-test also puts each band's power back (it restored only the start band's: 17 m and 30 m had stayed at 10 and 50 W, put back to 100 W; checked with a dry CQ on 17 m). '
'**53** (first part): each license feature the radio reports goes to the log when it comes and when it changes, and the subscription (RadioModel::licenseFeatureNames, not the radio_id); on the FLEX: "radio license: SmartSDR" and 25 features, e.g. digital_voice_keyer off (plus), auto_agc_t off (ea). Next: Ian's log from his AU-510 / FLEX-8400, then the DVK choice. '
'**The Connect page at a large text size** (Ian Gilchrist VK1HF's build b5): "OksaSDR" shrinks to fit beside the version and the buttons (it ran over the version at 130 %, down to 16 px), and a radio card grows with its text; rendered with OKSASDR_TEXT_SCALE 1.3 / 1.35 and --font-px 21 / 22 at 360x800, 390x844 and 412x915 (it fits), and at 100 % the same pixels as before at 390x844, 1366x1024 and 1024x1366. Still to do: a QSO with a WSJT-X Fox from a standard call (a tester), the devices (30).

Build 153 / beta 66 (the user, 2026-11-04: the crash log "Lisää seuraavaan buildiin"; Juho Kontio's crash and Ben Morris's PGXL / TGXL comment from build 152): **The SmartLink crash at Connect** (Juho: multiFLEX off, AetherSDR at his remote station, build 152 still crashed at Connect; his .ips from build 150 a SIGSEGV from the SmartLink socket): reproduced by a simulated refusal of "client gui" with AddressSanitizer: WanConnection::processLine erased a response callback's map node after the callback, whose refusal had ended the session and cleared the map (heap use after free); the callback now leaves the map before it runs. Upstream main still has this and build 152's destructor bug (upstream not pursued). **No retry after the radio refuses the app** (it only met the same refusal), an error saying why, and **Another station is on the radio** (Main.qml): on the radio's network with multiFLEX off the engine pauses before registering (multiFlexConflictDetected; the app sat connected with nothing), over SmartLink before connecting when the licence allows one station (the desktop's confirmClientSlotAvailability; the FLEX-6600M reports licensed_clients=2) and after a refusal from SmartLink's station list; Disconnect it and connect / Cancel. Tested on the FLEX with the cabin iMac's AetherSDR on it, multiFLEX off and a refusal simulated by local test patches (removed). **The crash log** (LogBuffer): kept lines also to run.log as they come, a crash signal's name and call stack (backtrace / _Unwind_Backtrace, alternate stack, then the system's own handling), the next start's notice and Log › Previous run (Share / Copy); tested with SIGSEGV. **PGXL / TGXL wording** (Ben Morris, iPhone 17 Pro Max, real devices: Control on, but where to Operate?): where the buttons are under Control, the address as the device's own (the reported one in the empty field, "the radio reports none"), "Read directly … (the radio does not report it)", *Use the address the radio reports* / *Clear the address*; the iPad over LTE and WireGuard reached genius-mock.py at an entered address (10.9.0.5 → 192.168.1.232), as a VPN user would. Then (the user: "voidaanko simuloida tilanne jossa radio luulee, että meillä on oikeasti pgxl ja tgxl"): **genius-mock.py --radio** registers the stand-ins with the FLEX as paired devices do (4O3A's PGXL amplifier-to-radio API: a non-GUI client per device, amplifier create, keepalive, ping; no interlock, no meters), and the radio then connects to the devices' own ports and relays: the PGXL gets operate=0/1, the TGXL operate set= / bypass set= and autotune, and with --radio-tune the TGXL's autotune keys the radio's tune carrier (transmit tune 1 / 0) as the real tuner does. Tested from this PC and the user's iPad over the home network, WireGuard on LTE and SmartLink without a VPN: the address filled from the radio, Operate / Standby, Bypass, Tune keying the radio (15 W), the relays, the fan, the remote code asked, entered and kept, a mock restart as a link outage. **4O3A's TGXL protocol description** (upstream discussion #2595, WP4SK; the user: these are on main, read them) checked against all the PGXL / TGXL code by a separate read: relays numbered 1 = C1, 2 = L, 3 = C2 (we sent 0..2: L moved C1, C2 moved L, in builds up to 152 and on the desktop), 3way=1 in info for the 1x3 model (the radio maps it to one_by_three, seen on the FLEX), operate set= / bypass set= and the device's state= / bypass= direct for a tuner the radio does not report, swr of either sign, a port live with a band or a frequency whatever feeds it; left unverified by the documents: antA's base, the PGXL setup write and its authcode, the PGXL sequence past 255, the SO2R model's channel. **Ben's real cause** (his FB update: On took continuous tapping): every telemetry frame rebuilt Radio Setup's device rows (the state maps in the Repeater's model), so a tap landed on a replaced button; a fixed model now, and the Amp tab's relay rows a count. Also: the radio's refusal of a relayed amp / tuner verb shown (FlexBackend's reply sink; a Tune refused with 5000003C did nothing on screen), Radio Setup's device headings and texts (which code, why asked, what works without a direct link), the Forward bars 0-100 W under 100 W, fast polling from the key. **Split by XIT** (Sebastian DG5CST, e-mail: his FLEX-6400M's second slice held by AetherSDR at his remote PC; a Maestro user splits by XIT): Controls › TX › Split, SPLIT then sets the active slice's xit_on / xit_freq (1 kHz CW, 5 kHz voice; hold +1 / +2 / +5, XIT's ±9.999 kHz), read back from another client on the FLEX. **The decoder's text in the Macros panel** (Tyler K0OTC, e-mail: the panel covered the CW decoder). **RIT / XIT steps on their grid** (the user on Android: from 9999 the steps went …9, −1 past zero). The same firmware refuses radio set mf_enable, binaural_rx and low_latency_digital_modes with 0x50001000 (could not parse; the desktop sends the same and fails the same), so those three are shown, not set; enforce_private_ip_connections is shown only (the permission check stopped even a same-value write). Build 150 / beta 63 (the user, 2026-11-03: "Lisää 1-3 buildiin", and "tutkia muita mahdollisia focus ongelmia"): **Macros by PTT** (Tyler Akerman K0OTC, e-mail: the voice keyer at the bottom of the TX tab and the CW memories halfway down the CW tab took scrolling past sliders on a phone): a Macros button beside Controls (phone), before the controls button in a phone's landscape row, and beside PTT (tablet) opens MacrosPanel over the display, the F-keys' mapping: CW memories with the call being worked, the Digi tab's RTTY / PSK macros, the voice keyer; buttons only, not modal, PTT in use, closed by the drawer; checked on phones and tablets in USB, CW and RTTY without sending (Tyler: "That sounds like a perfect solution"). **Tune for the RF2K-S's own tuner** (Jeff Stinger, FB build 149: he tunes by sending a carrier and pressing Tune on the amplifier's screen over VNC, and on one phone the carrier stopped as the app left the screen): RF-KIT's API cannot start a tune (upstream's applet shows the tuner read only; RFC #6712's TUNE needs a community-modified server; the amplifier's TRX tune interface is Icom / Yaesu / Kenwood's; over TCI the RF2K-S takes only the frequency: upstream #2597 read its passive TCI client), so the Amp tab's Tune (Control on, asked first) puts it in STANDBY, keys the radio's TUNE at the TX tab's Tune power (4-39 W, the manual's 7.2) and keeps it with the app away for 20 s at most, stopping once the amplifier reports a tune done, in OPERATE, over 45 W, a link lost, Stop or 20 s. Keyed at 5 W on the FLEX against the stand-in (rf2k-mock.py's press-tune), with a separate read-only client logging the radio's interlock: every stop path, and the app killed (kill -9) left the radio to unkey by itself as the connection closed. Layout sweep against build 149's: 27 sizes, no layout decision changed, no QML warnings (side by side caught the tablet's PTT row growing into the display, fixed before). Found on the way: in UDP mode the RF2K-S follows N1MM's radio-info broadcasts, so an OksaSDR frequency broadcast (Fuad Arnautovic asked about one) would feed it without a cable: later. 10. **FTx:** Fox / Hound and SuperFox: working a DXpedition as a Hound (Michael Whaley AF0F 2026-10-29; Gianluca IK6QOO on FB 2026-11-09). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-09* (WSJT-X 2.7 source, the FT8 DXpedition Mode and SuperFox user guides): Hound only; being a Fox is the DXpedition's side (SuperFox needs an NCDXF key) and is not planned. No upstream code to take (the desktop leaves FT8 to WSJT-X) and no upstream issues. (a) **Classic Hound** (*in build 163, in progress*; still used, e.g. 5R8IC Madagascar "FT8 (f/h)" until 2026-12-20 on NG3K's list): ft8_lib does not decode the Fox's combined message (type 0.1, "K1ABC RR73; W9XYZ <KH1/KH7Z> -12", c28 c28 h10 r5; message.c returns "not handled"), so today the app cannot even show it: ~60 lines in our copy and a test with our own packer. A Hound switch in the FTx controls and the rules WSJT-X enforces: transmit only in the second (odd) period; call at 1000-3000 Hz (moved there if below); decode below 1000 Hz unless all are asked for; after the Fox's report, R+rpt sent automatically on the frequency the Fox called on (300-900 Hz), a repeat 300 Hz off; RR73 (alone or in a combined message) stops and logs; the operator confirms calling again every 2 minutes; directed CQs (CQ EU, ...) honoured; MSHV multi-stream messages read as WSJT-X does. Mobile: no heavier than FT8 today; the radio's TX filter must pass 300 Hz. Nonstandard Hound calls (the user's OH6OKSA): the call goes as "<KH7Z> OH6OKSA", WSJT-X's Fox takes it (lib/decoder.f90) and answers by hash, but does not read the R report "KH7Z <OH6OKSA> R-11" (on the air with C8K 2026-11-10: see build 163). Size: medium, one build; tested by decoding a live Fox at the cabin and a low-power QSO. (b) **SuperFox Hound** (WSJT-X 2.7+, the large DXpeditions): the even period carries one 1512 Hz wide signal from 750 Hz to up to 9 Hounds with a 20-bit one-time password; Hounds call with normal FT8 anywhere at 200-3000 Hz, no frequency moves, only after decoding the SuperFox. The decoder is WSJT-X's (GPLv3, as the app): ~800 lines of Fortran to port to C++ (sfrx_sub, qpc_sync, qpc_decode2, sfox_demod, sfox_remove_tone, sfox_unpack, ...) and ~1100 lines of C (the polar code, usable as is); removing FT8 signals first needs FT8 subtraction, which ft8_lib lacks (later). "Verified": the code checked at https://www.9dx.cc (NCDXF's server; the Fox's call, the time and the code, nothing of the user; no terms found, the guide offers it to receiving software): an option, named on the privacy page; or the code shown for a later check. Test audio: no SuperFox recordings published; WSJT-X's sfoxsim needs gfortran (not on this PC) or our own port of the encoder; no SuperFox DXpedition on NG3K's list now. Size: large, its own build after (a). Both: Hound only on the Fox's announced frequency, never the FT8 sub-bands (a warning when Hound is on on a standard FT8 frequency), and off on a band change.

19. **Radio:** Optional VOX mode, the microphone always open (*in build 188*, with this device's microphone) Asked again by Christoph Steinert DM5HF (mail 2026-11-16, Mac with EarPods: "is there a VOX option? In the manual I found it, but not in the app? Would be cool, especially with EarPods"): the VOX in Controls › TX is the radio's own, shown only with the radio's microphone input; a VOX for this device's microphone needs the microphone always open (this item). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-18* (chosen for build 187, the user: "19 VOX laitteen mikille"): the radio's own VOX keys on whatever reaches its microphone input, and with mic input PC that is the audio the client streams (remote_audio_tx): the desktop (MainWindow_Session.cpp) streams the PC microphone all the time while PC is chosen ("remote_audio_tx still exists for VOX/met_in_rx"), so SmartSDR's and the desktop's VOX work with a PC headset; this app opens the device's microphone only while PTT is held (and for the TX tab's Mic meter, held off while VOX is on: "speech would key it"), so the radio hears nothing to key on, and the VOX rows show only with the radio's own inputs. Fix: with mic input PC and the radio's VOX on, the app keeps the device's microphone streaming to the radio (as the mic check does), and the VOX and Delay rows show for PC too; the radio keys and unkeys by its own VOX level and delay (nothing new on the radio's side); the app's own uses of the microphone that are not for the air stop the stream while they run (the voice log's listening, the voice keyer's recording), so they cannot key it; the stream stops when VOX goes off, the input changes, the app disconnects or goes to the background without background audio; a line under VOX says the microphone stays open and a headset is needed (a phone's speaker would key it with the receive audio). iOS shows its microphone indicator while it is open, as it should. Terms: none (the radio's own VOX); the privacy policy's Microphone section says the microphone is open only while PTT is held or a recording runs: it gets "or while VOX is on with this device's microphone". Test: on the FLEX, live at low power on a clear frequency (TX testing wanted): mic PC, VOX on, a recorded voice played into the PC's input keys the radio and the delay drops it; VOX off closes the microphone; the voice log does not key it. Size: small-medium. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-18)*: MobileEngine::updateMicCheck holds the microphone for VOX (with mic input PC, the radio's VOX on, not CW, not in standby, the radio able to transmit; re-checked on the phone settings, the transmit lock and the mode) and the Mic meter's check as before (never with VOX on); usingDeviceMicrophone and voxHoldsMicrophone for the TX tab, whose VOX and Delay rows now show with this device's microphone too (DEXP stays with the radio's inputs), with a line under Delay: the microphone open and a headset needed, or in standby that VOX cannot key until armed; the voice log's and the voice keyer's recordings send nothing to the radio (AudioEngine keeps a recording's audio to itself), and the Android recogniser closes the stream while it listens, as it did the meter's. The manual's TX tab and the privacy policy (the microphone open with VOX, and for the TX tab's meter) say so; the shot plan's phone command reads and sets the radio's phone settings. Tested on the FLEX, live at 10 W on 14.2365 USB (clear, the noise floor) with a virtual PipeWire microphone playing a Morse "TEST DE OH6OKSA" at 800 Hz twice: "mic check: microphone open for VOX", the radio keyed by its VOX on each word ("radio transmitting: yes (this app)") and dropped after its delay (13; Morse's word gaps are longer, so 14 key-ups for two plays; speech holds it), VOX off closed it; with the transmit lock on (SB) and VOX on the microphone stayed closed and the same Morse keyed nothing; frequency (14.155450 USB), power (100 W), VOX off, the lock off and the PC's default input restored. Rendered at 440x956 and the iPad's panel at 1180x820; the manual's tab-tx, fm-tx and tx-lock re-rendered on the FLEX (tx-lock's scroll 550 → 650 for the two rows). Christoph Steinert DM5HF (mail 2026-11-21, build 197 on the Mac and the iPad Pro): "simply great ... works with a good adjustments directly on Mac / iPad without headset"; a wish, "not priority": an *ANT-VOX* (anti-VOX) slider, so the receive audio from the device's own speaker does not key VOX. Rich Yost N2RY (mail 2026-11-21, AM and 10 m FM): "the VOX gets a little junky sometimes". To pre-analyse: the VOX is the radio's (it keys on the microphone audio the app streams), so an anti-VOX would be the app's own (the receive audio's level against the microphone's, or holding back the stream while only the speaker's echo comes in), and what iOS's voice processing (echo cancellation) already removes.

22. **Display:** Upstream FFT Smooth and De-comb (*in build 196 / Android beta 107*, the user 2026-11-20: "22 mukaan"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-20*: two of the desktop's Display panel sliders the app lacks; display only, the audio and the radio untouched. **FFT Smooth** (src/gui/FftTraceSmoothing.h, 63 lines, header only): each point the mean of its neighbours ±N logical px (0-16, 0 = Off) in linear power, back to dB, so the noise floor is not pulled down as a dB mean would. **De-comb** (src/gui/FftTraceDecomb.h + core/dsp/SpectralEnvelope.cpp, already in libaethercore with FftwPlannerLock.cpp, FFTW being in the iOS and Android builds): a zoomed-in SSB voice drawn as a comb of its pitch harmonics (100-250 Hz apart) is replaced by its cepstral true envelope over the peaks; one width in Hz (0-600, 0 = Off); over noise the trace stays as it was (clipped at the 20th percentile + 12 dB, blended in only above it); a steep edge rounds over about half the width. The desktop (SpectrumWidget::shapeFftTrace) runs both on the source bins, De-comb first, Smooth's half-width turned from px into bins (px × Hz per px / Hz per bin). The app today: SpectrumItem has only the time average (kTraceAverage 0.35, the desktop's SMOOTH_ALPHA) and the 3D view its row smoothing. Plan: SpectrumItem::onSpectrum keeps m_spectrum (the averaged bins) as it is for the levels, the noise floor and the CW / spot peak finding, and shapes a copy for drawing only (both draw paths, the scene graph's and the software one); the waterfall and the 3D view stay unshaped as on the desktop. Controls › View › Spectrum gets two sliders under Range: "FFT smooth  Off / N px" and "De-comb  Off / N Hz" (step 10 Hz), stored per device (MobileFftSmoothing, MobileFftDecombHz), off by default; the manual's View section gets both. Cost: De-comb is two DCTs of about 2048 points times up to 12 iterations per frame (about 0.15 ms on the desktop, a few times that on a phone at 25 fps); the plan for a new length only tries FFTW's planner lock, so a frame without it draws unshaped. To measure on the iPad and the OnePlus with the devlink log (frame time). Test on the FLEX: an SSB voice zoomed to 3 kHz with De-comb 0 / 150 / 300 Hz, the noise with Smooth 0 / 8 / 16 px, both off gives the trace bit for bit as before, CW peak tuning and the spots unchanged, a pan width change and a zoom. No terms. Size: small to medium. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-20)*: SpectrumItem::shapeTrace (De-comb, then Smooth, on a copy; m_spectrum untouched), MobileEngine fftSmoothing / fftDecombHz (MobileFftSmoothing, MobileFftDecombHz; the limits asserted equal to the desktop's), View › Spectrum "FFT smooth" and "De-comb" under Range, hidden with 3D on. Tested on the FLEX at 1366x1024 by dragging the sliders (a tap on a track does nothing since 101): an 80 m LSB voice at 3.694 zoomed to 5 kHz with De-comb Off / 140 / 280 Hz (the comb drawn as one outline, the noise beside it as it was) and Smooth Off / 8 / 16 px (16 gives the desktop's flat tops of a 33 px box), back to Off; at 393x852 the two sliders fit in the drawer.
23. **Display:** 3D with 768 columns on capable devices (*in build 196 / Android beta 107*, the user 2026-11-20: "23 niin että huomioidaan tehokkaat laitteet"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-20*: the desktop's 3D (DssRenderer) has 768 frequency columns; the app's Spectrum3DItem 512, "to spare a phone's GPU". 768 gives 50 % finer frequency detail, which shows on a wide view (an iPad at 1366 pt draws 512 columns 2.7 pt apart, 768 1.8 pt), hardly on a phone (393-430 pt upright), at about 50 % more mesh (104 rows × 1281 instead of 854 vertices) and texture upload. The shaders take texCols and meshCols as uniforms already; the C++ has kCols / kMeshCols as constants sizing std::array rows. Plan: the arrays sized for 768 and a column count chosen when the 3D view starts: 768 on a tablet or computer (the screen's shorter side 600 logical px or more: iPads, the Mac, Linux, Android tablets and a foldable's inner screen), 512 on a phone. The slow-GPU check (items 94, 99) first steps 768 down to 512 and keeps the 3D view, logged ("3D: 768 columns too slow, 512"), and only at 512 switches the view off as before. The column count in the log at start. Test: the render tool at 1366x1024 and 393x852 against the FLEX (768 / 512 in the log, the picture the same apart from detail), the step-down forced with a test switch, the iPad on the devlink log for frame times. No terms. Size: small. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-20)*: Spectrum3DItem rows sized for 768, m_cols chosen at creation (screen shorter side >= 600 → 768; AETHER_3D_COLS overrides), onGpuTooSlow steps 768 to 512 once (history cleared, the slow check warming up again) before switching the view off; the renderer makes its mesh and height texture again at the new size (buildResources / releaseResources), gets the columns at creation so the first build is right; AETHER_3D_SLOW_AFTER_MS (development) forces the step. Tested on the FLEX with the OpenGL RHI (llvmpipe) at 1366x1024: 768 columns drawn, the forced step at 35 s to 512 with the view kept and drawn again ("3D pipelines ready ... 512 columns"); llvmpipe's frame waits 768 / 512: median 1112 / 953 ms (a CPU renderer, only a relative cost: about +17 %); 393x852 with 512 drawn. To check: the iPad and the OnePlus on the devlink log.

37. **Radio:** Power per mode (*in build 190 / Android beta 102* as Controls › TX › Digital max), Sloan Davis (TestFlight, build 156, FLEX Aurora AU-520: low drive gave 140 W on FT8, SSB wants 65-70 %): RF power remembered per mode group (voice, CW, digital), with an optional upper limit for digital modes, set as the mode changes and shown when it does. Parked (the user, 2026-11-08): the radio already remembers the power per band, and within a band the radio's TX profiles (Prof tab) do it, which the user uses; with the set power now in the TX slice's tag (36) a forgotten power shows before a transmission. If it comes back, the clear part is a digital-modes limit (Digital max, off by default) rather than power per mode group. *Chosen for build 190* (the user, 2026-11-19, after "Mitä digtal max tekee": "37 mukaan"). *Done in the dev build (2026-11-19)*: MobileEngineDigitalMax.cpp, Controls › TX › **Digital max** under RF power (− / + in 5s, Off at 0, in the RF power's own unit: W, or % on an Aurora; kept on the device, MobileDigitalMax), a line under it with what it did. When the TX slice is in DIGU, DIGL or RTTY (FT8 / FT4, PSK, RTTY and RADE, which runs in DIGU / DIGL) and the power is above the limit, the power comes down to it; the power before comes back when the slice leaves those modes, only if the power is still the one the limit set (changed meanwhile, it stays); a power raised above the limit there (here or by another client) comes back down; the limit changed while it holds the power takes the power with it, up to the power before (found in the test: + from Off in DIGU put the power to 5 W and it stayed there, and USB then restored nothing, as the restore compared with the new limit). Off leaves the power where it is. Tested on the FLEX (power only, no transmission): in DIGU 100 W, + eight times: 5, 10 … 40 W following; USB: back to 100; DIGU: 40; 60 asked in DIGU: 40; USB: back to 100 (the power before the digital mode); 30 in USB, DIGU and USB: 30 kept; Off. DIGU and 100 W put back.

40. **Fixes:** The on-screen keyboard did not come for a focused text field, Sebastian DG5CST (e-mail, 2026-11-07, iPhone 15 Pro, iOS 27.0.1, build 157: the CW memory editor and the Log tab on 160 m CW, the field focused with its cursor; after an app restart it came). First guess a hardware keyboard (iOS shows no on-screen keyboard with one; build 158 logs them coming and going), but Sebastian answered (2026-11-07): nothing connected, a plain iPhone 15 Pro; he sends Log › Previous run if it comes again. So likelier the app's side: in Qt 6.12's iOS input context the keyboard comes only when Qt's text responder becomes UIKit's first responder at a focus change (showInputPanel is a no-op, the same focus object skips the reset), so a field that keeps the focus after the responder was lost, or a UIKit refusal (another responder or a presented view holding it), leaves the cursor in the field and no keyboard. Next: on iPhone / iPad too (now only on a Mac) log, when a field is focused or pressed and no keyboard shows within 0.5 s with no hardware keyboard, UIKit's first responder, the key window, a presented view and the app state; then recover as the Mac fix does (the input method updated, the field's focus dropped and taken again, the window activated), each step logged. Then (2026-11-07, 20.04) his screenshot of Log on build 158, a Mac screenshot ("Bildschirmfoto … um 19.03.07", the phone's clock 19:03): the iPhone seen on his Mac, likeliest through iPhone Mirroring, where the Mac's keyboard is the iPhone's and iOS shows no on-screen keyboard (Cmd-K toggles it), and "even" Share › Mail brought none, so iOS-wide, not the app. Asked: iPhone Mirroring? Typing with the Mac's keyboard into the field? The log by Copy or AirDrop to the Mac (build 158's keyboard lines). The diagnostic and recovery above wait for that answer. His log also shows the RF2K-S out of reach: "RfkitConnection: transport error on /info Operation timed out" and Qt's "QIODevice::read … device not open" every 9 s, worth one line per state change. Sebastian (e-mail, 2026-11-08): a plain iPhone, no mirroring, and it happened again: the app's side, so his log is needed (Log › Send to the developer, build 159).
41. **Radio:** Bluetooth PTT for operating with a Bluetooth headset or the car's hands-free (the user, 2026-11-07), in steps: (a) a button that is a Bluetooth keyboard (page-turner pedals and PTT buttons in keyboard mode, Space / Enter) keys TX already with Space while the page has the keyboard; a *PTT key* learned by pressing the button once, so any key it sends serves, also while a drawer is open; (b) the headset's or car's play / pause button as PTT, off by default: iOS MPRemoteCommandCenter (toggle only, press on and press off: iOS gives no release), Android a MediaSession in RadioService (hold where the device sends press and release); with a TX time limit, a beep at TX start and end (the screen not watched), TX ended at once when the Bluetooth device goes, and never keyed by the "play" many car units send on connect; (c) BLE PTT buttons with an open SDK, hold-to-talk: Flic 2 / Flic Duo (official flic2lib for iOS and Android, press and release; a press queued while the button was out of reach, wasQueued, never keys TX; Android adds BLUETOOTH_SCAN) and the programmable button of BlueParrott / Jabra Perform headsets (Apache-2.0 SDK, iOS and Android); (d) later, TX from the lock screen with Apple's PushToTalk framework (an entitlement; the system starts the audio session; BLE accessory events and the system's talk button). Not: buttons whitelisted for Zello only (AINA, Pryme BLE: no public protocol), CallKit. To learn on devices first: what a headset and a car unit send while the Bluetooth microphone (HFP) is open, and whether a remote command reaches the app in the background. Step (a) waits until the user has a Bluetooth button for the tests (2026-11-08). A handset's own PTT key, asked by Rob Metallo VA3MET (mail 2026-11-20 01:51 "Is it possible to add PTT button mapping?", then 19:07 "Was trying on the F400 android 15 and couldn't get the built in ptt to work"; TestFlight: iPad (10th gen), iOS 26.2, build 196; the F400 on the Android beta): rugged and PoC handsets send their side PTT key as a key code (often vendor-specific) or as a vendor broadcast intent rather than as Space, so the learned *PTT key* of step (a) would take the key-code kind; the broadcast kind needs the vendor's intent names. To find out first: the F400's maker and what its key sends (a key-event log line in the app, or the vendor's PoC documentation). Rich Yost N2RY (mail 2026-11-21 01:23, "Is it possible to implement a Bluetooth PTT ? I use AirPods and it would allow me to not touch the screen in a round table env"): step (b) with AirPods; besides MPRemoteCommandCenter, iOS 17's input-mute handler for apps that use the microphone (the AirPods stem press mutes and unmutes, AVAudioApplication) is a candidate to verify on a device. Until then VOX with the AirPods microphone (Controls › TX › VOX) keys hands-free. Rich (mail 2026-11-21 14:24): "it was more of a Bluetooth button device I was thinking of": step (a); a button in keyboard mode that sends Space keys TX today (hold; Tap on / off under PTT for a button that sends a short press only). A reply sent 2026-11-21 says so and asks which button. 48. **Radio:** Auto AGC-T in the app, for every radio (the user, 2026-11-08: as Thetis has it, so radios without FlexRadio's Early Access licence get it too, as the app's own CW keyer and voice keyer did; roadmap 32 is the radio's licensed one). The radio's AGC knee (agc_threshold 0-100) has no dBm mapping, so unlike Thetis (its AGC runs in the PC and knows the noise floor in dBm) the app finds it from what it hears: the engine already has the desktop's AgcTCalibrator (aethercore, docs/agc-t-calibration-design.md): on a quiet spot it steps AGC-T from high to low (~300 ms a step) and finds the knee where the post-AGC audio level (AudioEngine::levelChanged) starts to drop, with a quiet-spot check (S meter vs the noise floor, mobile NoiseFloorTracker). Planned: (a) ~~an **Auto** button by the AGC-T slider: one sweep of a few seconds, the value set just below the knee, remembered per band and AGC speed and set again on that band~~ *in build 162*; (b) later, Thetis-like tracking: the noise floor followed between sweeps and AGC-T moved by the slope the sweep measured, a new sweep on a band change. Must not fight the app's per-mode AGC (voice slow, data / CW fast) or a radio that has its own Auto (32); receive only, tested on the FLEX on several bands. Size: a build of its own.
    *Build 170*: the line's switch also in the AGC panel (Noise line, under Follow) and 2 px in 2D, after the user found nothing in build 169 with the switch Off.
    ~~(b)~~ *in build 169*: View › Noise floor line and AGC › Follow (tested on the FLEX: 20 m USB 56 at -127 dBm, 1.00 dB a step, ANT2 -134 dBm moved it to 63 and ANT1 back to 57; CW 500 / 250 Hz, AGC off, FM, AM and a TUNE carrier held as planned; a flat sweep no longer stays at Measuring 100 %, a build 162 bug). (b) asked again by the user, 2026-11-13: AGC-T set automatically as in Thetis, the noise floor shown on the panadapter as its own line, and a switch to turn it on or off in case a device lacks the power. *Pre-analysis 2026-11-13*: Thetis (MW0LGE, since v2.8.11) has Auto AGC, which keeps the threshold over its noise-floor estimate, and Setup › Display › RX1 › "Show noise floor on panadapter", a dashed line at that estimate (Apache Labs forum threads "Thetis v2.8.11 - UPDATES", "Noise Floor Line - Gone?"). Here the noise floor is already estimated on every spectrum frame for the display's auto range (SpectrumItem.noiseFloorDbm, NoiseFloorTracker with the desktop's transient rejection: a lightning crash does not lift it), so neither part costs a device more than a line drawn and an occasional command: no power switch is needed for that, but two user switches are, as some want AGC-T where they put it: Controls › View › **Noise floor line** (the line with its dBm at the right edge, 2D and 3D) and an **Auto: follow the noise** choice by the AGC-T slider beside today's one-shot Auto. Tracking: after a sweep the app knows the knee at that floor and, from the sweep's curve below the knee, the dB per AGC-T step; when the floor at the slice (the passband's own bins, not the whole pan: the noise slopes across a wide pan) has moved 3 dB or more for 10 s, AGC-T moves by that much over the slope, at most once every few seconds, never while transmitting, and a band or mode-group change sweeps again (or recalls). Off at once when the slider is moved by hand (as Auto now) and whenever the radio's own Auto (32) is on. The radio's 0-100 knee has no dBm mapping, so the line shows the noise floor, not the AGC-T knee (a knee line would be a guess). Tests: on the FLEX across a floor change (80 m at dusk, 40 m at sunrise), the commands counted, the line against the trace in 2D and 3D, a phone and an iPad. Size: medium, a build of its own. *The other modes (the user, 2026-11-13: "48 pitää huomioida miten toimitaan muilla kuin phone modeilla")*: the app's mode groups today are voice (USB, LSB, AM, SAM and also FM, NFM), cw and data (DIGU, DIGL, RTTY, DFM, FDVU, FDVL); each group keeps its own swept value, slope and AGC speed (voice slow, CW and data fast). **CW**: a narrow filter (500 or 250 Hz) lets about 6-10 dB less noise into the passband than 2.9 kHz, so the knee and the slope are the CW group's own, measured with the CW filter; a filter width change of more than half sweeps again (or recalls the width's value); the passband floor is read from the filter's own bins (a 250 Hz filter is only a few bins: averaged over a longer time), and a keyed signal in the passband holds the tracking (the quiet check as the sweep's). **Data**: FT8 / FT4 put many signals in one 3 kHz passband, so the passband floor is the lowest bins' (the quantile the display floor uses), not the mean; the tracking moves AGC-T only between transmit periods and never within 2 s after TX; RTTY, PSK and FreeDV / RADE (on DIGU / DIGL) are one signal in the passband: the same rules as CW. FlexRadio advises AGC off or slow for multi-signal digital modes: with AGC off the slider is agc_off_level (a fixed gain, no knee), so neither the sweep nor the tracking runs there. **AM / SAM**: the carrier always sits in the passband, so the passband floor is read beside the carrier (the bins outside the AM filter on both sides) and the quiet check refuses a sweep on a station (it already does). **FM / NFM / DFM**: the radio has no AGC in FM (the desktop hides the AGC panel there, docs/agc-t-calibration-design.md): Auto and Auto: follow the noise are off and dimmed with a note, and FM moves out of the voice group into its own (no AGC); today's Auto button still offers a sweep in FM, fixed with this. **Every mode**: the noise floor line is display only and drawn in every mode; the tracking is held while the slice transmits, while the radio's own Auto (32) is on, and in AGC off. Tests on the FLEX: SSB 80 / 40 m, CW 500 and 250 Hz on a quiet spot and beside a keyed signal, FT8 on 20 m through TX periods, AM on a broadcast station, FM on 10 m (Auto dimmed).
49. ~~**Fixes:** The Macros button's repeat mark an empty box on Android (the user's OnePlus screenshot, beta 74, 2026-11-08: ⟳ U+27F3 is in none of the fonts Android draws)~~ *in build 162 / Android beta 75*: ↻ (U+21BB, in the bundled Noto Sans Math subset's arrows); docs-check (the pre-push hook) now refuses a QML symbol between U+2190 and U+2BFF outside the bundled fonts' ranges (checked: ⟳ put back is refused).
51. ~~**FTx:** The FT8 / FT4 contest and DXpedition messages shown in the decode list~~ *in build 163* (the user, 2026-11-09, with 10 (a)). ft8_lib decodes only the standard, nonstandard-call, free text and telemetry types and drops the rest ("not handled"), so the Fox's combined messages and a contest weekend's exchanges never show. *Pre-analysis 2026-11-09*: the types and their bits are WSJT-X's (lib/77bit/77bit.txt, packjt77.f90 unpack77): 0.1 DXpedition ("K1ABC RR73; W9XYZ <KH1/KH7Z> -12"), 0.3 / 0.4 ARRL Field Day ("WA9XYZ KA1ABC R 16A EMA"), 3 ARRL RTTY Roundup ("TU; W9XYZ K1ABC R 579 MA", serial or state / province), 5 EU VHF contest ("<PA3XYZ> <G4ABC/P> R 590003 IO91NP"); 0.2 and 0.6+ are undefined or WSPR only. Decoded in our copy of ft8_lib (~150 lines and WSJT-X's section and state tables, GPLv3 as the app), the message parser taught "TU;" and the combined form, so worked-before, colours and PSK Reporter read the right calls. Tested with a bit packer for each type through the decoder, and WSJT-X's sample recording from the 2018 FT8 Roundup (its 2019 FT4 samples are from before FT4's release and decode in nothing). Display only: answering in a contest is not part of it. No terms, no mobile limits. Size: small, with 10 (a) in build 163.
52. ~~**Fixes:** The SmartLink sign-in kept through network dropouts, and a sign-out that sticks~~ (*in build 164*; Ian Gilchrist VK1HF, mail 2026-11-09, from his own Android build on a FLEX-8400 and an Aurora AU-510 over SmartLink; his code GPLv3, offered as a branch to cherry-pick). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-10*: confirmed in our code: SmartLinkClient emits authFailed for any refresh reply error, a timeout or no network included (src/core/backends/flex/SmartLinkClient.cpp:155-159), and the app's authFailed handler then removes the kept sign-in (mobile/src/MobileEngine.cpp:1517), so a phone losing the network loses its SmartLink sign-in; Android's secure store writes and removes with apply() (mobile/android/src/fi/oh6oksa/oksasdr/Platform.java:73, 96), so a sign-out may not be saved if the app is closed at once. Ian also found a token refresh or login answer arriving after Sign out signing back in. Fix (his approach): only a real refusal (Auth0 4xx other than 408 / 429, or the server rejecting the session) forgets the sign-in; no network, a timeout, rate limiting or a 5xx keep it, with "not reachable, trying again", a backoff (5 s doubling to 60 s) and a retry when the network or the app comes back; a generation counter drops answers after Sign out; commit() for the secure store. Size: small-medium; tested by cutting the network on a phone (airplane mode) and with Sign out during a refresh.
53. ~~**Fixes:** The voice keyer on the radio's DVK without SmartSDR+~~ *done in builds 163 and 167* (closed 2026-11-16, the user: "eikös meillä ole jo oma voice keyr ilman lisenssiä?": the app's own voice keyer clips need no licence; the radio's DVK is used only when the radio reports digital_voice_keyer licensed, and every licence feature is in the log; a manual choice of the radio's DVK was never asked for) (Ian Gilchrist VK1HF: his radios used the radio's DVK instead of the app's clips, no SmartSDR+ licence). *Second part in build 167* (the user, 2026-11-11): the radio's DVK only once the radio reports digital_voice_keyer on: radioFeatureLicensed() is false while the radio has not said, so a radio without SmartSDR+, or one that never says, keeps the app's own clips, which always work (the desktop fails open, #4210, having no clips of its own). Since build 163 the app subscribes to the license status, and the FLEX here says "digital_voice_keyer off (plus)": the app's clips, as before. *Pre-analysis 2026-11-10*: radioFeatureLicensed() takes a feature the radio has not reported as licensed ("older firmware never says", mobile/src/MobileEngine.cpp:3039-3050), and dvkOnRadio() then picks the radio's DVK until it refuses. To learn first what his AU-510 / FLEX-8400 report: the app does not log the licence features today (MobileEngine.cpp logs only SmartLink's licensed clients), so a log of his could not tell (found 2026-11-10 from his logs); each feature the radio reports goes to the log (name, enabled, reason), then ask him for a log; then: the radio's DVK only once the radio says the feature is on, or a choice (his build: a setting, the app's clips by default). Size: small.
54. **Radio:** Bluetooth MIDI controllers as a VFO knob and buttons (*iOS in build 164*; *Android in beta 93*): Lynovation's CTR2-MIDI / CTR2-Dial (Bluetooth LE and USB MIDI; used with SmartSDR for iOS / macOS), Hanno Vogels DK3HV, mail 2026-11-09; also Michael Walker, mail 2026-11-10 ("integrate the CRT series of controllers", with his idea of a screen for driving); numbered by the user 2026-11-10 ("Tee 1-4"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-09* (Lynovation's CTR2-MIDI manuals v3.00.01, our engine): (0) **No app change may be needed at home:** CTR2-MIDI firmware 2.00+ has a Flex mode that joins the radio over WiFi as a non-GUI client bound to the GUI client ("works with any Flex GUI client"; same LAN as the radio, no SmartLink); the app registers as a GUI client with an id ("client gui <id>"), so it should bind: Hanno to try it first. (1) **MIDI mode in the app** (needed over SmartLink or VPN): the engine already has the desktop's MidiControlManager (RtMidi 6.0, Learn, the relative VFO decoder, CW gates) and MidiSettings, built into the iOS engine with CoreMIDI; Android builds RtMidi as a dummy (RtMidi's AMIDI backend needs API 29, our minimum is 28). The CTR2 sends the knob as CC 100 "WheelA" (64 plus a speed offset), buttons as notes 1-6 and long presses 11-16; Lynovation ships an AetherSDR map for the desktop's import (their file, not bundled). To build: the app's own function list (VFO, step, band, mode, filter, MOX, CW paddles into the app's keyer), a MIDI page in Radio Setup (device, Learn, the map import), Bluetooth pairing (iOS CABTMIDICentralViewController and a Bluetooth permission text; USB MIDI on an iPad needs none; on a Mac Audio MIDI Setup pairs; Android a BLE scan, MidiManager.openBluetoothDevice and BLUETOOTH_SCAN), the privacy page. TX from MIDI (PTT, CW) off by default and ended when the device goes. Upstream open: #4402 (0/127 encoders), #3927 (VFO sensitivity), #743 (VFO step by notes), #6684, none blocking. No terms (MIDI is a standard). Test: no MIDI device here (a USB MIDI keyboard would do on an iPad or Android); Hanno has the CTR2; an iOS network MIDI session from this PC as a stand-in. Size: medium for iOS (one build), Android a second step. Hanno tried Flex mode 2026-11-10: it did not work in his network (a Fritz 7580, the radio on LAN, the CTR2 on WiFi), and WiFi never reaches a remote station; he uses the CTR2 over Bluetooth with another iPad app, very stable. Verdict: MIDI mode, iOS first. Michael Walker's CTR2 Mini worked with it, no issues (FB, 2026-11-12).
55. ~~**Display:** Big buttons, a screen of a few large controls for operating in the car, with gloves or at a glance~~ (*in build 164*; Michael Walker, mail 2026-11-10: "a mobile screen that only had a few buttons on it like up and down at the step rate so you could tap them easily without having to look at the phone", PTT about a third of an iPhone's screen at the bottom; the user, 2026-11-10: "voidaan miettiä"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-10*: one QML page over the operating screen, no new engine calls: the frequency and mode large, the S meter as a bar, **−** and **+** filling the middle (tuneBySteps by the step shown on them, held to repeat, a haptic tap each step), the step changed by a tap on it (stepList), PTT across the bottom third (setPtt, hold as the app's PTT; the app's rules: voice modes, the transmit lock), the screen kept awake; out by a long press on a small Exit, so a stray tap does not leave it. In View › Big buttons and the ⋯ menu. Portrait and landscape, phones and tablets. With the Bluetooth PTT (41) and the CTR2's knob (54) the eyes can stay off the screen. Terms: App Store guideline 1.4.5 ("Apps should not urge customers to ... use their devices in a way that risks physical harm"), so it is named and described as big buttons (the car mounted and used as local law allows, gloves, the field, low vision), never as for use while driving; CarPlay and Android Auto take only their own app categories (an Apple entitlement; ham radio is not one), so it is the phone's own screen. Size: small-medium, one build; tested by the user's path on every form factor (render), hold-to-repeat tuning and PTT on the FLEX (USB, no audio).
