57. **Branding:** The app icons made again from the vector master (the user, 2026-11-11: "Kirjaa", after the SVG master came in b8f30dd). *In build 167* (the user, 2026-11-11: "59, 53, 57 mukaan"): resources/branding/source/make-icons.sh makes the set (the mark trimmed from a 4096 px render, then placed by the measured sizes). Checked against the old set: every size the same, iOS and Play RGB without alpha, the Android foreground and round icons with alpha; the mark within 1-2 px of the old place, now centred; outside Android's 66 dp safe circle 537 px of the arrow's tip (the old set 8681), outside the 72 dp mask 238 (4265); side by side and a difference picture: the same mark with clean edges. On the devices after install: to do. *Pre-analysis 2026-11-11*: resources/branding/source/oksasdr-symbol.svg (traced from the 1024 px raster, two gradient paths, the white traces knock-outs) draws the same mark as the raster (side by side and a difference picture), with clean edges where the raster has compression noise and softness at 1024 px (the App Store's 1024 icon, the iPad's 167 and 152, Android's xxxhdpi). Its frame is tight: the mark takes about 93 % of the width and the upper arrow's tip touches the top edge, so `rsvg-convert -w 1024 -h 1024` alone is not an icon master: iOS's rounded corners could cut the right arrow, and Android's adaptive foreground needs the mark inside the safe zone. The set made again with today's margins, measured from the files: iOS the mark at 80 % on white, RGB without alpha (AppIcon-1024 and the 12 sizes Icon-20 … Icon-180, the Mac's too); Android the foreground's mark at 248 of 432 px (57 % of the 108 dp canvas, inside the 66 dp safe zone) over the white background layer, the legacy ic_launcher and ic_launcher_round at five densities, and the Play listing's play_store_512; made by a script beside the sources (rsvg-convert, magick), so later sizes come out the same. Under about 64 px the fine traces still run together, as in the raster: a simplified small mark is design work for later, not this item; also later, made possible by the vector: Android 13's themed icon (a one-colour monochrome layer) and iOS 18's dark and tinted icons. No terms beyond the stores' icon rules (iOS no alpha, Android the adaptive layers); no code. Test: old and new side by side at every size, the size and alpha checks, and on the devices after install (the iPad and iPhone home screens, the OnePlus launcher's mask). Size: small.
58. ~~**Display:** Big buttons with more to read and the main screen's features~~ (the user, 2026-11-11: "Mahtuisiko ... exit ja mode sanan väliin slice tieto tehoineen sekä palkkimittareita 2 kpl päällekkäin joissa valittavissa mitä ne näyttävät", then "Kaikki samaan 166:een"; *in build 166*). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-11*: the slice chip's TX tag with the set power (SliceBar), the meter readings and their chooser (MeterView) and the power slider and slice menu exist; shared, not copied ("vaikka näkymät muuttuvat niin ominaisuuksien pitää säilyä"): SlicePowerPopup and SliceMenu serve the main screen's chips and Big buttons'. Built with the user's corrections on the phones: every slice as a chip (the TX slice with letter and power, the others the letter; three or more on a row of their own; letter left, tag right, so a tap in the middle selects); two meters as the first version's S bar with the reading large, their own choices (MobileMeterBig1/2, S and Volts, Power and SWR), under the frequency upright and across the whole width on its side; Step two fifths of − / + upright (a big hand in the car), − and + narrower on its side. Found on the way: the main screen's tap handlers took taps through Big buttons (a tap on a meter opened the mode list): the page's controls are off under it. Tested on the FLEX: one, two and three slices, the tag's slider and the hold menu on both screens, a 5 W tune on 28.500 with Power and SWR live. Size: small-medium.

59. **FTx:** A DX working several stations at once without Hound: its RR73 inside its message to the next ends and logs the QSO (Ben Morris KT4O, mail with his log 2026-11-11, build 166, iPad: KH8A on 30 m sent "RC3KK RR73; KT4O <KH8A> -16", which gave the report, then "KT4O RR73; JK1TMP <KH8A> -16", which the sequencer skipped, as it read the DXpedition form's RR73 only with Hound on; the app sent R-07 again until he pressed Stop and nothing was logged; the user, 2026-11-11: "59, 53, 57 mukaan"; *in build 167*). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-11*: WSJT-X 2.7 (widgets/mainwindow.cpp, readFromStdout and processMessage) takes a five-word message whose first word is its own base call in the normal mode too, as a "dual Fox style message, possibly from MSHV": it logs the QSO and goes to Tx6, no 73. The same here: in handleDigiDecodes, outside Hound, a DXpedition message with our call as its RR73's from the DX being worked (its hashed call known and the same base call, or not known) logs and ends the QSO (digiFinishQso: back to CQ with Keep CQ, as after a 73). No terms. Test: --ft8-test multi, dry on the FLEX: CQ KH8A answered, the report from "RC3KK RR73; OH6OKSA <KH8A> -16" (R-08 next), another DX's "OH6OKSA RR73; W9XYZ <KH1/KH7Z> -11" left alone, "OH6OKSA RR73; JK1TMP <KH8A> -16" -> "RR73 from KH8A: QSO logged" (a dry run keeps it out of the log), idle, no 73. Size: small.
60. **Fixes:** What a MIDI controller's VFO knob sends, in the log (Hanno Vogels DK3HV, mail 2026-11-11, build 166, iPad Air 5, CTR2-MIDI over Bluetooth: "When I turn clockwise OR counterclockwise… frequency goes always downwards in selected steps"; the user, 2026-11-11, chosen with 59; *in build 167*). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-11*: the decoder (src/core/MidiRelativeCcDecoder.h) and midiTune take the CTR2's documented 64 ± speed (65 / 63) right both ways, through ctr2-mock too; down both ways fits a knob whose values decode negative either way (a Learn that caught a CC with a constant value, or 127 / 63, for example), so the cure waits for what his CTR2 sends. Asked by mail (Last message clockwise and counter-clockwise; Learn or Import a map…). Meanwhile the log names the VFO knob's binding when the port opens and, for each turn (1.5 s without its messages starts the next), its first six messages (CC, value, channel) and the first six steps they gave, so a log from Log › Send to the developer answers it. No terms. Test: ctr2-mock on this PC, +3, −3, +5×3 and −5×3 detents at a 1000 Hz step: "CC 100 = 65" with +2 and +1 steps, "63" −1, "67" +3, "61" −3, the slice back at 14.074000. Size: small. *Second part* (the user, 2026-11-11: "Otetaan nämä seuraavaan buildiin"; *in build 168, in progress*), *pre-analysis 2026-11-11* (his pictures by mail: the VFO knob learned as Ch 1 CC #104; the CTR2-MIDI MIDI Mode Manual v3.00.01, Knob Mode Indications and the map defaults): a short press of the CTR2's knob steps its four knob modes, Home CC 100, Mode 1 CC 102, Mode 2 CC 104, Mode 3 CC 106 (push and turn the odd ones); Map 1 makes only CC 100 WheelA, every other knob function SliderA, a position 0-127 kept over power cycles. Learned for the VFO it is marked relative, the decoder locks on passing 63 / 65 (or 1 / 127), and a position below 64 then decodes negative whichever way it turns: his symptom. Told by mail the same day: long-press the knob back to Home (yellow LEDs off) and Learn the VFO knob again (CC 100). To build: a VFO knob that sends a position (consecutive values stepping by one, not centred on 64) named on Radio Setup › MIDI and in the log, with the CTR2's Home mode (CC 100) as the hint; not tuned by the position's change (a slider stops at 0 and 127, and a fast wheel could pass for one). Test: ctr2-mock with a slider mode. No terms. Size: small.

61. **Devices:** Elecraft KPA1500 on the Amp tab and the meters, read only first (Rich Yost N2RY, tester request 2026-11-11, FLEX-6500: "What I would like to test: ... KPA1500 amplifier"; the user, 2026-11-11: "Otetaan nämä seuraavaan buildiin"; *in build 168, in progress*). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-11* (Elecraft's KPA1500 Programming Reference V3, ftp.elecraft.com, a public vendor document; upstream #4097 and docs/architecture/kpa1500-amplifier-design.md; our engine): upstream's Kpa1500Protocol (framing, the status decode, tests) and Kpa1500Connection (TCP, a 500 ms poll, control and a bounded ^TX key nothing calls) are in src/core, unused by the app. The reference: a TCP server on port 1500 (^CP moves it) and a UDP server on the same port, "a single TCP client and any number of UDP clients may be concurrently active"; the amplifier never sends by itself; over UDP one command a packet, at most one answer. So the app reads it over UDP: Elecraft's own software (KPA1500 Utility / Remote) or a logger keeps the one TCP connection, and a lost answer only leaves a reading a tick old. Read: ^PWF / ^PWR / ^SW (tenths) / ^PWI (the drive) / ^VI (PA volts in tenths, amps) / ^TM / ^BN / ^FL (the fault table's text) / ^OS / ^AN / ^AM / ^AI (and the reference's ^AT0 typo) / ^TP / ^ON (main supplies) / ^RV; not the serial number. A reader of its own (mobile/src/Kpa1500Reader) that can build nothing but queries, paced one packet at a time. Control (the user, 2026-11-11, the same build: "kuten tehtiin rfkit"): off by default, Radio Setup › Amp › Control the amplifier opens the amplifier's TCP connection (upstream's Kpa1500Connection with its own poll off: commands only); ^OS0 / ^OS1, ^AN1 / ^AN2, ^AMI / ^AMB (the ATU MODE, as holding the front panel's button), ^FLC, ^FT / ^FE; each confirmed on the page, one at a time, never while the radio transmits, done when the UDP readback shows it (the amplifier answers no SET; 3 s, else said so). Operate's question names the radio's power (the owner's manual: 50-60 W for 1500 W, fault 60 "PWR INPUT" at 65 W). Tune: the manual's full search "Apply 10 to 50 W", in STBY or OPER (the PA bypassed while tuning): ^FT, then the radio's TUNE carrier at the TX tab's Tune power (10-50 W, else refused), ^TP and ^FL asked every cycle; done once ^TP reads 0 and a fresh ^FL shows no fault (a no match is fault 92 at the same moment), else ended on a fault, over 55 W, a lost link, Stop or 20 s, with ^FE. A control link the amplifier closes at once (its one TCP connection held by another program) shows as not connected and is tried every 5 s. Not done: keying over the network (^TX / ^RX, firmware 3.07: parallel with KEY IN, 5 ms lead before RF, the hot-switching question upstream left open); the amplifier stays on its KEY IN cable (Elecraft's Flex guide: TX1 to KEY IN, a KXUSB CAT cable in the radio's USB port for band data). App: Radio Setup › Amp › Elecraft (address, read on / off, control on / off, state, forget), the Amp tab's KPA1500 page (power, reflected, SWR, drive, temperature, voltage, current, band, antenna, ATU, operate, fault; with control Mode, Antenna, ATU, Tune, Clear fault), the meters' Amp readings (temperature red from 100 °C: it faults at 112), the logged power in OPERATE. Over SmartLink: no (a device on the operator's network: same network or VPN). Test: mobile/tools/kpa1500-mock.py (UDP and TCP, the reference's reply formats, acting on commands as the amplifier: keyed / fault / standby / supplies off / tune / no match scenes) with the FLEX connected; done on this PC: readings, fault, supplies off, silence and back, logged power (USB 5 W no audio), Operate / ANT2 / ATU bypass / clear (and a temperature fault not cleared), Tune keyed at 10 W on 14.345 USB (tuned; no match; Stop; 5 W refused), the TCP connection held; Rich on his amplifier. No terms (the reference and manual are public). Size: medium-large.

62. **Connect:** Two SmartLink accounts with favourite radios that connect in one tap (*(0)+(a)+(c) in build 178 / beta 93*; (b) later if at all) (Ian Gilchrist VK1HF's own build, mail 2026-11-09; the user, 2026-11-11: "Tutki myös tuo 2 smartlink tiliä onko mahdollista"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-11*: possible with no new sign-in or protocol work. Today one SmartLinkClient (src/core/backends/flex) signs in with Auth0's password grant, keeps the refresh token in the secure store under one key (smartlink_refresh_token) and the e-mail in AppSettings (SmartLinkEmail, written by SmartLinkClient itself); each client has its own TLS session to smartlink.flexradio.com, nothing global but those two keys (and the desktop's keychain entry, not built on iOS / Android). The radio list and connect go through the account's live session (connect by serial, then the WAN link). Saved radios today are by address or name only (MobileSavedRadios). Steps: (0) small: the client's own e-mail / keychain writes made optional, a disconnect that keeps the sign-in; (a) medium: an account list (e-mail, label; device-bound, not in exported setups), one secure-store key per account (the old key migrated), Add account, a switcher, Sign out per account, a refused sign-in's own e-mail filled in; (c) small-medium on (a): SmartLink favourites in the saved list (account, serial, name): a tap switches account if needed, waits for its radio list and connects by serial; (b) medium-large, later if at all: both accounts signed in at once with one merged list (two background TLS sessions on iOS, and whether the server takes two registrations from one device, unverified). Privacy page: each account's sign-in in the secure store, the e-mails and favourites on the device (also today's e-mail, not mentioned yet). Tests: two real FlexRadio accounts (a second without a radio is enough for sign-in and switching), restart, airplane mode, one signed out and the other kept, a favourite from a cold start, switching while connected (refused or disconnected first), SmartLink bursts, background / resume. Size: (0)+(a)+(c) one medium build.

63. ~~**Fixes:** Pair a Bluetooth MIDI controller… crashes on iOS 27~~ *in build 169* (Apple DTS's workaround; to test on iOS 27 devices in a tester preview) (Rich Yost N2RY, mail with his log 2026-11-12, build 168, iPad Pro 13 M4, iOS 27.0.1: "clicked MIDI tab, selected MIDI device dropdown, app crashed"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-12*: the log's previous run ends in SIGABRT from an Objective-C exception raised in a layout pass inside CoreAudioKit, no "MIDI: opened" before it, so the tap was Radio Setup › MIDI › Pair a Bluetooth MIDI controller… (ios/MidiPairing.mm). A known iOS 27 bug (Apple Developer Forums thread 850584, FB25301268): -[CABTMIDICentralViewController loadView] makes its table view with a NaN height and CALayer raises CALayerInvalidGeometry; it crashes with UIKit's present as well, on iPhone (27.0) and iPad Pro (27.0.1), iOS 26.6.2 works; one user writes 27.2 may have fixed it, Apple has not said. Apple DTS's workaround, confirmed on an iPad Pro with 27.0.1: on iOS 27 give the controller its table view before it is shown (tableView = a grouped UITableView of frame zero) and call its viewDidLoad (without that no devices are listed). Fix: that in showBluetoothMidiPairing under @available(iOS 27, *), non-ARC (autoreleased). Test: on an iPad and an iPhone with iOS 27 (the user's devices, a tester preview), the sheet opens, lists a Bluetooth MIDI device (or none) and Done closes it; on iOS 26 unchanged. Size: small. Marvin Turner W0MET (tester request 2026-11-12) wants a CTR2 on an iPad Pro 13 too.

64. ~~**Devices:** The KPA1500's tune seen to its end~~ *in build 169* (^FT; on the control link ends it, a log line a second; tested with the stand-in) (Rich Yost N2RY, the same log: on the real amplifier the UDP reading and the TCP control link came up at once; a tune at 13 W ran its carrier for 20 s and ended "No tune seen in 20 s"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-12*: the app only watches ^TP over UDP, in turn with the other queries (in practice about every 0.5 s while transmitting), so a full search that is over sooner (the stored settings are tried first) is never seen; the reference's own end signal goes unread: "When the tune completes or is cancelled, the KPA1500 responds with ^FT;" on the TCP link that sent it (Kpa1500Connection parses it but the app does not listen there). It is just as possible that the amplifier never tuned (no RF from the radio at its input, the ATU in bypass); the log has no readings to tell. To build: the ^FT; reply on the control link ends the tune (then ^FL read: 92 no match, otherwise tuned with the SWR read while the carrier is still on), ^TP asked first and every cycle while the tune runs, and one log line a second during a tune (^TP, forward, drive, SWR, ^FL, OPERATE / STANDBY, ATU) so a report tells what the amplifier did. The stand-in (kpa1500-mock.py) answers ^FT; at the end of its tune as the amplifier does, with a --tune-seconds 0.3 case. Ask Rich (back home on Sunday 2026-11-15) what the amplifier showed (ATU IN flashing, the relays) and for a log with Control on. Size: small.

65. **Display:** A compact portrait layout, Larry Averbeck W9FLA (FB comment on the build 168 post, 2026-11-12: "a complete standalone display option ... while keeping the current setup as the default"; the user, 2026-11-12: a number now, the details changed from Larry's answer). His layout: the frequency centred at the top with the meters either side; under it the slice letters A, B… (grey free, white in use, red the TX slice; a circle round a locked one, a bar through a split one; one tap selects, two lock, three split); the mode and step in the slice flag ("USB - S 500"); the values in three columns, receive (ANT, gain, BW, TNF, DSP), audio (AGC-T, squelch, speaker, record, play) and transmit (tune power, power, TX BW, TX antenna), two lines each of short text (A1 G0 B2.7 AGT35 SQ100); a tap steps a value down, a double tap up, a hold opens its panel, or a tap opens a small − / + that closes 5 s after the last tap; the band row over the step buttons. *Pre-analysis 2026-11-12*: one app, not a version of its own (App Store guideline 4.3 and Google Play's repetitive-content rule; builds, listings, notes, manual and screenshots would all double, and only the main page differs), and no layout question at each start: Controls › View › Portrait layout, Standard (the default) or Compact, remembered. A new AppSettings key, so App setups (17) carries it with no work (a setup is every key that is not device-bound): a "Mobile" setup can be Compact and a "Shack" one Standard. Only a phone held upright: PhoneOperatePage's portrait branch (landscape, width > height × 1.3, and portrait of 480 dp and up keep theirs); the iPad's OperatePage unchanged. Every value is already an Engine property (antenna, RF gain, filter, AGC-T, DSP, TNF, volume, squelch, record, play, RF and tune power, TX filter, TX antenna, the slices, TX slice, lock, split); the work is QML: the slice letters A–D (a tap selects, a tap on a free letter opens a slice there so + Slice goes, a hold opens a menu: Make TX slice and Close slice as in SliceMenu, lock, split; the selected slice blue, the TX slice a red letter, as selected and transmitting are not always one), value chips of 47 × 36 with the value inside (a tap opens − / + beside it, closing 5 s after the last tap; a hold the full chooser a tap opens today; no tap down / double tap up, as every tap would wait for a second and a missed double tap steps the wrong way), AGC-T with receive, the mode and the step as two taps in the slice flag. A mockup (a Design canvas: today, Larry's as written, the proposal to try, its states) sent to Larry on Messenger 2026-11-12 with four pictures, asking his view. Costs: the spectrum 46 px shorter than today at 390 × 844 (B/S still frees 92 px); every later UI change checked and pictured in both portrait layouts (render tool, manual). Open: seven chips a row with Android's larger font or screen zoom (Larry has a Galaxy S26 Ultra) and on a 375-point iPhone; VoiceOver / TalkBack names and the hold as an accessibility action; an iPad mini upright. Size: medium-large.

66. ~~**Display:** The passband runs off the edge of the view as tuning follows it~~ *in build 169* (the passband's far edge kept inside; tested on the FLEX at 1366x1024 and 390x844) (seen 2026-11-12 in the demo video's takes: the operated slice only partly on the display; the user: "Tuo muuten on bugi jos toimii noin"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-12*: MobileEngine::tuneTo, as the view follows the slice, keeps the carrier kPanFollowEdge (a tenth of the span) inside the edge, but an SSB passband lies on one side of the carrier (USB above, LSB below; 2.9 kHz with the user's phone filter, DIGU / DIGL 3 kHz), so at narrow spans it runs past the edge: at 10 kHz the margin is 1 kHz and an LSB passband tuned downwards shows only 1 of its 2.9 kHz (a USB one tuned upwards likewise on the right). The paths through tuneTo: dragging the spectrum (PanadapterView), the step buttons and keys, the MIDI VFO knob (midiTune), CW and RTTY tuning onto a signal (short moves) and DevLink; a typed frequency, a memory, a band, a spot and the FreeDV list are centred already (42). The demo video's render tool used tuneTo for its freq command until a94c1a6b2 (now tuneToCentred). Fix: keep the passband's far edge (the carrier plus the filter's low or high edge) the tenth inside instead of the carrier, and centre the passband when it is wider than the view less both margins; CW and AM (passband about the carrier) unchanged. Test on the FLEX: spans 5, 10 and 50 kHz, LSB down and USB up by drag, held step buttons and the MIDI knob (ctr2-mock), DIGU / DIGL and CW, rendered at 1366 × 1024 and 390 × 844 with the slice at the edge; wide spans as before. No terms. Size: small.

67. **Logging:** A spot from the Log tab to the DX cluster (*(a) in build 170*: Log tab › Spot; *(b) in build 171*: POTA self-spot), Christoph Steinert DM5HF (mail 2026-11-14, build 169, iPad Pro 13 M4, portable POTA with an Aurora: "adding spotting in the log tab for dx spots would be helpful"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-14*: the app already keeps a DX cluster connection (Controls › View › DX cluster, MobileEngine::setupDxCluster, logged in with the user's call), and DxClusterClient::sendCommand sends a line on it; the desktop forwards a spot the same way (MainWindow_Wiring.cpp: "DX <kHz> <call> <remark>", a default remark because DX Spider drops a spot without one). Glue: a Spot button in the Log tab beside Log, with the call and the slice frequency already there and a short comment field; a confirmation showing the exact line before it goes out (a public post under the user's call); dimmed with the reason when the cluster is not connected; the spot also arrives back on the display. Terms: cluster etiquette only (real stations heard, own call). Christoph's answer (mail 2026-11-15): "normal spotting would be good - POTA self spotting superb - if possible". So in steps: (a) the DX cluster spot above; (b) a POTA self-spot from My park (pota.app's spot API, as HAMRS and Ham2K PoLo post), its own pre-analysis first (the API's terms and whether it wants a key). Size: (a) small. *(b) chosen for build 171* (the user, 2026-11-15: "Lisää 1, 2 ja 3"). *Pre-analysis of (b) 2026-11-15*: POTA's spot API is unofficial and undocumented (it may change without notice), and wants no key or sign-in: a self-spot is a JSON POST to https://api.pota.app/spot/ with activator, spotter (both the own call), frequency (kHz as a string), reference (one park), mode, comments and source; OpenHamClock (server/routes/pota-spot.js: its own User-Agent, source "OpenHamClock", POTA's answer shown as it is, 30 s between spots) and hunterlog (src/programs/apis/pota.py) post so, the WRL app likewise; upstream's src/core/PotaClient (in our tree) only reads spots. Here: a Self-spot button by My reference on the Log tab when My park is POTA (and the radio's callsign set): asked first with exactly what goes out ("OH6OKSA at US-1234, 14285.0 kHz SSB, comment", a public post), sent with "OksaSDR/<version>" as the User-Agent and source "OksaSDR" (no borrowed Origin or Referer), POTA's answer under the row, one spot per 30 s. The mode by the slice: USB / LSB SSB, CW, AM, FM, DIGU / DIGL with FTx on FT8 or FT4, RTTY, else DATA. A two-fer (US-1234,US-5678 in My reference) asks which park, one spot each. My park moves from the Log tab's own state to the engine (this session, as now), so Big buttons' log (75) and the spot read the same. Privacy page: the spot's call, park and frequency go to pota.app only on the tap. Terms: POTA's own rules allow self-spotting; the API being unofficial, nothing automatic (no re-spot timer). Test: a stand-in for the endpoint on this PC (the URL settable in the dev build only), the dialog and answers (200, an error text, no network); one real self-spot only during a real activation, with the user's word or by a tester (Christoph). Size: (b) small.

68. ~~**Radio:** The voice keyer F1-F12 and the CW memories M1-M12 on a MIDI controller's buttons~~ *in build 170* (Radio Setup › MIDI › Macros: F1-F12, Stop), Christoph Steinert DM5HF (mail 2026-11-15, CTR2-MIDI: "it would be great to push a 'real' button on the CTR2 to play a DVK F-file"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15*: Radio Setup › MIDI › Functions (MobileEngineMidi.cpp) has no macro functions yet; the engine's dvkPlay(slot) and sendCwMemory(i) are what the keyboard's F-keys call (OperateKeys.qml), so a learned button calls the same (voice modes the voice keyer, CW the memories, RTTY / PSK the Digi macros, as the F-keys). They transmit, so they obey Transmit from the controller (Off: nothing). Import a map: check whether SmartSDR's .map or AetherSDR's .xml names such actions (the desktop has CwxF1-F12 for FlexControl and the Ulanzi). Test with ctr2-mock and on the FLEX. Size: small.

69. ~~**Display:** Big buttons: the step as two halves (smaller / larger) and a bigger Exit with a shorter hold~~ *in build 170*, Michael Walker (mail 2026-11-15, after a day of driving with big buttons: "On the Step bar, it is possible to split the Bar and have an UP and DOWN"; "The Exit needs to be a big bigger and less of a HOLD time by 1/2"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15*: BigButtonsView.qml's StepButton only cycles forward (big.nextStep), so a smaller step takes a lap; two halves (− / +) step through the same list. Exit's onPressAndHold uses Qt's press-and-hold time (the style hint, about 800 ms): a Timer at half of it, and a larger font and padding.
