70. ~~**Devices:** The Tuner Genius XL first on the Amp tab, and a refused Tune explained~~ *in build 170*, Hanno Vogels DK3HV (mail 2026-11-12, build 168, iPad Air 5, a Power Genius XL and a Tuner Genius XL: "I use the tuner 10 times more than amp. Or switch at least the tuner on top"; a photo: "The radio refused it: code 0x50001000"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15*: GeniusControls.qml lists the PGXL, the TGXL and the Antenna Genius in that order; the tuner moves above the amplifier (one block, no other change; Amp tabs without a tuner unchanged). Tune goes to the tuner over its own link (port 9010) when that is up (TunerModel::autoTune), else the radio's `tgxl autotune`, which his radio refused (0x50001000, not supported; the code notes the radio's path broke for some in firmware 4.2): the notice then says the link is down and what to check, not only the code. Size: small.

71. ~~**Radio:** SPLIT 1 kHz up in the data modes~~ *in build 170* (seen 2026-11-12 recording the demo video: DIGU's TX slice on 14.079, outside the FT8 segment). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15*: Engine.toggleSplit used 1 kHz in CW and 5 kHz in every other mode, the second slice and XIT alike, as SmartSDR and the desktop; a narrow mode's DX listens 1-2 kHz up (RTTY "up 1"), and FT8 / FT4 split by TX Hz, where 1 kHz up keeps the slice in the segment. DIGU, DIGL and RTTY take CW's 1 kHz; voice stays 5 kHz; Split up chooses as before. Size: small.

72. **Devices:** The KPA1500's tune carrier off as soon as the amplifier says it is done (*in build 171*, the user, 2026-11-15: "Nämä kaikki seuraavaan buildiin") (Rich Yost N2RY, mail with his log 2026-11-15, build 170, iPhone, iOS 27.0.1: two tunes ended right, "The amplifier tuned (SWR 1.10)" and "(SWR 1.20)", but the second's reading after ^FT was "out 777 W, drive 13 W", the first's 773 W; the user, 2026-11-15: "Kirjaa ehdotukset roadmappiin"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15*: after ^FT (or ^TP back to 0) checkKpaTune keeps the radio's TUNE carrier on for kKpaTuneSettleMs (700 ms) so that a fresh ^FL can show a no match (fault 92) and the SWR is read "while the carrier is still on" (64's design). The amplifier bypasses its PA only while it tunes; in OPERATE the PA is back in line once the tune is over, so the 13 W carrier goes out at about 775 W for those 0.7 s, every tune. No harm at SWR 1.1-1.2, but full power was never asked for. In STANDBY nothing changes. Even off at ^FT the carrier would meet the PA for the radio's own delay (tens to hundreds of ms over SmartLink or a VPN), so the fix is the RF2K-S's way: an amplifier in OPERATE is put in STANDBY first (^OS0, the readback awaited, 5 s at most; the manual's full search works in STBY with the PA bypassed), then the tune as now; and as the RF2K-S says, "Put it in OPERATE when you are ready". *As built (build 171)*: in STANDBY the carrier after ^FT is the tune drive into the ATU, not amplified, so the 0.7 s for a fresh ^FL and the tuned SWR stays (the SWR is worth showing); the carrier stops at once if the amplifier reads OPERATE during the tune (^OS asked every cycle with ^TP and ^FL), and an amplifier already in STANDBY is tuned as before. The 20 s limit, a fault during the search and Stop unchanged. *The other amplifiers checked* (the user, 2026-11-15: "huomaa tarkistaa myös tuo KPA ongelma muiden amplifierien osalta"): the RF2K-S (MobileEngineAmp.cpp) asks for STANDBY before the carrier and waits for it, ends when the tuner leaves AutoTuning, and stops the carrier if the amplifier reads OPERATE: never amplified. The Tuner Genius XL: the app only sends autotune (and its abort); the tuner keys the radio through its interlock cable and handles its amplifier's PTT itself, so the app holds no carrier. The Power Genius XL has no tune of its own in the app. The radio's own TUNE button with any amplifier keyed from RCA TX1-3 or ACC TX: Radio Setup's Inhibit during TUNE (this device; off by default, as the desktop's) keeps the chosen outputs open while TUNE or the ATU transmits; the KPA1500 tune goes through the same TUNE, so with its KEY IN output inhibited it was never keyed (Rich's was not). No other change needed. No terms. Test: kpa1500-mock.py made to act as the amplifier after a tune in OPERATE (forward = drive × its gain from the next reading after ^FT), with --tune-seconds 0.3 and a no match: no reading above the drive after ^FT, the result right; a live tune at 10 W on the FLEX with the stand-in; Rich on his amplifier (a log: the tune lines after ^FT show the radio not tuning). Size: small.

73. **Radio:** A MIDI Learn that takes a control another function has, said so instead of silently taking it over (*in build 171*) (Rich Yost N2RY, the same log, CTR2-Max: Learn Slice volume took the VFO knob's CC 100 first, so the knob changed the volume, audio_level 51 to 46, instead of tuning, until he learned Slice volume again on CC 105 and 102; the user, 2026-11-15: "Kirjaa ehdotukset roadmappiin"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15*: MidiControlManager's dispatch index (src/core) is keyed on the source (channel, type, number) without the function, so of two bindings on one source the last one learned answers and the older stops without a sign. The desktop's typed binding form asks first ("Source Already Bound", MidiMappingDialog.cpp, the overlap rule: the same type and number, the channels equal or either Any), its comment noting that Learn "rarely produces this"; Rich's log shows a learn doing it, as a knob moved by accident or one sending more than one thing (a press with a turn) is learned first. Fix in the app's learn (MobileEngineMidi.cpp's learnCompleted, Radio Setup › MIDI): when the learned source is another function's by the same overlap rule, the row says so ("CC #100 is the VFO knob's") with Replace (the other function unbound) and Learn again (the old binding kept, the learn waits for another control); nothing changes until one is tapped. Import a map… keeps its own rule (a map's later row wins), said in its result if it shadowed any. Inline in the row, not a popup (the keyboard stays with the page). No terms. Test: ctr2-mock on this PC: the VFO knob on CC 100, Learn Slice volume, turn the VFO knob: named; Learn again, then another knob: both work; Replace: the knob sets the volume and the VFO knob row shows none; every layout of Radio Setup › MIDI rendered (phone upright, iPad, the narrowest) with the longer row. Size: small.

74. **Audio:** Receive audio packets put back in order before decoding, upstream's fix for #6051 (PR #6992, rfoust, merged 2026-11-15), *widened* to bring the receive path to upstream's (the user, 2026-11-15: "Tehdään näin": #6886 late FFT and waterfall packets no longer drop the frame in progress and pan teardown no longer races the decoder; #6704 a 1-byte datagram to the radio's VITA port 4993 every 5 s keeps a stateful firewall's return path open; #6700 IPv6 binding left out, as its own text says the radio binds IPv4 only; #6855 / #6874 desktop seam refactors left out) (*in build 171*, the user, 2026-11-15: "Lisää 1, 2 ja 3"; the developer's own radio is reached over LTE and WireGuard, where packets arrive out of order). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15*: today PanadapterStream (src/core/backends/flex) counts loss net of reordering (VitaSequenceTracker) but decodes each audio packet as it comes: a packet that overtakes another makes the decoder conceal the gap, and the late one is dropped (its slot concealed already), so a reordering path sounds like loss. Upstream's VitaRxReorder.h (229 lines, header only) holds a bounded window per stream: in-order packets pass at once, a missing successor is waited for at most 30 ms (bounded by one 16-count sequence cycle and a byte limit), then real loss goes to the existing capped concealment; Opus packets are ordered by their timestamps (a backward clock step over 60 s restarts, as FlexLib), PCM by the 4-bit count with the documented limit (13-15 lost packets followed by a bunched recovery can be discarded uncounted). Display packets (FFT, waterfall, meters) stay immediate. Our PanadapterStream has drifted from upstream's (about 540 lines: upstream's receive-telemetry migration #6874 and the display late-frame lookback are not here), so the PR does not apply; a hand port of the audio part only: VitaRxReorder with the Ordered and Opus timestamp keys (the display-frame key needs the lookback we lack, and display stays immediate anyway), the speaker streams' PCM and Opus through it before decoding, a 30 ms flush timer on the stream's thread, a stream's window cleared on its removal and on format or route changes, one Opus decoder per stream as upstream (today one shared). Upstream's tests/vita_rx_reorder_test.cpp (332 lines) ported for the kept parts and run in the desktop build. Latency: none in order; at most 30 ms only at a gap. Not expected to explain Barry Cohen's 30 s drops (an idea above, waiting for his log). No terms (GPLv3, our engine). Test: the unit tests; on the FLEX over the developer's WireGuard / LTE path and the LAN, PCM and Opus (SmartLink), with the receive statistics in the log (reordered, lost, concealed) before and after over the same minutes, by ear on a phone and an iPad; background / resume and a stream change (a slice closed and opened) for a stuck window. Size: medium.

75. **Display:** Big buttons: a Log button to a big-text log entry, the call and the report (Michael Walker, mail 2026-11-15: "Can you have a button to move to a LOG screen where you just type in the callsign and maybe the signal report?") (*in build 171*, the user, 2026-11-15: "Lisää 1, 2 ja 3"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15*: BigButtonsView.qml (over the operating page, not a popup) has Exit, the slice chips, the mode, the frequency, two meters, − / +, Step and PTT; the Log tab (LogControls.qml) logs with Engine.logQso({call, rstSent, rstRcvd, comment, timeOn, sig, sigInfo, mySig, mySigInfo}). To build: a Log button in Big buttons' top row beside the mode; it turns the overlay's lower part into a log entry in the same overlay: the call in a large field (readingPx), RST sent and received prefilled by mode from Engine.qsoDefaults() (as the Log tab: 59 / 599) as big − / + steppers rather than typing, Log (the call shown on it) and Cancel; the time on taken when the call is first typed, My park from the engine (moved there with 67 (b)), so an activator's QSOs carry the park; a logged QSO shown for a moment, then back to the buttons. The on-screen keyboard: on a phone upright the field and Log sit above it (about the upper half), on its side in one row at the top; with a hardware keyboard Return logs and Esc cancels. After Log or Cancel the page takes the keyboard back (Space PTT, the focussweep), as every text field must. PTT stays reachable (the overlay keeps it at the bottom while the keyboard is down). Callsign lookup's name shown under the call when it has one (the Log tab's lookup). Siri ("Hey Siri, log VA3MW") stays an idea. No terms. Test: rendered at the seven Big buttons sizes against build 170 (no empty space, nothing under the keyboard), the focussweep, a test QSO logged by the controls and removed from the test log, Return / Esc on a hardware keyboard, the iPhone and Android keyboards on the devices. Size: small-medium.

76. **Logging:** A clear button in the Log tab's callsign field (*in build 172*) (Christoph Steinert DM5HF, mail 2026-11-15, build 170 on a Mac: "a delete button would help in case of typing the wrong call"; the user, 2026-11-15: "Buildiin ehdottomasti"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15*: LogControls.qml's callField has no clear; its text is Engine.workedCall, shared with Big buttons' log and the CW tab's pick. An × inside the field's right end while it holds text, a tap clearing the call (and with it the time on and the lookup card), the keyboard left where it was. No terms. Test: the Log tab by the controls, rendered at a phone and an iPad size. Size: tiny.

77. **Logging:** A Name field beside Comment, filled with the lookup's first name (*in build 172*) (Christoph, the same mail: "beside comment a name field would be cool, great would be if this field would catch the qrz first name (no prio)"; chosen with 76). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15*: the lookup already fills ADIF NAME with QRZ.com's full display name (addLookupFields) and the lookup map has firstName; the operator never sees it. To build: Comment and Name side by side (Name a third of the row), Name filled from the lookup's first name when it arrives unless typed into, cleared with the call; logQso takes the field's name as NAME over the lookup's (an empty field leaves the lookup's). Big buttons' log keeps the lookup's. No terms. Test: a lookup with QRZ.com (W1AW), a typed name, an empty one, dry-logged; the Log tab rendered. Size: small.

78. **Logging:** Editing or deleting a contact already logged (*(a) in build 172*) (Christoph, the same mail: "if logged a wrong callsign there is currently no way to edit the logged contact? Double click for adjusting would be very helpful"; chosen with 76). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15*: QsoLog only appends (the ADIF file and its copy in Files / Documents); a logged contact also went to the logbooks set up: Wavelog (legacy /api/qso, insert only; Wavelog's v2 API has PATCH and DELETE by its own QSO id, which the app does not keep), the QRZ.com logbook (INSERT; an edit would be REPLACE, or DELETE by a LOGID the app does not keep), the shared ADIF file and Logger32 (UDP, no edit). So in steps: (a) this build: a tap on a contact in the Log tab's list opens it (call, date and time, band and frequency, mode, sent and received reports, name, comment, the parks) with Save, Delete (asked again) and Cancel; the app's own log rewritten in one safe write (the file and its copy), worked before and dupes counted again; the dialog says the logbooks it already went to are not changed (and names them). (b) later: the same edit in QRZ.com and Wavelog, keeping each service's id from the insert. No terms. Test: edit and delete on a copy of the dev log (not the user's), the file's other records byte-identical, the copy in Documents the same, a restart reads it back; rendered at a phone and an iPad size. Size: (a) medium. Christoph Steinert DM5HF (mail 2026-11-21 19:09): a contact deleted from the log stays in the **shared ADIF** file ("would be great to delete the contact in the shared adif as well"); the shared file is appended per QSO, so a deletion (and an edit) should rewrite or mark it there too.

79. **Logging:** Logging by voice, step 1: a voice button with our own phonetics, tied to no assistant (*in build 172*, to verify on devices; build 174: the older and DX spelling words, after Michael Walker's iPhone heard "Victor Alpha three sugar Kilo" right and the parser left the field empty: the Allied 1943-1956 alphabet, the ITU's of 1927, the DX places, letter names, two-word forms, digit homophones between spelled characters, "fifty nine", fillers left out; 41 parser cases, the manual lists the words; beta 87: on Android the default recogniser listens to the microphone itself (startListening while held), as Google's recogniser over the network returned nothing for the recording handed to it by EXTRA_AUDIO_SOURCE on the developer's phone (beta 85: 0 alternatives, 3 tries); the log gets the recogniser's steps and loudest level; beta 88: beta 87 heard speech (10 dB) and wrote nothing, 0 alternatives, error 7: the Google app's recogniser by component, else the default; beta 89: the on-device recogniser first when checkRecognitionSupport lists English installed (the model beta 85 asked to download); beta 90: beta 88 got text from com.google.android.tts (no Google app on the phone) but plain words for the alphabet ("Oscar Auto 6 Oscar kilo Seattle"), so the NATO words go as biasing strings to the on-device recogniser only, and each alternative is logged on its own line; beta 91: beta 90's biasing strings made the on-device service disconnect (error 11) at every press or hang after ready, so no biasing on Android at all, the probe recogniser listens, English checked once a run, error 11 before ready falls back to the default recogniser; OPEN after beta 89/90 on the developer's phone (2026-11-15 23:36): the on-device recogniser (English installed) gave 3-5 alternatives without a call, then mostly error 7 (nothing recognised) and error 11 (server disconnected) right at the start of each press, likely because the checkRecognitionSupport probe recogniser is destroyed and a new on-device one created at once: reuse the probe for listening and check English once per run; Android's general recognisers write plain English ("Oscar Auto 6 Oscar kilo Seattle"). Option to weigh: an own offline recogniser with a grammar of only the alphabet, digits and separators (Vosk's small English model, Apache 2.0, about 40 MB, grammar-restricted recognition), the same on Android and the desktop, as accurate as such a closed vocabulary allows; the size, the model download and a pre-analysis first; partial results kept for an empty final answer, no biasing strings, the log lists the services, the one used and the text) (Michael Walker, mail 2026-11-15: "Hey Siri, log VA3MW"; then "that LOG window is 'good enough'! If we get a Voice version, even better"; the user, 2026-11-15: this build or the next). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15* (the voice idea above, layers 0 and 1): (0) a parser in C++ (mobile/src/CallsignSpeech): NATO and ITU words (alfa, juliett, x-ray), plain letters, digits as numerals or words (niner, fife, tree, oh), slash / stroke / portable, optional "five nine" as the report; the result checked as a call (letters and digits, one at least of each) and read back; unit tests. (1) a microphone button beside the call field on the Log tab and in Big buttons' log: hold or tap to listen, the call filled from what was heard (never logged without the operator's Log tap). iOS (iPad, iPhone, the Mac): the Speech framework, on-device recognition (SFSpeechRecognizer, requiresOnDeviceRecognition) fed from the microphone the app already captures (AudioEngine's input, so no second capture of the same microphone), contextual strings for the NATO words, NSSpeechRecognitionUsageDescription in Info.plist (a new iOS permission prompt), mobile/ios/VoiceInput.mm. Android: SpeechRecognizer (on-device where offered, API 31+) from Platform.java over JNI; Android 10+ lets one app capture at a time, so the app's own capture pauses while it listens (or, on Android 13+, its audio is handed to the recognizer). Never while transmitting; the receive audio ducked while listening (the speaker bleeds into the microphone). The desktop (this PC) has no recognizer: a render-tool command feeds text so the parser and the button's flow are tested here; the recognisers on the user's iPad, iPhone and phones through a preview build before release. Languages (the user, 2026-11-15: "joka maassa puhutaan eri kieltä"): step 1 reads the ITU / NATO spelling alphabet in English (Alfa ... Zulu, niner: the radio regulations' alphabet, used on the air worldwide), so the recogniser runs in English whatever the device's language (Android may first download its English model: to see on the devices); national alphabets (Finnish Aarne, Bertta...; German Anton, Berta...; Spanish...) and digits in that language are a later step, one language at a time on request (a recogniser language setting, a word table and its tests each: the parser is table-driven), Finnish and German first. Terms: Apple's speech recognition (on device, nothing sent with requiresOnDeviceRecognition), Google's on-device recogniser; the privacy page names them. Size: medium-large.

80. **Radio:** The RIT and XIT offsets typed, and SPLIT by XIT to any offset (*in build 173*) (Sebastian Schumann DG5CST, mail 2026-11-15: "The Split xit function is great. Could it be possible to not just have fixed xit values? Let's say a kind of slider or put in for ex 1.34 kHz could be handy"; the user, 2026-11-15: "Mieti Sebin ehdotusta"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15*: the DSP tab's RIT and XIT rows (RxControls.qml OffsetRow) step 10 Hz a tap (50 and 100 Hz held) and a tap on the value resets it; SPLIT by XIT starts at 1 kHz (CW, data) or 5 kHz (voice), held SPLIT +1 / +2 / +5 kHz; the engine clamps to ±9.999 kHz (the radio's). The desktop has no typing either (a wheel over the value, 10 Hz); a slider over ±10 kHz is 20 Hz a pixel on a phone, too coarse to set 1.34 kHz, so typing first. To build: a tap on the value opens a small editor over it: the offset typed (kHz with a decimal, "1.34", "-0.5"; a whole number of 10 or more read as Hz, "1340"), quick values (−1, +1, +2, +3, +5 kHz), Reset (0) and Done; a value set turns that RIT / XIT on; Return applies and Esc closes on a keyboard, and the page has the keyboard back after. Held SPLIT (by XIT) gets "Other…" opening the same editor for the XIT, so "up 1.34" is two taps and the number. Beyond ±9.999 kHz is refused with the limit named. No terms. Test on the FLEX: 1.34 → xit_freq=1340 xit_on=1 read back; -0.5 → -500; 12.5 refused; 1340 → 1340 Hz; RIT the same; held SPLIT › Other… 1.34 in CW; reset; rendered at 440x956, 360x800 and 1366x1024; RIT / XIT put back to 0 and off. Size: small.
81. **Logging:** Voice entry that works on Android, and a spelling language for the user (*step 1 in Android beta 92, step 2, Vosk on iOS too, in build 178 / beta 93*; national spelling alphabets when users ask) (the user, 2026-11-15 night, after Android betas 85-91 on the developer's phone: "Tee Vosk siihen varmaan sitten helpompi jatkossa lisätä kieliäkin jos haluaa", "Ja sitten kielivalinta käyttäjälle", "Esimerkiksi suomen kieli suomessahan nuo aakkoset ovat erilaiset", "oletusvalintanahan voisi toimia aseman omistajan kutsu että siitä tunnistettaisiin se kieli tai sitten vaihtoehtoisesti sen voi vaihtaa kielivalikosta aina"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15*: (a) Why: Android's own recognisers on the developer's phone (OnePlus, Android 16, Finnish locale) cannot take the English alphabet: Google's over the network (com.google.android.tts) writes plain English words ("Oscar Auto 6 Oscar kilo Seattle" for "Oskar Hotel six Oscar Kilo Sierra Alfa"), the on-device one (com.google.android.as, English installed) hears speech but answers nothing (error 7) or drops out (error 11), and both fail outright with hints (biasing strings). iOS works (Apple's recogniser with contextual strings; Michael Walker's iPhone heard every word). (b) Vosk (alphacephei, Apache 2.0, Kaldi-based, offline): a C library (libvosk.so, release 0.3.45: arm64-v8a 8.9 MB needing only liblog / libm / libdl / libc; Linux x86_64 for the desktop tests) with a C API (vosk_model_new, vosk_recognizer_new_grm with a JSON grammar, accept_waveform_s, final_result); recognition restricted to a grammar of only the alphabet's words, the digits, the separators and the report, so "Hotel" cannot come out as "Auto": the closed vocabulary is what makes it accurate whatever the accent. It reads the app's own recording (the iOS path: AudioEngine's clip recorder, 16 kHz 16-bit after resampling), so the Android recogniser services and their quirks are out of it; a few hundred ms for an 8 s clip on a phone. Not on iOS (no official build; Apple's recogniser works there). (c) Models (small ones take a runtime grammar): English vosk-model-small-en-us-0.15, 41 MB (SHA-256 30f26242...0942498), Apache 2.0; German small-de-0.15 45 MB, French, Spanish, Italian, Dutch, Polish, Russian, Ukrainian, Czech, Turkish, Portuguese and more ~40-50 MB each, Swedish only 289 MB, NO Finnish model. Where from: bundled in the APK (+41 MB to ~160 MB, works at once offline) or downloaded on first use (from our own GitHub release asset, a copy of the model, checked by SHA-256; no third-party request). (d) Spelling languages: a table per language in CallsignSpeech (letters, digits, separators, report words) with its own tests: English ITU / NATO with the older and DX words (today's); Suomi: Aarne, Bertta, Celsius, Daavid, Eemeli, Faarao, Gideon, Heikki, Iivari, Jussi, Kalle, Lauri, Matti, Niilo, Otto, Paavo, Kuu, Risto, Sakari, Tyyne, Urho, Vihtori, Viski, Äksä, Yrjö, Tseta (Finnish Armed Forces' alphabet; Å Åke, Ä Äiti, Ö Öljy not in calls), digits nolla ... yhdeksän, "kautta" / "vinoviiva" for /, "portable"; Deutsch (DIN 5009: Anton, Berta... and its 2022 city names) and others later, one at a time on request. Recognisers by language: iOS Apple's in that language (fi-FI, de-DE) with its words as contextual strings; Android English (and the languages Vosk has) by Vosk; Suomi on Android by the phone's own recogniser in fi-FI (no Vosk model; the alphabet's words are ordinary Finnish names, which a Finnish recogniser writes right; to verify on the developer's phone). (e) The choice: a Spelling language row on the Log tab beside the voice button's line (and in Radio Setup › Logging), default from the station's own callsign: its prefix's country (the cty data the app already has) to a language with a table (OH / OG / OF -> Suomi, DL / DA-DR -> Deutsch when it exists), else English; changed at any time from the menu. (f) Privacy page: Vosk runs on the device, nothing sent; the model download (if chosen) is one request to GitHub. Licences page: Vosk and Kaldi Apache 2.0, the model Apache 2.0. (g) Tests: the parser per language (--voice-selftest), Vosk on the desktop with the grammar on recorded or synthesized speech (this PC has no speech synthesis: a TTS voice or the developer's own recordings), then the developer's Android phone through devlink (every alternative in the log since beta 90). Size: large (Vosk integration medium, Finnish table small, the choice and default small, model packaging small to medium). To choose: the English model bundled (recommended: works at once offline, in the car too) or downloaded; Suomi in the same build or after English works. Decided 2026-11-16 ("näin aluksi tosiaan voidaan mennä englannilla"): English first, the model bundled. Step 1 *in Android beta 92*: mobile/src/VoskSpeech (libvosk by dlopen, the model copied out of the APK assets by a file list, the grammar CallsignSpeech::recognizerWords() less the plain letters and the 1927 words, 0.4 s of silence each side, Vosk's locale decimal comma repaired before the JSON is read), CI fetches vosk-android-0.3.45.zip and the model by SHA-256, the render tool's voicewav; on this PC with two piper voices: clean 20/20, 20 dB SNR 19/20, 10 dB 19/20; 8 kHz narrowband (an old Bluetooth headset) about half: noted in the manual. Next: Suomi, the choice and its default from the own call. *Research 2026-11-16* (the user: "voitaisiin tutkia mille kaikille kielille löytyy nämä amatööri aakkoset omalla kielellään, onko monia versioita ja mitä voitaisiin tukea Voskilla"; 25 languages, 47 variants, sources in the scratchpad report): national letter words are used on the air by hams in Czech (decree 155/2005, required domestically), Russian (order 184/2012, Morse-mapped: W Василий, V жук, Q щука; "/" дробь), Ukrainian (2023 regulation, Morse-mapped), Slovenian (the ZRS handbook teaches it), Hungarian (the amateur decree), Polish (first names, two ham versions), Finnish (Aarne, Bertta …; the Defence Forces moved to NATO + Å Ä Ö in 2005) and Turkish (city names, the set disputed); partly Swedish, German and Italian; ICAO letters in the national language elsewhere (NL, FR, ES, PT, BR, RO, CH, JA, GR for calls), but the digits are spoken in the national language almost everywhere (Japan uses English). Several versions in many languages (German DIN 5009 1950 / 2022 city names, Austria ÖNORM / 2025, Swiss; Polish SP5HS 1980 / SQ3ME 2024; Turkish TS 13148 / ANTRAK / army). Vosk small models (dynamic grammar) exist for de, fr, es, it, nl, pt, pl, cs, ru, uk, tr, ja (and sv only as a 289 MB repack); none for fi, no, da, is, et, hu, sk, sl, hr, sr, ro, and Greek only as a big server model. Vocabulary check (vosk_model_find_word, the downloaded models): de DIN 1950 46/48 (Xanthippe, Eszett missing), AT ÖNORM 48/48, fr 37/37, it 53/55, es 38/39, nl 43/48, pl 38/40 (Xawery, Ypsylon), cs 43/49 (Čeněk, Gustav, Quido, Těšnov, dvojité v, Xaver), ru 64/66, uk 44/45, tr 36/41, pt place names 24/36; the ICAO words: it / nl 26/28, fr 24/28, de / es 23/28, pl 22/28, pt 18/28, cs 15/28, tr 12/28, ru / uk 0/28 (foxtrot, juliett and x-ray missing nearly everywhere). So: Vosk can take cs, ru, uk, pl, de, it, tr and the ICAO-letters-with-national-digits languages (fr, es, nl, pt) with a few words replaced by a spelling the model knows or added to its lexicon (a recompiled model); fi, hu and sl (national spelling common) need another recogniser (Apple's in that language on iOS, the phone's own on Android, or Whisper). Models of other languages downloaded on first use (40-50 MB each, our own GitHub release copy by SHA-256), English stays bundled. Beta 92 on the developer's OnePlus (2026-11-16, devlink): OH6OKSA 2 of 2 with all five alternatives the same call, SP3JHO ("sugar papa tree juliet hotel oscar") once, once SP3FYJO (an extra "foxtrot yoke" heard), twice nothing with a good level, once cut by the 8 s limit; the user: "Vosk tosiaan toimii paremmin". Build 174 on the iPhone (Apple's recogniser) mostly OH6OK: the parser stops at the first word it does not know, so a misheard "Sierra" filled a cut call. *Step 2 chosen for build 178 / beta 93* (the user, 2026-11-16: "Eikös täältä löydy suoraan myös ipad tuki", then "62, Android symbolit, 54"): Vosk on iOS too (react-native-vosk's libvosk.xcframework, Alphacephei's iOS build made public there in 2023, Apache 2.0, pinned by SHA-256; arm64 runs on iPhone, iPad and a Mac running the iPad app; needs only Accelerate); a call cut by an unknown word is not filled; the recording up to 15 s; one spelling per word in the grammar, so the five alternatives are different hearings. Decided 2026-11-16 after the research (the user: "Pysytään nyt aluksi tuossa englannissa palataan kieliin kun kyselyitä niistä tulee"): English only for now; another spelling language when users ask for one, then by the research above.
82. **Display:** The band plan for the operator's own licence class, Bill Sturm (FB post 2026-11-16, iPad and FLEX-8400, iOS 27.2.1: "I can't seem to tell where the band edges are on the screen", "It would also be good to add license class and show band edges for the operators license class"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-16*: the edges are there (Controls › View › Band plan), but the default is IARU Region 1 (MobileEngine::bandPlan: the first users were in Europe), so a US station sees Region 1's segments, not the ARRL's, unless it chooses ARRL (US): the strip at the foot of the spectrum, each segment in its colour, those open to fewer classes darker (SpectrumItem::drawBandPlan, the desktop's contrast), but the strip does not say which class a segment is for. The data has it: arrl-us.json (T / G / E per segment, "E,G" etc.) and rac-canada.json (B / BH / A); the IARU plans and SSA have no classes. The desktop keeps the operator's class (AppSettings OperatorLicenseClass, used by the ATU pre-tune) and labels a wide segment with its lowest class ("PHONE General"). Here: a Licence class row under Band plan in Controls › View, shown only for a plan with classes (ARRL: Technician / General / Extra; RAC: Basic / Basic with Honours / Advanced; none by default = as today); with a class chosen, the segments it may not use drawn dim and crossed by thin diagonal lines, the label with the class where it fits ("PHONE Extra"), and a line on the spectrum at each edge where the class's privileges begin or end; nothing blocks transmitting (the radio's own TX band limits stay the guard), a notice by PTT on an out-of-class frequency to weigh (no room on a phone: decide from a picture). The class kept per plan and in App setups. With it the default plan from the station's own callsign (as the spelling language in 81: the cty data's country; K / W / N / AA-AL -> ARRL (US), VE / VA -> RAC (Canada), SM / SA -> SSA (Sweden), else the IARU region of its continent), the one chosen kept. Terms: none (the plans are already bundled). Test: the render tool on 20 m and 40 m with each class, phone and iPad sizes, the strip and the edge lines checked against the ARRL chart. Size: small. Held 2026-11-16 (the user, not in the build with 84): "siinä on epäselvää mistä se lisenssitieto tulisi pelkkä kutsu ei nyt riitä kertomaan ... onko hän perusluokkalainen vai yleisluokkalainen ... en mä edes tiedä ovatko luokat samoja maailmalla". To settle before it is built: the class can only be chosen by the operator (no callsign tells it, outside a few countries' call blocks), and the classes differ by country (ARRL T / G / E, RAC B / BH / A, the CEPT / HAREC split elsewhere, Finland's yleisluokka / perusluokka, none in the IARU plans' data); which countries' classes to offer, and what a station of a country without class data sees. Held again 2026-11-18 (the user: "Edelleenkään ei ole selvää tiedämmekö aidosti kaikki 82 tarvittavat lupatiedot joka maasta"): only the ARRL and RAC plans carry licence classes; before building, which countries have classes with their own band privileges, and from where each country's data would come and be kept up to date.
83. **Devices:** The Ulanzi D100H dial (Ulanzi Dial) as a control on Android, iPad and the Mac (*in build 190 / Android beta 102*, fixes in build 192 / beta 104), Christoph Steinert DM5HF (mail 2026-11-14, build 169, iPad Pro 13 M4: "would it be possible to add the Ulanzi D100 like in AetherSDR (more convenient use for portable due to the internal accumulator)", for the voice keyer; the user, 2026-11-16: "Ulanzi D100H pitäisi ainakin tutkia varmaan seuraavaksi"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-16*: (a) The device: Ulanzi D100H "Dial Creative Controller" (I003, presented March 2026, about 40-60 EUR), Bluetooth LE 5.0 only (USB-C charges), up to three paired hosts, a 1000 mAh battery, a haptic knob, the knob press, three keys on top and two on each side. It is a plain Bluetooth keyboard (HID over GATT, VendorID 0xFFF1, ProductID 0x0082, named "Ulanzi Dial Keyboard"): the knob sends Volume Up / Down (held while turning, so autorepeat), its press Mute, the top keys Previous / Play-Pause / Next, the side keys Ctrl+V, Ctrl+C, Ctrl+Y, Ctrl+Z (Mode Cycle Ctrl+Y with Previous); Ulanzi Studio remaps only on a computer where it runs (nothing kept in the dial). The desktop reads it raw (evdev / IOKit / hidapi) and decodes it with src/core/UlanziChordDecoder (pure, Linux key codes, its own test), which the app can reuse. (b) Android: every control reaches the app while it is in front: OksaActivity.dispatchKeyEvent sees the volume, mute, media and Ctrl keys with press and release and the InputDevice (vendor / product), so only the dial's keys are taken (the phone's own volume buttons and a headset keep theirs); the codes mapped to the Linux ones and fed to UlanziChordDecoder; the knob as a VFO knob (or another wheel action), each key an action, press-and-hold PTT possible. In the background the keys are the system's (media keys to the media session). (c) iPadOS and the Mac (the iPad app): the system keeps the consumer keys: the knob and its press stay the device's volume and mute (a fair use: the listening volume), and App Store guideline 2.5.9 rejects apps that take over the volume keys, so no volume-observation trick. The side keys come through GCKeyboard (SideKeys.mm already reads it) with press and release, so they can key PTT and the voice keyer; the top keys as MPRemoteCommandCenter commands (previous / play-pause / next, a press only, while the app is the Now Playing app; shared with 41 (b)). So on the iPad and Mac: 4 keys with any action (hold included) plus 3 keys as triggers, no tuning by the knob; enough for Christoph's voice keyer, for tuning there the CTR2 (54) stays the answer. (d) Settings: Radio Setup › Ulanzi Dial (beside MIDI): on / off (on Android off by default until the dial is seen), a row per control with the same action list as MIDI's buttons and the knob's wheel actions (VFO, volume, filter, RIT / XIT, zoom), the last event shown; no TX action by default. (e) Terms: none (a keyboard; no Ulanzi SDK). Privacy: nothing leaves the device. (f) Test: a key-event stand-in on this PC through the same decoder (like OKSASDR_MIDI_FAKE), then a real D100H on the OnePlus and the iPad (the developer has none yet; Christoph on his iPad Pro and Mac); hold PTT released when the dial goes (Bluetooth off, out of range), the phone's own volume buttons untouched. Size: Android small-medium, iPad / Mac keys small (the remote commands with 41 (b)). Christoph (mail 2026-11-16 evening, OksaSDR on his Mac, with a D100H): with Ulanzi Studio running on the Mac the dial already works: his mapping (a screenshot) turns the knob into the arrow keys (→ / ←, so OksaSDR tunes), the top keys into F1-F3 (the voice keyer slots) and the side keys into PTT, VOX, ATU and a two-tone tune ("you will not belief - it works with the VFO", with a spot made that way). So on the Mac the app needs nothing for it (worth a manual note: Ulanzi Studio's key combinations for the knob and keys); on the iPad Studio does not run and keeps no mapping in the dial, so (c) stands. His wish beside it: "a streamdeck integration (or the cheaper Ulanzi one)": an idea, the same key shortcuts on a Mac; no way in on the iPad. *Chosen for build 190* (the user, 2026-11-19: "83 mukaan"). *Done in the dev build (2026-11-19)*, as another controller on Radio Setup › MIDI rather than a tab of its own (the tab row stays as it is): **Ulanzi Dial** on there makes Learn take the dial's controls for the same functions as a MIDI controller's (MobileEngineUlanzi.cpp: src/core's UlanziChordDecoder, the desktop's, makes Linux key codes into the knob's steps and the controls' presses and releases; the knob for the VFO knob or a level, slice volume, AGC-T, RF power or CW speed, a step a detent; the keys for the buttons' functions, a held one (PTT, the paddles, the straight key) while down; one dial control a function, a control moved from another function said so, a knob pressed for a button's function or the other way round refused with a line); under the same **Transmit from the controller**; until learned otherwise the knob is the VFO knob and its press Step larger, no transmit function; the function rows show both sources ("Note 6 · Dial Ctrl+C"); Last message names the dial's control; × and Clear all clear the dial's too; a transmission the dial started ends when the dial goes. Android: OksaActivity.dispatchKeyEvent takes only the dial's keys (vendor 0xFFF1 / product 0x0082, or a name beginning "Ulanzi") while the switch is on, through a registered native ulanziKey (QJniEnvironment), so the phone's own volume buttons, a headset and other keyboards keep theirs; the dial removed (InputDeviceListener) or the app paused sends -1, which lets go of all; the line names a connected dial (ulanziDevice). iOS: SideKeys.mm's GCKeyboard handler hands Control and V / C / Y / Z on while the switch is on (iPadOS keeps the knob and the top keys as volume and media keys), -1 when the keyboard goes or a text field takes the keys; with the dial on, Control is no CW paddle; not on the iPad app on a Mac (GameController is off there; the switch is hidden and the line points to Ulanzi Studio). The render tool's "ulanzi KEY VALUE". Tested on this PC against the FLEX at 440x956 by the user's path (the switch, Learn, the keys in the decoder's codes): the knob +2 / −2 at 1 kHz (14.074 → 14.076 → 14.074); Learn Step smaller, Ctrl+Z: learned, then 1000 → 500 Hz; Learn VFO knob with Ctrl+C: refused with the line; Learn PTT (hold), Ctrl+C: with Transmit from the controller off nothing keyed, on (allowed through its question): USB 5 W no audio, "radio transmitting: yes" while held, no on release; held and the dial gone: released ("Ulanzi Dial: gone, what it keyed ends"); Learn F2 with Ctrl+C: moved from PTT (hold). The Java compiled here against android-36's android.jar (a stand-in QtActivity); the Android and iOS key paths themselves only on a device with a dial (Christoph's iPad Pro and Mac; nobody with a dial on Android yet). Put back: the step 1 kHz, DIGU, 100 W, Transmit from the controller off, the dial off. *Found after release (2026-11-19, the review after the push; the user: "korjaa ja miksi Huomasit vasta nyt")*: (a) the dial's "it keyed" flag was never reset, so the dial going later (on iOS any text field taking the keys, on Android the app leaving the screen) could end a transmission it did not start (the screen's PTT or Space), if it had once keyed; (b) Android: the switch told Java the dial was on even if the native ulanziKey had not registered, and the first dial key would then have thrown UnsatisfiedLinkError and closed the app. *Fixed in the dev build*: the flag is set only when the dial's function really keyed (m_midiKeyed set by the action, i.e. Transmit from the controller allowed it) and cleared when the radio stops transmitting with nothing of the dial's held; Java is told only once the native is registered (otherwise a log line, the dial unread). Tested on the FLEX (USB 5 W, no audio): the dial's PTT on and off; the screen's PTT on, the dial gone: still transmitting, then off by PTT; the dial's PTT held, the dial gone: ended; Transmit from the controller off, a refused dial press, the screen's PTT, the dial gone: still transmitting. Put back: DIGU, 100 W, the switches off. (b) only by reading (Android). Christoph (mail 2026-11-21, build 197): the side keys work on the iPad ("buttons work on iPad - great!"), but "on the Mac the D100 does not work anymore with Ulanzi studio in the background (or not)". Nothing in builds 182-197 changed the Mac's key path (GameController is not attached on a Mac; the arrows and F-keys go through Qt as in 182); first to rule out that the dial is still connected to the iPad over Bluetooth, then his Mac log after the knob and F1 (the keyboard diagnostics say whether keys arrive and where); asked in a reply sent 2026-11-21. Resolved at Christoph's (mail 2026-11-21 18:25): "Ulanzi now works - not sure what happened, but VFO / DVK F... / ESC / PTT works!" on the Mac; nothing changed in the app (the dial was likely still connected to the iPad). Then (mail 2026-11-21 18:37): "the Ulanzi is not stable, sometimes it works sometimes not ... will test further": the Mac's lost key routing (the app's window not the key window, no first responder: Pentti OH6PP's MacBook, build 146) is a candidate; his log right after a failure would show it (keyboard: key routing lines). Christoph (mail 2026-11-21 19:09, after reboots): "sometimes D100 works, sometimes not ... The behaviour is as you described with the beeps. Interesting is, that space on the keyboard also do not work when the D100 does not work": the Mac's key routing is lost (no first responder or the window not the key window, as on Pentti OH6PP's MacBook in build 146), so every key goes nowhere, the dial's and the keyboard's alike. The app repairs it only when a text field takes focus (MobileEngine.cpp: macKeyRoutingLost() → requestActivate()), not for the page's own keys. Fix to pre-analyse: the same check and window activation when the app comes to the front and on any press on the page, each logged. His log did not arrive (asked to resend; a text field clicked as a workaround and a test of the cause).
84. **Fixes:** SmartLink after a network change while the app runs (*in build 179 / beta 94*, (g) *in build 180 / beta 95*, (h) (i) *in build 181 / beta 96*, (j) *in build 182*, iOS only; (k) the background time's log line *in the dev build*: "held ... 2147483647 s" (Mohammed A71AT's log 2026-11-17, the developer's iPad) is iOS's DBL_MAX while it keeps the app running anyway, now "no limit from iOS now") (the developer, 2026-11-16, build 178 on the iPad, the FLEX at the cabin over SmartLink: "jos verkkoa vaihtaa vauhdista eli mobiilista wifiin tai wifistä mobiiliin niin softa ei toivu ennen kuin se varmasti on sammutettu kokonaan taustalta", the Connect page held at *Connecting…* with *SmartLink server not reachable. Retrying…*, no button working). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-16* (the devlink log, 18:15-18:20): on Wi-Fi the SmartLink connect took 3 s; at the change to the phone's hotspot the remote session dropped and the retry sent its connect request over the SmartLink server connection still on the network that went (the client's keepalive is never answered, so a dead socket counts as connected until TCP gives up, here 75 s), no connect_ready came, nothing timed it out; after the server connection was noticed dead and signed in again nothing asked for the radio again; meanwhile the page showed idle with the retry pending, so a tap started a second request beside it (two requests 0.4 s apart), and while *Connecting…* every control was off with no way out but killing the app; the radio listed the dropped session as *In use by OksaSDR* until it timed out (two slots, so no question). A fresh start on the new network connected at once. Fix: (a) a network change (online again after the network went, or another medium) makes the SmartLink server connection again at once (SmartLinkClient::restart: the socket aborted, a refresh still waiting dropped, signed in again from the refresh token); (b) a connect request with no connect_ready in 10 s does the same and asks again, as soon as the new connection lists the radios; (c) a dropped remote session being retried shows *Connecting…*, not idle, and one request at a time; (d) **Cancel** beside *Connecting…* (Connect page) stops a connect or a retry; (e) the log names the TLS / UDP ports, UPnP and the hole punch port of each connect request (never the address), and whether a remote connection ended before or after the radio answered (for Mohammed's case below). No terms. Tested on this PC against the FLEX over SmartLink, the server reached through a local relay that can leave its connection dead (OKSASDR_SMARTLINK_RELAY, development builds only; the certificate still checked against SmartLink's name) and the render tool's new netchange and smartlink steps: T1 the server connection dead before a connect: the 10 s watchdog, a new connection, connected 14.5 s after the tap (stuck before); T2 dead under a session, then a network change: a new server connection, the session unaffected; T3 Cancel while a connect waited: idle at once, nothing fired after; T4 the session ended by the radio (client disconnect of this app's handle from a LAN API connection): *Connecting…*, back by itself in 8 s. The Connect page with *Connecting…* and Cancel rendered at 390x844, 360x800, 1366x1024 and 820x1180. To verify on the iPad: Wi-Fi to a hotspot and back with the app running, both ways. Size: small-medium. (f) A remote connection the radio closes before it answers is tried once more with "wan validate" 1 s after the TLS handshake (Mohammed A71AT: his radio closed every iPad connection over 5G 30-40 ms after the TLS handshake, before its version line; Android and SmartSDR connect on the same network); tested by refusing the first connection on purpose (OKSASDR_WAN_REFUSE_FIRST, development builds): connected "on the second try". Found on the way: a FLEX given a handle it does not know does not close at once, it answers after the same ~1.9 s as always (counted from the TLS connection, not from "wan validate") and closes at the first commands; so Mohammed's 40 ms close is not a plain unknown handle on a FLEX, and the TLS session line (1.3 here, OpenSSL) is there to compare with the iPad's. (g) Build 179 on the iPad (the user: "Ei toimi vieläkään lennosta"): the server connection was made again at once, but the Auth0 refresh reused a kept-alive HTTPS connection from the old network and waited out its 15 s timeout, twice; now the connection cache is cleared on restart and on every transient sign-in failure (see build 180). (h) Build 180 on the iPad (2026-11-16 20:31-20:37, devlink, four switches between Wi-Fi and the hotspot): every one came back by itself (12, 12, 31 and 39 s), the sign-in in 1-2 s each time; the slow ones: once SmartLink answered the connect request only after 10 s and the radio closed the first new session after 1.2 s (the old one still being cleared; the second try held), once the new server connection itself took 37 s (its first packets lost while the network came up, the system retrying after 15 and 31 s). Now a server connection not up (TLS) in 8 s is made again, up to three tries (SmartLinkClient's connect watchdog; the app sees no disconnect meanwhile). Tested (T7, OKSASDR_SMARTLINK_FIRST: the first connection to an address that never answers): a new connection at 8 s, connected 3 s later; T6 and T1-T4 again. *In build 181 / beta 96.* (i) Rich Yost N2RY's second log (2026-11-16, build 180, iPhone, "Came back into house from 5G to WiFi app just froze to 17m screen"): at the change to Wi-Fi the server connection was made again, but the session stayed "connected": iOS kept the cellular TCP connection while the radio's spectrum and audio (UDP) went to the old address, so nothing dropped and the display froze. Now a network change under a live SmartLink session makes the session again over the new network (the radio told, then the usual reconnect; T8: back in 7.7 s on the same slice). The test run after it found a stall of its own: a connect cancelled half way left the WAN socket closing, and the next connect_ready was refused ("already connected or still closing") with the app at Connecting for good; now a cancelled, unvalidated connection is aborted and a new connect_ready drops any socket left over. And for Mohammed A71AT's case the log names the way of the server and the radio connections (IPv4 / IPv6, no address); his build 180 log (22:20) showed the radio closing the TLS connection 38 ms after the handshake even with "wan validate" held back 1 s, so it is not the validate; a FLEX probed from this PC closes an unannounced TLS connection before its handshake, so his radio accepted the connection as announced and then refused it. T1-T8 again. (j) *Pre-analysis 2026-11-16*, the user's iPad on build 181 (22:26-22:36, Wi-Fi dropped and back five times): with the app in front SmartLink came back in 7-14 s every time; with the app in the background it came back only when the app was brought to the front (the user: "yhteys palautuu vasta kun järjestelmä tuodaan etualalle"). The log: the network change at 22:33:33 was handled in the background (new server connection 22:33:36, connect request 22:33:40), then nothing until the app came to the front at 22:34:33, when the connect_ready arrived 53 s late, the radio closed that stale connection and the second try connected at 22:34:39. Cause: the app stays running in the background only through UIBackgroundModes audio, i.e. while it plays receive audio; when the session drops the audio stops and iOS suspends the app within seconds, halfway through the reconnect. Fix: UIApplication beginBackgroundTaskWithName:expirationHandler: when a session drops (or the network changes) while the app is in the background, ended when the session is up again (its receive audio then keeps the app running, as it did before the drop) or when the reconnect gives up, or at expiry (iOS gives about 30 s; the reconnects took 7-14 s). The other way, playing silence to keep the audio mode alive, is against App Review 2.5.4 and is not used. Test: only on a device (the iPad, Wi-Fi off and on in Control Centre with the app in the background, the radio's audio back without opening the app). Size: small.
85. **Radio:** Keys for TUNE, the ATU and a two-tone tune (*in build 181 / beta 96*) (Christoph Steinert DM5HF, mail 2026-11-16 evening: his Ulanzi D100H on the Mac through Ulanzi Studio, which turns each knob and key into a key: the knob already tunes by the arrows, F1-F3 play the voice keyer; his keys labelled ATU and "2 tone tune" had nothing to press in the app; the user: "Näppäimet TUNElle, ATU:lle ja kaksisävytestille"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-16*: the operating page's keys (OperateKeys.qml: arrows, + / -, F, F1-F12, Ctrl / Cmd digits, Esc, Space) and the engine's toggleTune and atuPressed (both held by the transmit lock); the desktop has no default keys for them (its T is MOX; atu_start, tune_toggle, two_tone_tune unbound); the radio's tune mode (single_tone / two_tone) is Radio Setup's; a PTT for a key that sends only a tap already exists (TX tab › PTT › Tap on / off, Space then toggles). Chosen: T TUNE on / off, Shift+T a two-tone TUNE (the radio's own tune mode put back afterwards), A an ATU tune cycle (always a tune, unlike the button, which bypasses an ATU tuned on this frequency), none repeating when held, only while the page has the keyboard (not in a text field), the transmit lock holding them. Tested on the FLEX on the air (tune power 5 W, 14.154 USB, a few seconds each, through the render tool's keys step, now also <Shift-X>): T keyed and unkeyed; the first two-tone version put the mode back as the tune began (the radio's answer to the mode change still says tune=0) and a FLEX ignores a mode change while it tunes, so it stayed two_tone; now the mode goes back once the radio's interlock leaves TRANSMITTING (or 1.5 s after a stop before it ever transmitted): two_tone while tuning, single_tone after, stopped by Shift+T or T; the first A press bypassed the tuned ATU (the button's rule), now A always tunes ("atu start", TUNE_SUCCESSFUL). The radio put back: single_tone, tune power 10 W, the ATU tuned on 14.154. Manual: the keys table and "A Ulanzi dial on a Mac". No terms. Size: small.
86. **Logging:** The DX cluster after a network change (*in build 181 / beta 96*; follow-up (a) *in build 182 / beta 97*) (the developer's iPad, build 180, 2026-11-16: after each switch between Wi-Fi and the hotspot the cluster closed the new login at once, "closed 2 times right after login", and the app said "is OH6OKSA logged in from another device?"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-16*: DxClusterClient already spaces its retries after short sessions (5, 10, 20, 40, 60 s; the iPad's closes at 42, 49, 61 and 83 s followed it), so no change there; what was wrong: the message (another device, when it was this device's own login from the network before, which the cluster keeps until it notices the old connection is gone) and a log that never said what the cluster said. Now: each close soon after a login logs the cluster's last line ("closed 1 s after login; its last line: ..."), and within 5 minutes of a network change two such closes say "The cluster still has this device's login from the network before, and lets the new one on once it notices that one is gone (usually within minutes)." Tested with a stand-in cluster on this PC that answers each login with "Sorry OH6OKSA is already connected" and closes (the render tool's new dxconnect / dxstate / dxoff steps; settings untouched): the old message before a network change, the new one after, the cluster's line in the log. Manual: the Spots section. No terms. Size: small. *Follow-up 2026-11-16* (the user's iPad, build 181): dxc.nc7j.com refused the new login after each network change ("Another session for OH6OKSA is already connected. Existing session preserved.") for more than 5 minutes, once a minute; (a) the network-change message is shown only 5 minutes after the change, then the same refusal shows the old "another device" message: keep the network message while the refusals go on in a row after a network change; (b) idea, to analyse: after such a refusal log in with an SSID (OH6OKSA-1, as DXSpider allows) and say so, or the operator's choice of SSID in the cluster settings.
87. **Fixes:** The slice back where it was after a reconnect (*in build 181 / beta 96*) (Rich Yost N2RY, mail with his log 2026-11-16, build 180, iPhone17,4, FLEX-6500 over SmartLink: "Was on 18.130ish USB, walked outside and switched from WiFi to 5gUW, app connection dropped for like 5 secs then came back to CW to 14.111"; the user: "Tee niin"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-16* (his log): the network change handled as meant (cellular at 14:08:57, signed in again, the radio back at 14:09:04.9); the radio (one station) still held the app's session from Wi-Fi with its slice on 18.130 USB, refused a slice on the pan it restored (5000002a), so the app's slice recovery removed the pan, made a new one, evicted the stale session and made a slice: the radio had put one on the new pan itself, so he had two (his log: "slices 2"), the app's at the pan's default (14.111 CW). Now the app keeps its slice's frequency, mode and filter as they change, takes them along when a session ends, and in a slice recovery (on the same radio, within 10 minutes) waits 1.2 s for the radio's own slice on the new pan and puts that one back where the slice was, or makes one there itself when the radio put none; a slice the radio keeps through a reconnect (a two-station FLEX-6600M after the radio dropped the session, T4) is left as it is. Tested on the FLEX with the render tool's new slicerecovery step (the radio told to drop the slices, the pan made again): the FLEX put a slice on the new pan itself, the app put it back on 14.160 LSB with its filter (-2800..-100) and made no second one; the first versions of the test showed both the duplicate and the step needing the radio's own "slice remove" (removeSlice keeps the last slice). The radio put back: one slice A on 14.154 USB, 100..2800 Hz, TX, active. No terms. Size: small.
88. **Fixes:** Radio Setup scrolled into empty space under every section (*in build 182*) (the user, 2026-11-16, build 180 on the iPad: "Radion setup välilehdet muuten rullaavat liian pitkälle eli tulee vain tyhjää ... sama tilanne joka setup valikon osalta"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-16*: RadioSetupPage's scroll took its height from the StackLayout of the ten sections, whose implicit height is its tallest section's (Amp or Transmit), so every shorter section scrolled on into empty space; the Controls tabs have a scroll each and are not affected. Fix: the scroll's height from the shown section only. Rendered every section scrolled to its end at 1366x1024 and 390x844 against the FLEX: each ends at its last row, the short ones (XVTR, Amp, USB) do not scroll at all. No terms. Size: small. *In build 182 / beta 97.*
89. **Logging:** Each logged QSO to a logger on the network in WSJT-X's UDP format, for RUMlogNG and the others (*in build 182 / beta 97*: Log tab › Logging program (WSJT-X UDP); tested with a reader of WSJT-X's messages on this PC: Heartbeat, QSO Logged and Logged ADIF decoded, every field as logged; RUMlogNG itself by Christoph) (Christoph Steinert DM5HF, mail 2026-11-16: RUMlog imports the shared ADIF from iCloud, but "the logger udp to Rumlog, the QSOs will not logged into Rumlog ... maybe a Rumlog exchange is more useful for Mac, because Logger32 is a Win app"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-16* (the user: "Tarkista rumlog vaatimukset"): RUMlogNG (Mac, Thomas Lindner DL2RUM, 6.5.1 of 2026-09) takes QSOs from other programs only as WSJT-X / JTDX / JS8Call send them: its manual (dl2rum.de/RUMlogNG/docs/en/pages/WSJT.html) has Preferences › UDP › WSJT-X with a *Data port* that must match the sender's UDP server port (2237 by default; unicast to the Mac, a broadcast or a multicast address), *Save QSOs to logbook* (each QSO logged in WSJT-X written to the logbook with no further step; "only the start time is not taken from the Log QSO dialog"), *Overwrite user fields* (my_gridsquare, time_on, operator, station_callsign) and *Call check* (the active call, mode, report, grid and frequency into its entry fields from WSJT-X's status). Its N1MM / "Contact N1MM & ADIF" UDP settings send RUMlog's own QSOs out (to qsomap.org, other RUMlogs); that RUMlog logs N1MM contacts received is only a forum report (SmartSDR for Mac's N1MM option "works better with RumLogNG", AA5SH, community.flexradio.com), not documented. The app's Logger32 output is raw ADIF over TCP 52001, which RUMlog does not listen for: hence nothing arrived. Plan: a "Logger on the network" output beside Logger32: host[:port] (2237 by default), each logged QSO (Log tab, Big buttons, FTx) sent as WSJT-X sends it: Heartbeat (type 0), QSO Logged (type 5) and Logged ADIF (type 12, QsoLog::adifRecord), magic 0xadbccbda, schema 2, QDataStream as src/core/WsjtxClient reads it; unicast only (multicast needs Apple's multicast entitlement; the Local Network permission is in Info.plist already); the same queue as Logger32 is not possible over UDP (no answer), so sent once, and the status line says so. One output serves RUMlogNG, MacLoggerDX, Log4OM, HRD, N1MM+ (its WSJT-X port), GridTracker, CQRLog and Wavelog Gate. Not sent: WSJT-X's Status (Call check) in the first step, because RUMlog's *Switch PTT* keys its radio from Status (the manual says to leave Switch PTT off). Known trap: a forum user had two QSOs in RUMlog for each, where both WSJT-X and GridTracker forwarded; the app sends each QSO once. Tests here: the packets decoded by a Python reader of the protocol and a Linux logger that takes WSJT-X UDP (CQRLog or KLog), then Christoph's RUMlogNG on his Mac as a tester preview (ask his RUMlog version and UDP tab). No terms (the protocol is documented in WSJT-X's NetworkMessage.hpp, GPL as this app). Size: small to medium. *Not working at Christoph's (mail 2026-11-21, build 197 on the Mac and the iPad)*: "RumLog does not receive any logged QSOs ... tried the IP from Mac / iPad as well the 127... with the correct UDP ports". Asked (reply sent 2026-11-21): the status line under the field after a QSO (sent or not), RUMlog's Preferences › UDP › WSJT-X page and version, a log. To check here meanwhile: the packets with WSJT-X's own reader (UDPExamples' udp_daemon / message_aggregator, the authoritative NetworkMessage code), and whether RUMlog listens on a multicast address only (a unicast send would not reach it) or wants WSJT-X's Status before a QSO. Christoph's screenshot (mail 2026-11-21 18:25, his Mac): the app's side works, its line reads "DM5HF sent to 127.0.0.1:2237 at 16:16 UTC"; RUMlog's UDP tab has WSJT-X *Datenport* 2237 with *QSOs speichern* on and *Multicast* empty, but port 2237 is also its "QSOs empfangen von Flex / ADIF" and "QSOs empfangen von N1MM" port (and its N1MM outputs go to 127.0.0.1:2237). Several sockets sharing one UDP port get each unicast datagram on one of them only (macOS / BSD SO_REUSEPORT), so the WSJT-X listener likely never sees the app's QSOs. Suggested (reply drafted 2026-11-21): WSJT-X Datenport 2238 and the app at 127.0.0.1:2238. Noted for later: RUMlog also takes plain ADIF over UDP ("Flex / ADIF"), a second way the app could feed it. **Confirmed working** (Christoph, mail 2026-11-21 18:57: "tried only the 2238 & works", with a screenshot): with RUMlog's WSJT-X *Datenport* 2238 and the app at 127.0.0.1:2238 the QSOs arrive in RUMlog; the app was right from the start, RUMlog's shared 2237 was the cause. The manual's RUMlogNG lines now say to give WSJT-X a port of its own (goes with the next build).
90. **Radio:** The F-keys and Esc on a Mac, and from the Log tab's fields (*in build 182 / beta 97*: tested with the render tool on the FLEX: F1, a Mac's F2 / F4 / F5 (AppKit's character, new keys token <Mac-F1>..<Mac-F12>), ⌘3, ⌘0 and Esc from the Log tab's call field in the phone's drawer and the tablet's pinned panel and on the page, nothing typed into the field, letters still typed; the focus sweep clean on every tab; Christoph on his Mac to confirm) (Christoph Steinert DM5HF, mail 2026-11-16 20:53, his Ulanzi D100H through Ulanzi Studio on the Mac: "F keys and Escape - no chance, doesn't work", and "if I'm in a log field, the key will be shown - logically, but in a pile up a bit too busy"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-16* (the user: the item quoted, i.e. do it): (a) on a Mac an iPad app gets keys Qt's UIKit mapping does not know as AppKit's function-key characters (0xF700...); AppleControlKeys (MobileEngine.cpp) maps the arrows, Home, End, Delete and Page keys to Qt's keys since build 171, but not F1-F12 (0xF704-0xF70F): an F-key reached the page as an unknown key (nothing happened) and a text field typed it as a private-use character (the key "shown" in the field). (b) the CW tab's CWX field and the Digi tab's Text to send already take F1-F12, ⌘ / Ctrl 1-0 and Esc while typing; the Log tab's 8 fields do not, and on the Mac a field keeps the keyboard. Fix: the same in every Log tab field (the call, reports, comment, name, the parks, the spot comment), routed as the page routes them (CW memories in CW, the Digi macros in RTTY / PSK, the voice keyer in voice modes, Esc stops them all), through one engine call the page's keys use too; letters, digits, Space and the arrows still type there (T, A and Space PTT stay the page's). Tests: the render tool typing F1, ⌘1 and Esc into the Log tab's call field and the page, and AppKit's F1 character (a new keys token) as a Mac sends it, in phone (drawer) and tablet (pinned panel) layouts; then Christoph on the Mac. A detached slice outside the band the panadapter's receiver is set for gives noise or a copy of another slice's audio: four slices on 20 m 500 Hz apart decoded each on its own offset (correct), but with D moved to 28.074 D's DAX carried C's audio exactly (same messages at C's offsets), and with the panadapter on 10 m slice A on 14.074 gave flat noise; the DAX channels and streams themselves were right every time (the radio's stream statuses, the app's stream-to-channel table, and four slices on one band). Slices on other bands work when the panadapter covers them (14, 18 and 21 MHz together did). To do: (a) a slice tuned to another band than its panadapter's others gets a panadapter of its own (as SmartSDR does), within the limits the radio reports itself, never a table of models: its free slices and panadapters (the status "radio slices=N panadapters=N") and its SCUs ("num_scu" in its info: 2 on the FLEX-6600M here; a 6700 has eight slices on two SCUs, a 6600 four on two, a 6400 two on one; the user, 2026-11-17: "6700 on 8 vastaanotinta"), else the app says why the slice cannot be heard there; (b) the FTx / Digi tabs show a decoding slice that is detached or outside its panadapter's band ("Slice A is outside its panadapter's band: the radio does not receive it there"), and the log says so once; (c) the decoder tag in the log stays (ft8: A ch3 slot ...). The user, 2026-11-17: "+pan pitäisi lisätä aina uusi ja +slice lisää vain olemassa olevaan pan adapteriin uuden vastaanottimen ... digimodella pitäisi aina luoda oma panadapteri per bandi", and chose "Oma paneeli itsestään". *Done in the dev build*: a slice going to another band than the other slices on its panadapter (the Band buttons, a frequency typed or from a spot, a memory, the FTx band buttons) first gets a panadapter of its own: the slice removed, a panadapter made (the radio's own slice on it, or one made there), the new slice given its mode and transmit, then the action again on it; the radio gives the freed letter, so the slice keeps its letter; never while transmitting; no panadapter free: said why and tuned as before; none in 8 s: the slice put back. A slice left on another band (a drag or the knob over a band edge, another program) has a warning-colour outline on its chip and, while FTx decodes, a line on the FTx tab; the radio's detached state is read (SliceModel::isDetached) and logged. 97. **Distribution:** Asking money in the App Store and Google Play, or a voluntary tip in the app (the user, 2026-11-17: "tällä ohjelmistolla mahdollista pyytää rahaa play kaupassa ja apple storessa", then "Kirjaa"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-17* (not legal advice): (a) The licence: OksaSDR is GPL-3 like AetherSDR, with GPL parts inside (WDSP, FFTW, FlexRadio's smartsdr-dsp among them). The GPL allows a price, but the buyer gets the source (the repository is public), anyone may build and give the app away (our own APK on GitHub already is free), and no further restrictions may be added: a price buys convenience and support, not exclusivity. (b) Apple: the FSF holds the App Store's terms (DRM, device limits) to be further restrictions the GPL forbids; this already applies to the TestFlight distribution, priced or not. Any copyright holder (AetherSDR's contributors, the other GPL parts' authors) could ask Apple to remove the app, as one did with VLC in 2011; a price adds visibility and a motive. The safe way: the upstream maintainers' consent (an App Store exception) before any paid listing (we no longer send upstream PRs, see upstream-not-pursued, but asking is separate). (c) Google Play: its terms do not clash with the GPL; a paid app there is the simpler case. (d) The stores' payment rules: digital features and tips only through the store's own billing (Apple In-App Purchase, Google Play Billing), 15 % commission for small developers; no PayPal donation link inside the iOS app (the Android rule on external donation links to be checked). The stores handle VAT as the seller; the income is the user's taxable income (as a person or through his company); Apple needs the Paid Applications agreement, bank and tax details in App Store Connect (the user's, never entered by me). (e) Store releases first: TestFlight purchases are sandbox only (no real money) and the APK is on GitHub, not Play: real payments need a public App Store release (full App Review, which needs a demo mode for a reviewer without a radio) and a Google Play listing. (f) FlexRadio: the app signs in to SmartLink with FlexRadio's sign-in; commercial use is worth asking FlexRadio first. Some features (Auto AGC-T, our own voice keyer) are SmartSDR+ licence features on the radio, so selling them in an app could strain the good relations (Mike VA3MW welcomed product posts in FlexRadio Enthusiasts). *Asked 2026-11-17* (the user: "Kysytään GPL päätös upstreamistä", then "Tee 1"): a comment on upstream discussion #6409, Jeremy KK7GWY (ten9876) mentioned, whether the project objects to OksaSDR (GPLv3, source public, free) in the App Store (https://github.com/aethersdr/AetherSDR/discussions/6409#discussioncomment-19690807); no answer yet. Recommendation: keep the app free and add a voluntary tip through the stores' own billing (an iOS tip jar with StoreKit, consumable IAPs of a few sizes; on Android Play Billing once the app is on Play), which changes nothing in the licence and limits no one; for a real price, ask the upstream maintainers and FlexRadio first. Size: the tip jar small-medium per platform (StoreKit 2 in an .mm bridge, non-ARC; Play Billing in OksaActivity), the store releases themselves larger (App Review, Play listing, privacy forms). Not chosen for a build.
