Size: Apple small-medium (texts, shots, forms), Google larger and at least three weeks (the closed test). *Started 2026-11-17 (Apple)*: the listing texts and the App Privacy / age rating / DSA answers in mobile/appstore/ (README.md, en-US/*.txt); the purpose strings name OksaSDR (three said AetherSDR); PRIVACY.md covers the App Store, points the logbook settings to Radio Setup › Logging (95) and lists the logging program (WSJT-X UDP, 89). The screenshots: mobile/tools/appstore-screenshots.sh (5 iPhone, 4 iPad, rendered on the FLEX; made again with --build N at submission). In App Store Connect (2026-11-17, mobile/tools/appstore_sync.py): version 1.0's texts, the subtitle and privacy URL, App Review's notes and the demo video v5 (720p) as an App Review attachment, Apple only (the user's choice over a GitHub release); the store name OksaSDR (was "OksaSDR Mobile"; the user: "OksaSDR"). Open: upstream's answer to the GPL question (97 b), the user's forms (App Review contact, App Privacy, age rating, DSA, price), the version string and the build (the next one, with the purpose strings), the screenshots uploaded, then the submission.
99. **Fixes:** SmartLink on an iPad or iPhone over a mobile network that closes the radio's connection: the radio connection over TLS 1.3 with Apple's own TLS (*in build 188*, a crash fix in 189) (Mohammed Alkhayarin A71AT, mail with his log 2026-11-18 00:43, build 184, iPad, iOS 27.0.1, the AU-510, Qatar: "the same problem with SmartLink connection to 5G show him back again"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-18*: his log, two runs: the first on his home network (a local session, FT8 on 40 m), then after a disconnect a SmartLink connect, and the second a fresh start on Cellular only; in both every attempt the same as on 2026-11-16: connect_ready, the TLS session up ("TLS 1.2 over IPv4"), the far end closing 30-40 ms later before the radio's version line, the second try with wan validate held back 1 s the same (6 attempts). The iOS build's TLS is Qt's Secure Transport backend (no OpenSSL on iOS), which goes no higher than TLS 1.2; Android (OpenSSL 3) and Linux use TLS 1.3. In TLS 1.2 the server's certificate crosses the network in the clear, in TLS 1.3 it is encrypted. What works for him: OksaSDR on Android over 5G, SmartSDR for iOS on the same iPad, 5G and account (16.11), our iPad app on Wi-Fi; what fails: only our iPad app over 5G, sometimes after it had worked that morning. So the likely cause is a filter on the mobile network (operators in Qatar filter VoIP-like traffic) that classifies the TLS 1.2 connection by what it shows (the radio's self-signed certificate, or Secure Transport's ClientHello) and cuts it; not proven: a test on his side would be the iPad on his Android phone's hotspot with both apps (if the Android app connects and the iPad does not over the same link, it is the client). Found on the way: on iOS Qt's Secure Transport gives no peer certificate with VerifyNone, so the TOFU pin of the radio's certificate ("peer presented no certificate; skipping TOFU check", our own iPad too) never runs on iOS. Fix: on iOS (and the iPad app on a Mac) the radio's TLS connection through Apple's Network framework (nw_connection with TLS 1.2-1.3, the verify block taking the radio's self-signed certificate and doing the same TOFU fingerprint check), behind WanConnection's present interface (connect, write, readyRead, the session line), Qt's QSslSocket kept on the other platforms; the SmartLink server's own connection unchanged (it works). No terms (Apple's framework). Tests: only on a device (the CI builds iOS; .mm non-ARC): the user's iPad over SmartLink to the FLEX (the session line "TLS 1.3", the certificate pinned, a reconnect, the network changes of 84), then Mohammed over 5G. Size: medium. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-18, chosen for build 187)*: src/core/backends/flex/AppleTlsSocket.h/.mm (iOS only, not ARC): nw_connection with nw_parameters_create_secure_tcp, TLS 1.2 to 1.3 (sec_protocol_options), a verify block that accepts the radio's self-signed certificate and keeps its DER (SecTrustCopyCertificateChain's leaf); encrypted() at the ready state with the negotiated version and cipher (sec_protocol_metadata), the peer address and local port from the path; reads by nw_connection_receive, the end of the stream as RemoteHostClosedError, failed and waiting states as errors (as QSslSocket fails at once), disconnectFromHost by the final message then cancel (2 s at most), abort at once; every event handed to the socket's thread for its own connection only (a connection id, the owner cleared under a lock before it goes). WanConnection uses it behind #if defined(Q_OS_IOS) (QSslSocket elsewhere, unchanged) and pins the SHA-256 of the DER as QSslCertificate::digest does, so the TOFU check now runs on iOS; the core links the Network and Security frameworks on iOS. Checked here: the Linux build (the QSslSocket path) and the iOS path of WanConnection.cpp and both mocs compiled with Q_OS_IOS on Linux; the .mm only compiles on the CI. To test on a device: SmartLink on the iPad (the log's "SmartLink: the radio's TLS session up, TLS 1.3 …", "pinned cert fingerprint … first-use TOFU" once, then "matches"), a disconnect and reconnect, a network change (84), then Mohammed over 5G. *Found while testing (2026-11-18)*: WanConnection called its TLS-ready step on QSslSocket's connected(), the TCP connect, before the handshake: so on every platform the session line showed no cipher and a protocol read too early ("TLS 1.3  over IPv4" on Linux), and the peer had no certificate yet, so the TOFU pin was always skipped, not only on iOS. Now on encrypted() (the iOS socket's encrypted() is the same point): on Linux over real SmartLink to the FLEX the session is "TLS 1.2 ECDHE-RSA-CHACHA20-POLY1305 over IPv4" (the radio negotiates TLS 1.2 with OpenSSL 3 too, so the "TLS 1.3" of the Android and Linux logs was that early reading), the first connect pinned the certificate and the second "cert fingerprint matches stored pin". So the iOS change does not bring TLS 1.3 to this radio: what it changes is Apple's current TLS stack and its ClientHello instead of Secure Transport's, which is what still tells the iPad app from SmartSDR for iOS and the Android app on Mohammed's 5G; whether it is enough, only his test can show (his hotspot test, an Android phone sharing 5G to the iPad, would tell client from network meanwhile). *Found after release (2026-11-19)*: build 188 crashed when a SmartLink connection failed before it was up (Barry Cohen N2BJ: his log, two connect_ready, each followed 150 ms later by "OSStatus error -9816 - server closed session with no notification", then EXC_BAD_ACCESS in nw_connection_cancel_inner on the framework's queue). Cause: AppleTlsSocket held the connection by one reference, which its state handler released at the cancelled state; when the framework itself cancelled the connection (a failed handshake, the radio closing, a waiting state), m_conn still pointed at it and finishConnection() then cancelled a freed connection. The same race was open on a normal disconnect and on a write after a failure. Fix: two references, each released by its holder, as Apple's nwcat sample: this object's (from nw_connection_create, released in finishConnection() after the cancel) and the state handler's (an nw_retain, released at the cancelled state); no other code uses the Network framework. Why the radio closed: the user's FLEX does the same to every TLS client that arrives without a SmartLink connect pending (openssl s_client from this PC on the LAN: its default ClientHello, TLS 1.2 only, TLS 1.3 only and X25519MLKEM768 all end in "unexpected eof", no alert), so the radio decides by whether it expects the connection, not by the TLS stack: Barry's radio did not take his connection as the one SmartLink announced. A likely cause, to ask him: his two radios on the account (a FLEX-6600M and a FLEX-8600M) both on TLS port 4994 behind one router, which forwards the port to only one of them; SmartSDR would fail the same way. A possible follow-up after his answer: a clearer message for a TLS connection the radio closes before it is up. The iPad test of 188 (2026-11-19 07:55, SmartLink to the FLEX): "TLS 1.2 ECDHE-RSA-CHACHA20-POLY1305 over IPv4", no "peer presented no certificate" (the pin lines are debug level, not in the devlink log).
100. **Fixes:** The Network & performance panel hangs the app on iOS (*in build 185*) (Scott Stanford W5SLS's two TestFlight crashes on build 181, "When I touch the top of the screen that shows “xxxMB” it crashes"; the developer's iPad on build 184, 2026-11-18, three times; Android beta 98 the same day: no hang). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-18*: the iPad's reports (.ips, mailed by the user) are watchdog kills (0x8BADF00D, "scene-update watchdog transgression ... 10.00 seconds"), the main thread in Qt Quick's polish from the display link: QQuickWindow polishAndSync → QQuickLayout::updatePolish → ensureLayoutItemsUpdated → a layout's own size changed → QQuickLayout::geometryChange → QQuickGridLayoutBase::rearrange → a child layout's geometryChange → rearrange, again and again, with text shaped anew inside it (HarfBuzz over CoreText); the cpu_resource report: 90 s of CPU in 105 s, every sample there. So a layout and a text in it set each other's size without end (Qt 6's polishItems allows INT_MAX rounds, so it never gives up); the functions named from the strings each references in the stripped binary (build 184's symbols artifact has no dSYM). Not reproduced on Linux: the panel opened on the demo radio at 1376x1032, 1032x1376, 1180x820, 440x956, 402x874 and 390x844, fonts default and Liberation Sans, --font-px 13-18, distance-field, native and curve text rendering (144 runs), and on the FLEX at 440x956: the iOS font metrics are what tips it. Fix: the panel's rows without text-driven layout (a fixed row height, the value right-aligned and elided, the note at a fixed width, the per-stream rows updated in place instead of a Repeater rebuilt every second), so no text size can feed back into a layout; and for the next such report the iOS CI keeps the unstripped binary (Qt's own function names) with the dSYM, so a .ips symbolizes. Test: only on a device (the iPad: the badge tapped, the panel open a minute, closed and opened again; an iPhone), as a preview build first. Size: small. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-18)*: NetworkDialog.qml's rows are items of a fixed height (FontMetrics), the value right-aligned and elided over the label, the sections the same, the network badge in an item of a fixed size, the note at a fixed width (the room beside a scroll bar), the four stream rows kept (model 4) with only their values changing; no RowLayout or text-sized item left in the column. Rendered on the demo radio at 440x956, 390x844, 844x390 (scrolls, the bar beside the rows) and 1376x1032: as before. The iOS CI keeps aether-mobile.unstripped (the linked binary of the same UUID before the archive's strip) in testflight-symbols, and that artifact stays 30 days (the others 2). *In build 185 / beta 99 (preview)*; tested on the developer's iPad 2026-11-18 12:13-12:14 (iOS 27.2.1, the FLEX over the VPN): the panel opened from the top row several times, no stall in the devlink log (the user: "Yläpalkki avattu useita kertoja ei ongelmaa"). The CI's unstripped binary was not found where it looked ("unstripped none"): the search path to fix with the next build. *Fixed for build 186*: there is no unstripped copy (Xcode links the app into the archive and strips it there), but the dSYM made before the strip lay beside the build products (build-xcode/mobile/Release-iphoneos), not in the archive, so the export said "Upload Symbols Failed": mobile/tools/ios-symbols.sh puts the dSYM with the shipped binary's UUID into the archive and the artifact.
101. **Fixes:** A slider moved by a finger that only meant to scroll the page (*in build 186*) (Rich Yost N2RY, mail with his log 2026-11-17, build 182, iPhone17,4, FLEX-6500: "suddenly the waterfall partially disappeared"; his FFT frames/s at 5 and Waterfall speed at 1, both their slow ends, kept in the radio; chosen for the next build, the user 2026-11-18: "Liukusäätimet ja vieritys"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-18* (Qt 6.12's QQuickSlider source): a touch press only arms a Slider and a move along it past the drag threshold drags it, but the release puts the value where the finger lifts even with no drag (handleRelease), so a tap on the track (a finger stopping the page, or one that lands on a slider as a scroll begins and lifts before the page takes it) sets the value under it; a mouse press (a Mac) moves it at once (mousePressEvent calls handleMove). A vertical scroll across a horizontal slider is taken by the tab's Flickable before the slider moves, so the tap is the cause. 30 sliders: 12 plain (the View tab's seven, CW sensitivity, the TX processor, the Info panel's pan, the EQ's vertical bands) and 18 FineSlider (power, gains, levels; − / + beside them). Fix: one AppSlider, a Slider with a MouseArea over it that takes the press: the value moves only by a drag along the slider past the drag threshold, from where it was by the finger's travel (as an iOS slider's thumb; a tap on the track moves an iOS slider no more); a tap does nothing; pressed is true only while dragging and moved() comes on each step, so the sends on release (the View tab's FFT and waterfall pacing, FineSlider's levels) and the live sends work as before; the mouse wheel still scrolls the page (no wheel handler). The EQ's vertical bands in a vertically scrolling tab: to see whether the drag or the page wins. Terms: none. Test: the render tool gets a touch drag through the window system (dragname: a finger, as a phone's, or the mouse): a tap on a slider's track leaves its value; a vertical drag across one scrolls the tab and leaves it; a sideways drag moves it by the travel; − / + and a screen reader's steps unchanged; then the user's iPhone and iPad. Size: small. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-18)*: mobile/qml/AppSlider.qml, used by all 12 plain sliders and FineSlider's own (so all 30); a vertical one (the EQ) drags only from its handle (a finger's width round it), a drag elsewhere scrolls the page. The render tool's new dragname (a finger through the window system as a touch screen, or the mouse) on the demo radio at 390x844, against Qt's own Slider (the MouseArea switched off): a tap at the left end of Black level took it from 51 to 5 before, leaves it now; a mouse press on FFT average took it from 0 to 50 before, leaves it now; a finger's vertical drag across FFT frames/s scrolled the tab both before and now with the value kept; a sideways drag of 60 px moves FFT average by 14 from where it was (before it jumped to the finger); the mic level's FineSlider: taps (finger and mouse) and a scroll keep it, − / + step one, drags of ±40 px move it 12 and back; the EQ: a tap at a band's top keeps it, a drag from the handle moves it (60 px: +9), a drag from the track's foot scrolls the Audio tab (76 px) and keeps it. focussweep on every drawer tab at 430x932: nothing keeps the keyboard. To verify on the iPhone and the iPad.
102. **FTx:** Answer CQ's calls per station set by the user (*in build 188*) (Kurt Hanisch DL5EAG, mail 2026-11-18: "Is it possible to make the auto-response to a CQ call configurable? Currently, the calling sequence is fixed at 5 repetitions", and the same evening: "It would be best if it were freely selectable. Sometimes 2 calls are enough, other times 20 might be needed"; the user, 2026-11-18: "102 Kurtin kutsumäärä" for build 187). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-18*: Answer CQ (MobileEngineDigi.cpp) calls a station kAutoAnswerTries = 5 times, then picks another (one called is left alone 30 minutes); the same limit counts each later message of an Answer CQ QSO sent without its reply (a tapped QSO has 6, the CQ watchdog 10, a Hound 4). WSJT-X has no count, only its Tx watchdog in minutes. Fix: the count a setting, 2-20 (Kurt's range), 5 by default, remembered (MobileDigiAnswerCalls), a row under the presence limit in the FTx settings (More): "Answer CQ calls each station N times" with − / +; the manual's two Answer CQ passages. No terms. Test: --ft8-test autoanswer,dry,calls=2 on the FLEX (two dry calls a station, then the next), the row rendered on a phone and the iPad. Size: small. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-18)*: MobileEngine::digiAnswerCalls (2-20, 5), the FTx settings row (digiAnswerCalls, − / + held repeat), --ft8-test calls=N (put back at the end). On the FLEX, dry, 20 m FT8, calls=2 for 5 minutes: EC3A, EA3DVP, EH5CCE and EA4EQ each called twice ("try 1", "try 2"), then "No answer from …" and the next; frequency, mode, power, TX slice and the setting (5) restored. Rendered at 440x956, 390x844 and 1180x820 (the iPad panel): the row under "Auto stops after …", − / + 5 → 7 → 5.
103. **Devices:** The KPA1500's fan from the Amp tab: − / + for its minimum speed, with the speed shown (*in build 190 / Android beta 102*) (Rich Yost N2RY, mail 2026-11-19: "possible to add a plus and minus Fan button to control the fan speed with a number indicator? i do a lot of AM, and very conscious of the amp temp"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-19*: the KPA1500 Programming Reference V3 has ^FC, the fan minimum speed (GET ^FC;, SET/RESPONSE ^FCn; n 0 to 5, also ^FC+; ^FC-; and ^FC/; back to the value before the last change; updated in firmware 02.62) and ^FS, the speed the fan runs at now (0 to 5, which can be above the minimum when the amp is hot); ^DW the dwell time (3-255 s). So: ^FS and ^FC in the status poll (the temperature ^TM is there already), on the Amp tab a "Fan" row with − / + (^FC-; / ^FC+;, only with Control on, as the other commands) and the minimum and actual speed shown ("Fan 3 (min 2)"); the speed also offered on the meters as the KPA1500's other readings. The stand-in simulator answers ^FC/^FS too, for the tests; then Rich on his amp. Experimental, as the KPA1500's other control. No terms. Size: small. *Chosen for the next build* (the user, 2026-11-19: "otetaan"). *Done in the dev build (2026-11-19)*: Kpa1500Protocol's Status has fanSpeed (^FS) and fanMinimum (^FC), each one digit 0-5 (anything else left out), buildSetFanMinimum(n) gives ^FCn; (the number itself: every firmware takes it, ^FC+; / ^FC-; only 02.62 and later); Kpa1500Connection::setFanMinimum; the UDP reader asks FS and FC in its slow round (now 11 queries, 5.5 s); MobileEngine::kpaSetFanMinimum, the one KPA1500 command without a question and allowed while the radio transmits (kpaCommandAllowed's whileTransmitting: a fan setting switches no RF and the amplifier still speeds the fans up as the heat sink warms), done when ^FC reads it back, then ^FS read at once. The Amp tab: a Fan reading ("2 (min 1)") under Temperature and with Control on a Fan minimum row, − / + with the read-back value between them (− off at 0, + at 5; usable while transmitting, the tab's other controls not); the notes under the controls say so. The meters: Amp fan (0-5, the six steps as its marks, "Fan 2 (min 1)", named Fan on a narrow face, Amp Fan on a wide one), offered while a KPA1500 is the amplifier read (no RF2K-S set up). The stand-in (kpa1500-mock.py --fan-min N): ^FC / ^FS answered (the speed 2 keyed, 0 in receive, never below the minimum), ^FCn set, ^FC+ / - / / with firmware 02.62 and later. *Found in the tests and fixed*: the command goes over the control link (TCP) and its readback over UDP, so the readback could reach the amplifier first and get the old value; the next one came 5.5 s later in the round, so the tab said "has not shown the change in 3 s" and the next − counted from the stale value (^FC1 sent for 3 → 2). Now a pending command's readback is asked every cycle until it shows (Kpa1500Reader extra queries; a tune keeps its own); this was there for Operate / Standby, ANT1 / ANT2, the ATU mode and the fault clear too (64), only less likely to show. Tested on this PC against the stand-in with the FLEX connected (2026-11-19): kpa1500_protocol_test (FC2, FS4, FS0; FC6, FC+, FC12, an empty FC, FS-1, FS9 refused; ^FC3; parsed; ^FC0; / ^FC5; built, -1 / 6 refused); the user's path at 440x956: Control on in Radio Setup › Amp, − / + from 0 to 2 and back to 0, each step one ^FCn on the TCP link and shown by the readback within a cycle, − at 0 sent nothing; while transmitting (USB, 5 W, no audio, "radio transmitting: yes"): + twice (^FC1, ^FC2), Standby and Bypass off (nothing sent), the state line "Not while transmitting (the fan minimum works)."; the Amp fan meter on the right in transmit; mode DIGU and 100 W put back. Rendered at 440x956, 1366x1024 and 1180x820 (the iPad panel): the rows fit, the meter "Amp Fan 0 (min 0)". Manual: the Amp tab's Fan reading, Fan minimum, the meters' Fan, Radio Setup › Amp's control list; tab-kpa.jpg and kpa-controls.jpg anew (the controls picture scrolled 14 px above the end: ShotPlan's scroll takes -PX). To test on Rich's KPA1500 (firmware, the fans heard). *Tested by Rich Yost N2RY on his KPA1500 (mail with his log 2026-11-19 18:33, build 190, iPhone17,4, iOS 27.0.1)*: "Tested the fan speed up and down, receive and transmit also set the meter to display speed. All appears to work great. Monitored the amp temp thru out test"; his log: 27 fan minimum steps, each shown by the readback (0.02-0.07 s), Tune in STANDBY twice ("The amplifier tuned (SWR 1.00)"). One OPERATE asked 4 s after the second tune (12:02:16) was not read back in 3 s (the first, at 11:19:48, in 0.17 s): to ask Rich whether the amplifier went to OPERATE then. Rich's answer (mail 2026-11-19 21:04): "I did not press anything on the amp itself, The amp tune seemed to work fine I also tuned the flex 6500 once too". So the OPERATE asked 4 s after a tune was not read back in 3 s, while the one asked 10 s after the first tune was at once: perhaps the KPA1500 ignores ^OS1 for a few seconds after a tune (storing the ATU setting); not known, to watch in the next log (no change without it).

104. **Fixes:** A Disconnect over SmartLink, or a switch from SmartLink to the direct connection, logged on iOS as the radio closing the connection (*in build 190*) ("The remote host closed the connection") and kept as the app's last error (builds 188-189, AppleTlsSocket of 99; the developer's iPad 2026-11-19: four such ends in the devlink log, 08:43, 08:52, 16:02 and 16:16, read here first as the radio dropping SmartLink; the user: "ei se ainakaan minulle sitä katkaissut ennenkuin itse katkaisin"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-19*: WanConnection::disconnectFromRadio sends 0x04 and calls disconnectFromHost; AppleTlsSocket goes to ClosingState, sends the final message and the radio closes its side in answer; the receive loop takes that end (is_complete with the final context) for the radio closing and posts handleFailed, which took ClosingState as a live session: errorOccurred(RemoteHostClosedError), so WanConnection logged a socket error, MobileEngine "SmartLink: the remote connection ended after the radio answered" and setLastError("Remote connection: The remote host closed the connection"), shown on the Connect page after the user's own Disconnect. QSslSocket (builds before 188, Android, Linux) reports no error for a close it was asked for. Each end in the log matches the user's own action: at 16:16 no reconnect followed (a Disconnect), at 08:43, 08:52 and 16:02 the direct connection over the VPN came up a second later (the radio's card tapped). Fix: handleFailed in ClosingState ends the session as asked (disconnected() only, a debug line), as QSslSocket does; a session that was up and ends by itself still reports the error. iOS only (the file is not built elsewhere). No terms. Size: small. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-19)*; it builds only in the iOS CI, so to test on the iPad: SmartLink connected, Disconnect (no "remote host closed" in the log, no error on the Connect page), SmartLink then the radio's direct card, and a real drop (the radio's network unplugged, or airplane mode) still reported. *Verified on the developer's iPad, build 190 (2026-11-19 18:42, the phone's hotspot over mobile data)*: Disconnect over SmartLink logged no "remote host closed" and no socket error; connected again at once.

105. **Fixes:** A binding loop in Controls › Prof › App setups: the Export question (*in build 190*) (ProfileControls.qml:229, "QML Dialog: Binding loop detected for property implicitHeight", in Basic's Dialog.qml:16) logged each time the controls drawer was first opened, on every platform (the devlink log: builds 180-189 and Android beta 98; the dev build's runs; the user chose its fix for build 190, 2026-11-19). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-19*: its contentItem was the wrapping Label itself, so the dialog's implicit height and the label's wrapped height were set from each other; the app's other questions (AmpControls' confirm) put the text in a ColumnLayout with Layout.fillWidth and log nothing. Fix: the same layout. No terms. Size: tiny. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-19)*: no binding loop on opening the drawer (the demo radio, 440x956); the question opened from a saved setup at 440x956 and 1180x820 (the text wrapped in the 420-px dialog, OK / Cancel), Delete's question too, the test setup deleted after. The other binding loops in the devlink log (MeterView.qml:128 / 142, BigButtonsView, PanadapterView) are older builds' lines; none in this dev build's runs.

106. **Fixes:** SmartLink to the radio fails on Michael Walker's iPhone on build 189 with "Remote connection: connection failed" (*in builds 190, 192 and 193*: the reason shown, then SmartLink's TLS and UDP through an IPv6-only network's NAT64) (mail 2026-11-19 16:48 with a screenshot: iPhone 15 Pro, iOS 27.2, build 189, his Aurora AU-520; the Connection check: this device's network OK at 192.0.0.2/32, 192.168.1.102/24 and 192.0.0.6/32, SmartLink signed in, the radio listed Available, 1 of 1 station slots free, no radio On this network; SmartSDR for iOS connects; SmartLink worked for him on build 184, voice logging from the car). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-19 (first part)*: "connection failed" is AppleTlsSocket's text for a framework state with no error, which is the waiting state (no usable path, before TCP or TLS): so the system held the connection back (this app's mobile data or Local Network off, a path the framework found unusable) rather than the radio refusing it; 192.0.0.2 / .6 are iOS's 464XLAT addresses of an IPv6-only mobile network, 192.168.1.102 his Wi-Fi. Builds 188-189 made the connection with Apple's Network framework (99), which applies the per-app network settings that BSD sockets (Qt, up to 187) did not meet the same way. *In the dev build (2026-11-19)*: a wait now says what its path is (satisfied / unsatisfied and why: mobile data off for the app, Wi-Fi denied, Local Network off; over Wi-Fi / mobile data / wired / other), in the log and on the Connect page. Asked him (reply drafted 2026-11-19): his log right after a failed try, at home on the radio's Wi-Fi or away, OksaSDR's Local Network switch. Size: to see from his log. Tested on the developer's iPad with build 190 (2026-11-19 18:34-18:42): OksaSDR's Local Network permission was off all along, and SmartLink to the radio's public address connected on the home Wi-Fi, on the phone's hotspot shared from Wi-Fi, and on the hotspot over mobile data (after the network change the app made the connection again by itself, TLS 1.2 over IPv4 in 11 s): so Local Network is not what stops a SmartLink connection (it only hides On this network's radios). Not reproducible here: an iPhone's own IPv6-only mobile network (464XLAT, his 192.0.0.x addresses) with Wi-Fi on at the same time; for that his build 190 log, or an iPhone on its own mobile data. If the log shows the wait on a path that is up or can come up, the fix is to give the framework a few seconds in its waiting state instead of cancelling at once (QSslSocket had no such state). *Cause found (2026-11-19, his logs from builds 189 and 191, mail 19:37 and 19:43; his answers: "cellular and other SmartLink applications work", "OksaSDR has always worked")*: on Wi-Fi at 09:52 SmartLink to the radio worked (TLS 1.2 over IPv4); at 12:37 the iPhone moved to mobile data, the SmartLink server connection went over IPv6, and every connection to the radio's IPv4 address failed in the same millisecond as connect_ready: build 189 "connection failed", build 191 (106's line) "no usable network path", no reason, no interface. So on his IPv6-only mobile network (464XLAT) Network framework has no path for an IPv4 literal, where Qt's BSD sockets (builds up to 187) went through the iPhone's CLAT. Not a setting; likely every IPv6-only mobile network (T-Mobile US and others). *Fixed in the dev build*: for an IPv4 address AppleTlsSocket first asks getaddrinfo (AI_DEFAULT, on the framework's queue, never the GUI thread) how the network reaches it, as Apple's "Supporting IPv6-only Networks" asks for IPv4 literals; an IPv6 answer (only NAT64 gives one for an IPv4 literal) becomes the connection's endpoint (nw_endpoint_create_address), logged "an IPv6-only network: the radio's address through its NAT64"; elsewhere the IPv4 address as before. The UDP streams stay Qt's to SmartLink's IPv4 address (as up to build 187), the certificate pin is the radio's whatever the address. Checked here: the helper compiled and run on this PC (no NAT64: empty, the IPv4 path as before); the async part syntax-checked with clang -fblocks against stubs; the iOS build only in CI; to verify by Michael on mobile data. Replied to him 2026-11-19 (the cause, the fix in the next build, Wi-Fi meanwhile), his threads archived. *Build 192 on his mobile data (log, mail 2026-11-19 21:33: "I have no audio and no pan adaptor or waterfall. The Vita49 data is not passing On this Network")*: the TLS session came up over IPv6 (the NAT64 path worked) and the radio took the connection, but not one UDP packet arrived (display check frames 0, audio underruns with no packet at all, "no audio data received for 15008 ms"). The UDP had stayed on an IPv4 socket to the radio's IPv4 address (through the iPhone's CLAT), so the radio saw the TLS session and the UDP come from two different paths, and likely two different public addresses of the carrier's NAT64 (the CLAT has an IPv6 address of its own); up to 187 both went through the CLAT. The assumption above ("the UDP streams stay Qt's ... as up to build 187") was wrong. *Fixed in the dev build (2026-11-19)*: PanadapterStream::wanUdpTarget: when the TLS session reached the radio's IPv4 address through the NAT64 (its peer a plain IPv6 address, not v4-mapped, loopback or link-local), the WAN UDP goes to that same IPv6 address from an IPv6 socket (startWan binds AnyIPv6 for an IPv6 address); everything else as before (IPv4 bind, the public IPv4); RadioModel logs "WAN UDP through the NAT64 like the TLS session". Checked here: wan_udp_keepalive_socket_test with a real IPv6 loopback (udp_register reaches the IPv6 address, VITA over IPv6 confirms the registration and is dispatched, back to IPv4 rebinds IPv4) and the selection's cases, all PASS; the Linux dev build's SmartLink to the FLEX over IPv4 unchanged (TLS over IPv4, frames 54, no NAT64 line). Android and desktop: their TLS peer is IPv4 (Qt, CLAT on Android), so unchanged. *Tested on an IPv6-only network before the release (the user, 2026-11-19: "Rakenna tämä ennen julkaisua Jotta ollaan varma, että se toimii")*: mobile/tools/ipv6only-testnet.sh (a network namespace with IPv6 only, set up by the user's sudo; no packages), nat64-test.py (a NAT64 on a TUN device, tayga's way, the kernel's NAT44 behind it) and dns64-test.py (AAAA as 64:ff9b::A; --no-a answers A with nothing, as iOS hides IPv4 addresses on an IPv6-only network: Linux Qt's HTTP/2 to Auth0 hung on the unreachable IPv4 addresses otherwise, curl did not), the dev build inside it with OKSASDR_TEST_NAT64=64:ff9b:: (the radio's TLS through the NAT64, as AppleTlsSocket from getaddrinfo; Linux and desktop builds only). SmartLink to the FLEX: signed in, the radio's TLS over IPv6, "WAN UDP through the NAT64 like the TLS session", display check frames 56 / 45, 6233 packets in through the NAT64, no underruns; FT8 on 14.074 DIGU at 10 W: "CQ OH6OKSA" sent, "TX ended, peak 8.4 W" (the TX audio over the IPv6 UDP), FT8 decodes after it; restored Decode off, RF power 100 W, slice A 14.074 DIGU TX, disconnected. (SWR 3.2 in that TX, where earlier FT8 runs showed 1.2-1.4: told the user.) Still to verify by Michael on his carrier (with its CLAT).

107. **Display:** The notch panel beside the notch, not over it (*in build 191 / Android beta 103*; on every screen and in 3D *in build 192 / beta 104*), Larry Averbeck W9FLA (FB comment on the build 189 post, 2026-11-19, Galaxy Tab 11A+ in landscape, Android beta 101: "I have a Solar farm nearby that produces a lot of interference requiring frequent use of the Notch feature. Unfortunately, the menu is frozen dead center on the pan and waterfall and the area that I need to see to easily adjust it. The ability to move it out of the way would be useful"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-19*: PanadapterView.qml's tnfPopup.place() centres the panel on the notch (anchorX) at 12 % of the pan's height, so it covers the notch and the signals beside it while its width and depth are set. Fix: put it beside the notch, on the side with more room (its edge a finger's width from the notch), and where the window is too narrow for that (a phone upright: the panel is nearly as wide) under the pan, over the waterfall's lower part; the panel's header also drags it (kept inside the window), for a spot of the operator's choosing, remembered for the session. Test: the render tool at 440x956, 956x440, 1180x820 (the iPad panel), 1366x1024 and an 11" Android tablet's landscape (1340x800): a notch at the left, middle and right, the band and its neighbours visible while the panel is open; the hold-to-add path the same. No terms. Size: small. *Chosen for the next build* (the user, 2026-11-19: "Otetaan 107 ja 108 mukaan"). *Done in the dev build (2026-11-19)*: tnfPopup.place() puts the panel a finger's width plus half the notch's band beside it, on the side with more room (right when both fit and the right has as much), else at the foot of the pan; PanelHeader gets draggable (a DragHandler on the title row, the scene translation, kept 8 px inside the window) and dragged(x, y), which the notch panel keeps (userMoved, userPos) for its later openings this session; no other panel is draggable. Rendered on the FLEX by the hold-to-add path (holdmhz at 14.0740 / 14.0756 / 14.0768-14.0772, the pan 14.0736-14.0776; nothing added, Close each time): 440x956 at the foot, the notch marker visible above; 956x440 right, right and left of the notch; 1180x820 and 1340x800 right, right, left; a drag of the title by 200,250 at 1340x800 and 440x956 moved it (clamped inside) and the next notch's panel opened there. Manual: the Notches paragraph; tnf.jpg anew (the panel beside the notch). *Found in Android beta 103 (2026-11-19, the developer's OnePlus Open; the user: "Androidilla ei aina mene notsia oikealle kohtaan ... laatikkoon päälle", "Sama ongelma etukannen kanssa", "Ongelma on 3D-näytös, 2D-näytös näkyy ihan ok")*: on the inner screen (about 700 dp wide) a notch near the middle left no room beside it, and on the cover screen none either, so the panel went to the pan's foot, where the 3D view draws the notch's handle (the strip under its picture, notch.bandTop = spectrumFraction): it covered the notch (devlink screenshots 19:29 and 19:33). *Fixed in the dev build*: beside the notch only within the pan's own width (also keeps it off a pinned side panel: the iPad's middle notch had overlapped it), else under the pan, else above it, else above or below the notch's handle (a finger's height under it kept clear), only last the pan's foot. Rendered on the FLEX: 3D at 406x903 (the cover screen) above the pan at the left, middle and right, the handle and waterfall in sight; 3D at 700x753 beside, above, beside; 2D at 700x753, 440x956 (above the pan) and 1180x820 (beside within the pan, above it for the middle notch).

I sometimes find it hard to remember how to close it - just tap on the Pin Controls header. An X or close button on this header would remind me."). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-19*: OperatePage.qml's drawer has its Close (and the PTT copy) only upright (visible: page.portrait); in landscape it closes by a tap outside it, and Pin Controls at its top pins it as a side panel instead. Fix: in landscape a × button beside Pin Controls (the same row, its own width, "Close controls" for a screen reader), closing the drawer as the upright Close does; no change upright or for the pinned panel (Hide controls in the top row). Rendered on the FLEX: 1340x800 and 1180x820 the × beside Pin Controls, a tap closed the drawer, focussweep: nothing keeps the keyboard; 1024x1366 (upright) and 956x440 (the phone page) unchanged, their Close at the foot. Manual: Controls (§8).
109. **Logging:** POTA spots on the display (*in build 195*, with WWFF), Robin Bishop G5OUT (FB post in OksaSDR Users, 2026-11-20 about 03:50 Finnish time: "Would it be possible to add POTA spots? I see the logbook already has a facility for entering park numbers, if it could also pull in pota spots it would be absolutely amazing! Save me using a seperate logbook as i mostly POTA!"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-20*: the app has the POTA self-spot (67 b, MobileEnginePota.cpp, posting to api.pota.app/spot/) and Their park / My park in the Log tab, but no feed of other activators. The desktop engine already has one: src/core/PotaClient (upstream's, not yet in the mobile build) polls https://api.pota.app/spot/activator every 60 s (the public feed loggers such as Ham2K PoLo read), one DxSpot a new spotId: the activator's call, frequency, "reference name mode" as the comment, the API's expire as its lifetime, PotaSpotColor (#FFFF00); the desktop sends them to the radio as spots like the DX cluster's (MainWindow_Spots.cpp queueSpotCmd). In the app the DX cluster's spots go to the radio the same way (MobileEngine::setupDxCluster), so its tag on the display, tuneToSpot and the Log tab's spotted call (Engine.lastSpotCall) work for them unchanged. Glue: PotaClient into mobile/CMakeLists.txt; a switch under Controls › View beside DX cluster (off by default, its own colour); its spots through the cluster's path; a tapped POTA spot also sets Their park to POTA with its reference (beside the call it already offers); the spot's mode for the slice (SSB / CW / FT8) as the cluster's. Size: small to medium, one build; tested against the live feed receive only. Later: the same for WWFF (spots.wwff.co) and SOTA (SOTAwatch) with the SOTA idea below. *Sources checked 2026-11-20*: POTA api.pota.app/spot/activator (JSON, 14 spots then: activator, frequency, mode, reference, name, locationDesc, grid4/6, spotTime, spotter, comments, expire; unofficial and undocumented, open by the volunteers' goodwill, light polling asked); WWFF spots.wwff.co/static/spots.json (JSON, 45 spots: activator, frequency_khz, mode, reference, reference_name, remarks, spot_time; no terms found), so WWFF costs little beside POTA; SOTA: the official API moved to api-db2.sota.org.uk (/api/spots/{n}/all/all, /api/spots/epoch; api2 answers "deprecated"), its terms: no commercial use, developers on the SOTA Reflector and its API-consumers group first, and "no AI-generated software may connect to the SOTA API without prior approval": SOTA only after the SOTA Management Team approves (the user's call; ties to 97).

With B/S off the step buttons go but their row stays (Macros, the controls button and PTT at the right), its left two thirds empty, while −, +, Band, B/S and + Slice, + Pan still cover the waterfall's bottom; his idea picture moves them down into that empty row. *Pre-analysis 2026-11-20*: PhoneOperatePage.qml's stepRow stays in landscape (visible: page.landscape || Engine.tuneRowsShown) and with the steps hidden holds only a filler; PanadapterView.qml's zoomRow (− + Band B/S) and addRow (Close N other pans, + Slice, + Pan) are anchored to the waterfall's bottom (z 5, opacity 0.85). Only the phone in landscape: upright the row goes with B/S, and the iPad page hides its whole step row. Room: 667 pt (iPhone SE) less PTT (150), the controls button (53) and Macros (about 62) leaves about 400 pt for six buttons of about 320; split paddles (two PTTs of 110) leave about 330, the tightest case. Check: 852x393, 932x430, 667x375, an Android phone and the OnePlus outer screen in landscape, buttons right both ways, split paddles, FT8 (no PTT), B/S on and off, and the focus sweep. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-20)*: PhoneOperatePage's panButtonsBelow (landscape, B/S off): the step row carries − + (zoomOutButtonBelow / zoomInButtonBelow), Band (the band chooser), B/S ▲ (amber frame; shows the steps again), a filler and + Slice / + Pan, mirrored by View › Zoom buttons; PanadapterView's buttonsBelow leaves its − + Band B/S and + Slice / + Pan out (Close N other pans stays), cornerButtonWidth and openBandChooser() for the row; the CW / RTTY decode strip then takes the full width. Rendered on the FLEX: 852x393 (also Right), 667x375 and 800x360 with split CW paddles (667 also Right), 868x390, 932x430, FT8 running (no PTT, no Macros), 393x852 upright unchanged; taps: Band opened the chooser, − then + back to the same span, B/S ▲ brought the steps back and the buttons onto the waterfall; focussweep: nothing keeps the keyboard; the radio back at 14.074 DIGU, the settings as they were.
111. **Distribution:** A check for a newer version in the Android (and later Linux) app (*in Android beta 107*) (the user, 2026-11-20: "Linux ja android versioihin voisi lisätä toiminnon jolla voisi aina päivittää uusimpaan versioo eli softa tarkistaa onko uutta versiota. iOSin osalta taitaa testflight sen hoitaa"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-20*: iOS, iPadOS and the Mac need nothing: TestFlight tells the tester of a new build and installs it by itself with its Automatic Updates (on by default; TestFlight › OksaSDR), the App Store later the same. Android: the APK comes from GitHub, so nothing tells a user of a new beta. The app knows its beta (AETHER_MOBILE_BUILD_NUMBER, from release.env's TAG in CI), and GitHub's https://api.github.com/repos/OH6OKSA/OksaSDR/releases/latest gives the newest release (tag android-beta-N, the APK's download link; no key, 60 requests an hour per address, a generic User-Agent "OksaSDR"). Plan: at start and then once a day, with a "Check for a new version" switch on, the tag's number compared with the app's; a newer one gives a notice on the Connect page and once on the operating screen: "Android beta N is out" with Download (the APK link opened in the browser; Android's own installer then asks, as at the first install, and keeps the settings, the signing key being the same) and What's new (the release page). No download or install inside the app: that needs REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES, which Google Play restricts, and a Play build (98) must not update itself (Play does), so the check is built only into the GitHub APK (a CMake switch the Play build turns off). Linux: not published yet (91, own use); the same check works once a Linux build is a release asset with its own tag, until then off. Privacy: the request shows GitHub the device's address and the app's version; PRIVACY.md (and Play's Data safety later) says so, and the switch turns it off. Test: an own beta number below and equal to the latest (a development override), no network, GitHub answering 403 (rate limit), the notice's buttons on the OnePlus. No terms beyond GitHub's API rules. Size: small. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-20)*: MobileEngineUpdate.cpp (AETHER_MOBILE_UPDATE_CHECK, a CMake option on for Android only; OKSASDR_UPDATE_CHECK / _OWN_BETA / _URL for development): the first read 15 s after start, then every 24 h and on return to the front when the last read is over 12 h old, User-Agent "OksaSDR"; the tag android-beta-N against the app's own beta, the APK from the assets. UpdateOffer.qml (in Main.qml, over either page; never while transmitting; the page gets the keyboard back): *Android beta N is out* with Later (remembered per beta), What's new and Download (both open the browser). View › **New versions** On / Off, the last check in a line, *Download beta N*. Tested on this PC against GitHub and a stand-in: own beta 105 offered 106 (dialog, Later remembered, not offered again, the keyboard back on the page), 106 the newest, HTTP 403, another tag, no JSON, a refused connection; the View row at 1366x1024 and 393x852. Manual (13. Android › New versions, View), privacy (GitHub), README, the tester guide, Android notes. To verify on the OnePlus with beta 107 (the first beta that has it: it can only offer 108).

112. **Display:** AGC-A in the info line while AGC-T is on Auto (*in build 197*; chosen for it by the user 2026-11-20: "Lisää se seuraavan buildin listalle"), Tommy Pierson W6TPR (mail 2026-11-20 17:35, iPhone 17 Pro Max, Aurora AU-510M over SmartLink: "when we put AGC in auto, can we have it say the little a letter instead of still saying whatever mode it's in ... I use auto when clicked on my radio and now AGC-A ... I use slow and mine still says AGC-S"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-20*: the info line's AGC word (InfoPanel.qml agcText, the only place the app shows the mode) is "AGC-" and the mode's letter (S / M / F, AGC-OFF). Engine.agcAuto already tells when AGC-T is automatic: radioOn (the radio sets it itself, SmartSDR 4.3's Auto AGC-T, slice agc_threshold_mode=auto, what Tommy's Aurora shows as AGC-A) and follow (the app's own AGC-T that follows the noise, roadmap 48, the same idea where the radio lacks it); the one-shot Auto measurement is not continuous and keeps the letter. Plan: AGC-A while radioOn or follow (not in FM, which has no AGC); the screen reader's name "AGC slow, AGC-T automatic"; a tap opens the AGC panel as now, where the mode and Auto show. The same five characters, so no width changes on any screen. Manual (the info line, the AGC panel). Test on the FLEX: Auto on and off in the AGC panel, Follow on and off, the mode changed meanwhile (the A stays, the panel shows the mode), FM, a phone and the iPad. No terms. Size: small. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-20)*: InfoPanel shows AGC-A while the radio's own Auto AGC-T is on (radioOn) or Follow actually follows; MobileEngine's Follow tick now says so (agcAuto.following: false while it waits for a measurement, measures, transmits, in FM or with AGC off), so Follow switched on before any measurement shows the mode's letter (found in the test: AGC-A had shown while Follow only waited); the screen reader hears "AGC fast, AGC-T automatic". Tested on the FLEX through the AGC panel with the receive audio on: Auto measured 56 (the knee) and the label stayed AGC-F; Follow following: AGC-A; AGC Off: AGC-OFF; fast again: AGC-A; FM: AGC-OFF; DIGU: AGC-A; Follow off: AGC-F. This FLEX has no Auto AGC-T of its own (radioAvailable false), so the radio's path is the same flag, not seen here. Put back: AGC fast, AGC-T 50, Follow off, the test's measurement and per-mode AGC let go.
113. **Display:** A layout the operator arranges: the desktop's Workspace Canvas on a touch screen (*in build 197*) (Larry Averbeck W9FLA, FB comment on the build 195 post, 2026-11-20 about 19:00: "AetherSDR has an option for a 'Workspace Canvas' where you can rearrange the display elements for a custom look", "A blank canvas where you can Drag, drop, resize and hide portions of your current display. Also, the ability to Save and recall the setups", "I use the Step buttons frequently but seldom the Band buttons. On a larger display, smaller Step buttons would be easier to use in one corner vs. spread across the full width. My Tab display sits on a shelf about chest high tilted back from 30 degrees from vertical. The ANT1 and STEP lines would be more easily accessible below the Waterfall"; his Galaxy Tab in landscape). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-20, second* (the user on the first, three fixed View settings: "Tuo sortaa täysin ideologiaa mikä oli tuossa ajatauksena eli mietippä uudelleen"): the idea is that the operator, not the app, decides where each part of the screen is and how big, hides what he does not use and keeps several arrangements. **The desktop's model** (RFC #4887, src/gui/workspace): items placed freely as fractions of the surface (NormRect, so a layout survives another window size), moved by a title strip and resized by edge and corner grips, snapped to the surface's edges and the other items' edges and centres, a minimum size per item, named workspaces saved without a save step (a debounced auto-commit), "Classic" the shipping layout as default and reset. Its rules are widget-free and tested headless upstream (CanvasLayout, CanvasInteraction applyDrag / snapping, WorkspaceGeometry, WorkspaceDocument; tests/workspace_*_test.cpp), so the app can build those four files and keep the same rules and the same document format. **In the app:** every part of the operating screen a block: Frequency (mode, step), Info line (ANT, width, AGC, speaker, lock, DSP), Band buttons, Slice row, each meter, Display (spectrum and waterfall), Step buttons, Macros, PTT, Controls (the pinned sections; later each section its own block, and Big buttons' − / +). Each block fills the room it is given and reflows into it (the steps as one row, two rows or a column; the bands wrapping; the info line one to three lines; a meter as an arc or a bar), and declares the smallest size its content fits: the editor never goes under it. **Editing:** View › Layout › Edit (also from the top row's menu): the blocks get frames, a block's title strip moves it, an edge or corner resizes it, snapping as on the desktop; × hides a block, + Add brings a hidden one back; Undo, Reset to Classic, Done. While editing nothing transmits or tunes (PTT, the steps and the display inert), and afterwards the page gets the keyboard back (Space PTT on an iPad). **Layouts:** Classic (today's pages, unchanged and the default) and the operator's own, named ("Shelf", "Car"), each with a landscape and a portrait arrangement, chosen in View › Layout, saved as they change, carried by App setups (17) and stored in the desktop's document format. **Tests:** each block alone at its minimum and at a large size (the render tool), the canvas rules by upstream's headless tests built here too, Classic as now; an arrangement nobody listed is then still made of blocks that fit. Screen readers: move and resize as accessible actions in steps. **Phases:** (1) the tablet in landscape (Larry's Galaxy Tab; iPad, Mac, Android tablets) with the blocks above, Classic and one own layout; (2) named layouts, portrait, phones, single control sections and meters as blocks; (3) several panadapters as blocks. The main work is OperatePage split into self-contained blocks (today they lean on the page: its compact, infoScale, the dialogs, the drawer). To ask Larry with a mock of the edit mode first. No terms. Size: large; phase 1 medium-large. (The first pre-analysis, three fixed View settings for the band row, the steps and the info line, is dropped: it decides for the operator.) *Phase 1 done in the dev build (2026-11-20, not in a build yet; the user: "Se että työtä on ei ongelma keskity vain miettimään ratkaisua sekä testaamaan")*: MobileWorkspace (C++, Engine.workspace) on the desktop's WorkspaceDocument / applyDrag / snapRect, built into the app from src/gui/workspace (WorkspaceGeometry, CanvasInteraction, WorkspaceDocument); one layout = a workspace, "main" its landscape and "portrait" its upright arrangement, block ids land:… / port:… (the document's item ids are workspace-wide), stored in MobileWorkspace (App setups carry it), written 750 ms after a change and when the app leaves the front. OperatePage: Classic (the grid) and LayoutCanvas side by side, the display, the pinned controls, Macros and PTT one instance each, moved between Classic's cell and their block (parent bindings), so the waterfall, a held PTT and their cost stay one; the light blocks (FrequencyBlock, ModeBlock, StepSizeBlock, InfoBlock, ButtonGridBlock for the bands and the steps, the slice row) made for the canvas, each filling its block and telling whether a size fits it (the steps 8 / 4 / 2 / 1 columns, the bands any, the info line one or two rows scaled from probes). A new layout or a new orientation starts from Classic measured on screen (Classic shown for a frame); Classic stays as it was (B/S only there). Edit (View › Layout › Edit / New, the top row's ⋯): frames over the blocks, a drag anywhere on a block moves it, handles straddling the corners and edge middles resize it, snapping to the canvas and the other blocks (the desktop's rules), a resize stops where the content still fits, × hides and + Add brings back, Undo (whole document, 50 steps), Reset (Classic again), Delete (asks), Done; the name in the toolbar renames. While editing nothing transmits or tunes (the blocks' content disabled, OperateKeys and Space PTT off, PTT and the CW key released). A block is never dropped on another: dragged over one it goes back to the last free place on its way (red while it would not stay; the desktop's Tidy, push-down only, made a full screen worse and is not used). Tested at 1340x800 (Larry's Tab) against the FLEX by finger drags: a new layout from Classic, the bands hidden, the slice row moved up and snapped, the display grown, the steps to a 4 × 2 block in the bottom-left corner, the info line and the step size under the display, Macros and PTT beside the steps (his arrangement), a too-small steps block stopped at its fit, Undo, rename, Delete; restarted: the layout back; Classic and the layout switched; PTT held while editing at 5 W: nothing sent; the same layout at 1366x1024, 1180x820, 1024x768 and 1440x900; upright 820x1180 Classic until edited, then its own; the controls hidden: the top row's Controls opens the drawer; Big buttons over a layout and back; the focus sweep and the keyboard back after Done, a dialog and Delete; a phone's View without the Layout section. Classic at 1340x800 the same as before the change. **Gesture tests, second round** (the user: "Onhan kaikki siirtelyt testattu aidosti?"; the first round had not tried the corners, every block's limit, a mouse, portrait or a rotation), each result read back in pixels by a new render-tool verb (layoutitems [land|port]): the display's four corners (the opposite edges stay, one edge snapped to the info line's), Undo four times back to the start; every block's limit (frequency 160, mode 60, step size where its type stays 12 px, info line where its scale stays 0.55, bands one row 34, slice row 320, display 240 × 120, controls 300, Macros 80, PTT 100). **Found and fixed:** two blocks sharing an edge had their handles in one place and the upper block took the other's resize (the bands' bottom moved the slice row; the display's corner went to the steps); now only the selected block (a tap or a drag selects it) shows handles, on top of the others. A fast drag past a limit was refused whole; the limit is now found between the last size taken and the finger (bisection), so a 150 ms fling stops the steps at their 8-in-a-row width. Then: the bands' bottom to 34 with the slice row untouched, the display's corner to its minimum with the steps untouched, an unselected block's handle not there to grab, a mouse corner drag (snapped), portrait: the steps' bottom stopped at the controls (no drop on a block), the steps' edge at their row limit, the info line and the mode dropped over another block went back to the last free place; a rotation while editing ends the edit, the keyboard back, landscape and portrait kept apart. **Third round, wider** (the user: "juuri tämän takia tärkeää, että testit tehdään mahdollisimman laajasti"): every block at its limit after Done, looked at: the display's own corner buttons met at 240 × 120 (now at least 300 × 160) and the info line at 55 % was too small to read and tap (now 70 %); the others fit (frequency 160, mode 60, step size 94 × 33, slice row 320 with bar meters, Macros 80, PTT 100). In the code: an App setup applied wrote the layout to the settings but the app kept the old one in memory and wrote it back at the next background; now the layouts are read again when a setup is applied, written before a setup is saved, and written only when changed. Tested through the Prof tab: a setup saved with a layout, Classic chosen, the setup applied: the layout back at once and still stored after the app went to the background; the test setup deleted through the app. FT8 / FT4 running hides PTT and Macros in a layout too (their place stays empty); two slices in the slice block (the second closed again); 130 % text (the info line's limit measured at that size); Split View 678 x 1024 and a foldable's 698 x 751 (upright: Classic until edited) and back to the layout; the radio left at 14.074 DIGU, 100 W, one slice. **Screen readers in Edit** (the user: "saadaanko tämä testattua?"): each block's title is its button ("Frequency block", "…, selected"; its description where it is in points) and selects it; the selected block gets an arrange bar of eight named buttons (Move left / up / down / right, narrower, wider, shorter, taller; 8 points a press, held to repeat; no snapping; the same limits as a drag), and every step is said (Accessible.announce): the new place, or why not ("at its smallest or the edge", "its content would not fit", "another block is there"); the toolbar buttons named for what they do ("Add a hidden block", "Undo the last change", "Reset to Classic's arrangement", "Delete layout", "Done editing the layout"). While editing the blocks' contents show as a picture (ShaderEffectSource) and are hidden, so a screen reader hears 28 elements (11 blocks, 11 hide buttons, the toolbar) instead of 147, and after Done the 116 controls as before. The render tool logs what is said (a11y on installs an update handler for announcements). Tested only by accessibility actions (a11ypress, as a VoiceOver or TalkBack double tap): New, selecting Steps (said where it is), shorter to its limit ("not moved"), up, narrower, right, Mode right until the info line ("another block is there"), wider refused there, Hide Frequency, + Add › Frequency, Undo, Done (the keyboard back), Edit, Delete layout › Delete. **Found and fixed:** the block's frame and its title shared one name, and the press went to the frame (nothing selected); + Add's menu stayed open after a screen reader's choice (its item was taken away with the list before the menu closed; now the menu closes first, for a finger too); "addMenu is not defined" from the menu's delegate. **With audio** (the dev VM's test sink had vanished, so the receive audio had nowhere to go: made permanent, see the dev notes; the render tool's own slice mute lifted for the test by a new plan verb, mute off / on): the receive audio measured from the sink (RMS 744 on 14.074, 0 before), and the blocks' contents in a layout driving the radio: +1k / −1k in the Steps block (14.074 → 14.075 → 14.074), 40m and 20m in the Bands block (and back to 14.074 DIGU), the info line's volume panel opened by a screen reader's press. **The rest** (the user: "Tee loput testit"): in a layout, every block's own controls against the FLEX: the frequency (its entry dialog), the mode and the step size (their choosers), the slice chip (its power panel), a tap on the display (tuned to 14.075), − / +, Band with the Bands block hidden (the bands chooser), Macros (its panel), a hold on the display (the notch panel beside it, no notch made), the arrows (±1 kHz), 3D in the display block (also as Edit's picture); two layouts switched (each its own blocks), Reset (Classic's arrangement again) and its Undo, Delete with another layout left (Classic shown, the other kept), Edit layout from the ⋯ menu in a narrow window. **A fault of the render tool, and an unintended transmission:** its keys verb did not know <Right> and typed the letters onto the page, whose T key toggles TUNE: a tune carrier at the tune power (10 W) on 14.080 MHz for about 1-2 s, then off (the radio checked: receive, tune=0). Fixed in the tool: the arrows are named keys now, an unknown <name> is refused, and text is typed only into a text field (else the page's letter keys would act: T tune, A ATU). Not tested: a real device's touch and VoiceOver / TalkBack themselves (the platform bridges; only Qt's accessibility interface here), two fingers at once, a Mac trackpad, the Android and iOS compiles (no Qt for Android on this PC; CI): these need a build on the devices (a preview), and the developer link has no gesture or plan command yet to run these tests there. Open: the manual (View › Layout, pictures), the Android and iOS builds (CI), a screen reader's move and resize steps, a mock for Larry. Follow-ups after the release, Larry Averbeck W9FLA (FB comment on the build 197 post, 2026-11-21 about 03:00, with a photo of his first layout on his Tab: the frequency centred on top, the slice row between the two meters, a tall display, Macros, the info line, the step size, six steps and PTT in the bottom row; "The quick implementation and ease of use is beyond my expectations!"): (a) "break up the Slices and Meters entry into Meter 1, Meter 2 and another window to view the slices"; (b) "Allow the slice panels to be displayed in one line vs. the two over two. The first two are displayed OK, but the second two are truncated and the next four are hidden behind the pan": with more slices the Slices block's SliceBar wraps into rows taller than the block and runs under the display: a defect of the block (the canvas only; Classic's slice row is its own), to fix first, e.g. one line in the block with the slices narrower or scrolled sideways, never drawn outside the block; (c) "Consider adding an option for a Light version for my old eyes": a light theme changes how Classic looks, so it is a variant (a View setting, Classic's dark the default); approved by the user 2026-11-21 as a choice: "Vaalea teema pitää vaihtoehto tummalle eli käyttäjä voi valita". Pre-analyses to do (b first, then a, then the light theme).
114. **Display:** Hide controls and Controls in the top row also in an own layout (*in build 198*) (the user, 2026-11-21, with build 197 open on the iPad: "mistä saan controls ikkunan suljettua", then "Niin pitää ehdottomasti korjata"). Pre-analysis: in Classic the top row's Hide controls hides the pinned panel and Controls opens the drawer, whose Pin Controls pins it again (Engine.controlsVisible). In an own layout with its Controls block the top button was hidden (build 197), so the controls went away only through the editor's ×. Same model as Classic, on the layout: Hide controls closes the layout's Controls block (its place kept, the other blocks not moved), Controls then opens the drawer, and the drawer's Pin Controls shows the block again where it was; if that place is now taken (or the block was never placed: Classic's controls were a drawer when the layout was made), Pin Controls opens the editor with the block selected to place it, never drawing it over another block. Each orientation keeps its own. Classic's behaviour unchanged (classic-check). Tests: hide / drawer / pin in landscape and portrait, the never-placed block, restart, the screen reader's names, the keyboard back on the page, Classic's own flow. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-21)*: hiding first left a hole where the block was, so Hide controls now gives its room to the blocks beside it as Classic gives the panel's width to the display (MobileWorkspace::hideAndFill): the side is the one where the display takes the room in its lane, else the one whose blocks cover the block's edge most; a block grows only within the hidden block's span, and where the display is behind a neighbour in its lane the display grows and the blocks between move (portrait: the display taller, the steps below it); what grew or moved is stored with the layouts (MobileWorkspaceFill) and Pin Controls (or + Add in the editor) puts it back unless edited since. Tested on the FLEX by the controls' own names, read back in pixels: 1366x1024 (Classic's arrangement): hide: bands, slices, display, steps and PTT 964 -> 1338 wide; pin: all back exactly; restart in between: hidden and grown kept; the display edited while hidden (1322 wide): pin keeps its new size, restores the rest, and as the controls' place is now taken opens the editor with Controls selected ("Controls selected, left 978..."); the controls shrunk into the bottom-right corner (978,500 360x444): only steps and PTT grow (the display spans past it), back on pin; a layout made while Classic's controls were hidden (the block never placed): Controls opens the drawer, Pin Controls opens the editor with it selected; 1024x1366 (Classic's portrait, the controls under the display): the display 236 -> 824 high and the steps moved below it (546 -> 1134), PTT and Macros unchanged, back on pin; Classic's own Hide controls / Controls / Pin Controls as before; the screen reader reads Hide controls / Controls; the page keeps the keyboard throughout. The waterfall fills a taller display over a few seconds, in Classic alike.
115. **Display:** The drawer's × in landscape without Pin Controls took a row of its own, otherwise empty (*in build 198*) (the user, 2026-11-21, on the Android foldable's inner screen: "androidissa se controlsin sulkeva ruksi tuo yhden koko rivin lisää controlssiin ja rivi jää muuten kokonaan tyhjäksi"; build 191's 108). Pre-analysis: a tablet-layout window too narrow to pin the controls (the OnePlus Open's inner screen 751x698 in landscape, Split View, small iPads) shows the drawer with the × alone in the top row. Putting it into the section buttons' grid cut "FreeDV" (the cells narrower) or, as a cell of its own, made 5 + 5 + 3 rows (no row saved); so the × sits beside the first row of buttons and the drawer is 48 px wider (Theme.touchTarget + 4) there, the buttons keeping their width and rows. A change to Classic asked by the user. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-21)*, tested by measurement and pictures: 751x698 the grid 347 wide in 6 + 6 rows, the × at its end (was 3 rows), every label whole, the × closes the drawer; with the Amp tab 5 + 5 + 3 and the × in the first row; 698x668 and 930x864 two rows; 1180x820 (Pin Controls fits) and portrait (Close at the bottom) unchanged.
116. **Display:** The Slices block shows the slices in one line and never draws outside itself (*in build 198*) (Larry Averbeck W9FLA, 113 (b), FB comment on the build 197 post: "Allow the slice panels to be displayed in one line vs. the two over two. The first two are displayed OK, but the second two are truncated and the next four are hidden behind the pan"). Pre-analysis: the block holds Classic's SliceBar, whose chips are two columns that grow downward (two over two, four over four); Classic's slice row grows with them, a block has a fixed height, so the third and later chips ran over the display (z under it). In a block the chips go in one line when the width gives each at least the chip's least width (150 px on a tablet), else in as many rows as the block's height holds at the chips' least height (34 px), else in one line that scrolls sideways; clipped to the block always. The chip itself (frequency, mode, the TX tag with the power, the power slider, the slice menu, the off-band outline, the screen reader's name) becomes a component of its own (SliceChip) that SliceBar and the block share, so Classic's chips stay exactly as they are (classic-check). *Done in the dev build (2026-11-21)*: SliceChip (Classic's chip, unchanged by classic-check and by pixels) in SlicesBlock: tested on the FLEX with this app's slices and more put in by status lines with its own client handle (the render tool's status verb takes $OWN): 3 slices in a 534-wide block one line of 175 px; 8 slices 3 + 3 + 2 rows of 175 x 42 inside its 135 px; the block lowered to 68 px: one line of 150 px clipped at its edge, a sideways drag scrolled D..F into view; nothing drawn outside the block in any.
117. **Display:** Meter 1, Meter 2 and the slices as blocks of their own (*in build 198*) (Larry Averbeck W9FLA, 113 (a): "break up the Slices and Meters entry into Meter 1, Meter 2 and another window to view the slices"). Pre-analysis: three blocks replace "Slices and meters" in own layouts: Meter 1 (the left meter, its pair or two bars as View sets it), Meter 2 (the right one), Slices (116). Each meter sizes its face from its block (the dial's height from the block's, the width capped by it) and offers what the meter offers on Classic (a tap for the meter's chooser). A new layout takes them from Classic's slice row (the meters' and the chips' own places). Layouts made with build 197 keep their look: the first time a surface with the old combined block is shown, it is split into the three at the places the meters and chips had in it, measured on an unseen SliceBar of that size (not guessed), and stored. Classic unchanged. To test: 1, 2, 4 and more slices (status lines put in where the radio has no more), one line / rows / scrolling, the meters' styles (analog, bar, two, analog2) in their blocks and their limits, the split of a 197 layout in both orientations, the screen reader's names, Classic by classic-check. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-21)*: MeterBlock (the face's height from the block, as wide as that face needs, scaled down only where the block is narrower; at the top as on Classic), the three blocks seeded from Classic's slice row (SliceBar's leftMeterItem, chipAreaItem, rightMeterItem) and build 197's block split on first show (splitBlock, measured on an unseen SliceBar). Tested: a new layout from Classic is pixel for pixel Classic's slice row (the meters 208 x 135 and the chip 210 x 55 at the same places, landscape 1366x1024 and portrait 1024x1366; only the live needle differs); a stored build 197 layout split into meter1 0,182 208x135, slices 215,182 534x135, meter2 756,182 208x135, as a new one, in landscape and in portrait (0,168 / 215 566 wide / 788), stored; Hide controls (114) now lets the slice chips take the row's room and moves Meter 2 to the right edge as Classic does (a lane's display, else its slice chips, take the room); the meter styles bar / two / analog2 in the blocks (analog2 at 208 px two bars, as Classic's rule); the screen reader's "Meter 1 block", "Slices block", "Meter 2 block" and their Hide buttons.
118. **Display:** A light theme beside the dark one, the user's choice (Larry Averbeck W9FLA, 113 (c), FB comment on the build 197 post: "Consider adding an option for a Light version for my old eyes"; the user 2026-11-21: "Vaalea teema pitää vaihtoehto tummalle eli käyttäjä voi valita", chosen for build 199: "1 ja 2"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-21:* the colours are Theme.qml's tokens (background #0f1419, surface, surfaceRaised, text, textDim, accent, transmit, the tags, warning, passband; the buttons by Engine.buttonStyle, "lit" by default and no longer offered in the UI), used 619 times, but 130 colour literals sit in 21 QML files (PanadapterView 27, DigiControls 22, AnalogGauge 10, RadioSetupPage 8, LoggingSetup 8, NetworkBadge 7, InfoPanel 7, BarGauge 5 ...) and 26 named colours / Qt.rgba, Main.qml sets the Controls palette (menus, popups, text fields), and C++ draws the spectrum's axis band and grid (SpectrumItem) and the 3D view's background. Plan: Controls › View › **Theme**: *Dark* (the default, as now) and *Light*, switched at once and remembered (AppSettings MobileTheme; in App setups too). Theme.qml gets the light set of the same tokens and new tokens for the literals that are not the display's (the status greens "#00cc66", the network and CPU colours, the white text on red / blue tags, Main.qml's palette, the meters' faces); every dark value stays exactly as now, so Classic and every dark screen stay pixel-identical (classic-check at all sizes is the proof). Stays dark in Light: the display (spectrum, waterfall, 3D and what is drawn on them: slices, filters, notches, spots, the FTx and digi overlays, the axis band), as a radio's screen: the waterfall's palette is made on black, SmartSDR's is dark too, and its overlay colours stay readable without a second set. Follows the theme: the frequency, the info line, the rows and buttons, the meters (a light face with a dark scale and needle, like a panel meter), the slice chips, the panels and the drawer, dialogs and menus, Radio Setup, Connect, Log, Big buttons, App setups and the manual viewer. Contrast: text 4.5:1 and large text / icons 3:1 against the light surfaces, measured from the shots; the status colours get darker light variants (a green that reads on white); the red TX and blue slice tags keep white text (4.5:1 already). The system bars: dark icons on a light page (iOS status bar style, Android's light status / navigation bars), checked on the devices. Not in this step: *Follow the system* (Qt's colour scheme hint, later on request) and a light display. Tests: classic-check (Dark unchanged, 27 sizes, DIGU and USB); the same sweep in Light at every size with the panel plan and the focus sweep (every drawer tab, the pinned panel, popups, dialogs, Radio Setup pages, Big buttons, Log, Connect), side by side with Dark, every text's contrast read as numbers; switching back and forth on the page and after a restart; the screen readers' names unchanged; the manual: a Theme paragraph under View and two Light pictures (the operating page, the View tab). Size: medium to large (about 21 QML files and Main.qml's palette; no C++ while the display stays dark). *Done in the dev build (635eb649e)*: Theme.qml's light set and a fixed dark set (Theme.dark) for the display; PanadapterView's items on the display (19 uses) on the dark set, its four popups following the theme; the meters' faces repainted with dark ink on a light face; the info line, the network badge, the press highlights, Main.qml's palette and the status greens (25, now Theme.good) follow; the drawers opaque in Light (the page's text showed through at 0.96); Light asks Qt for the light colour scheme (QStyleHints::setColorScheme: iOS's status bar dark on the light page), Dark asks nothing as before; the View tab's Theme row placed before Text size; the render tool's theme verb (also in link plans) and render-sweep.py --theme. Contrast computed for every light token against the page, panels, raised rows and buttons: text 13.7-18.2, dim text 5.4-7.2, accent 5.3-7.1, red 5.0-6.7, amber 5.0-6.7, green 5.0-6.6, white on the blue / red tags 4.9-5.0. Rendered on the FLEX in Light with no QML warning: the page at 390x844 and 1366x1024, all twelve Controls tabs in the phone's drawer, the band, mode, AGC, filter and meter choosers, Radio Setup, the stations dialog, Macros, Big buttons, the layout editor (a copy made and deleted), the Connect page; the switch back to Dark at once. Manual: a Light theme section with its picture (theme-light), Theme in the View tab's list; README feature and status rows; TestFlight and Android beta 110 notes. classic-check against build 198 (after its fix, 6be92169c: the first run had swept build 198's QML twice, found by a light sweep that came out dark): layout decisions 0, panels moved 0, masks live content plus the Theme row itself where the pinned View tab reaches it (2560x1375; a shorter scroll bar at 1440x900, 1710x1045, 1728x1050), approved. The light sweep at all 27 sizes (render-sweep --theme light): no QML warning, layout decisions identical to Dark's (layout-diff 0 / 0), every size light around the dark display, the bar meters (844x390) with dark scale numbers on a light track. To do on devices (preview): the iOS status bar on the light page, Android's status and navigation bars, a real touch and screen reader pass.
119. **Devices:** Android logs the keys and the push-to-talk broadcasts the app does not use, so a handset's own PTT key can be read from a log (41, Rob Metallo VA3MET's UNIWA F400 on Android 15: its key works with Zello, QSO 1 and Pocket SDR; chosen for build 199: "1 ja 2"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-21:* a handset's PTT key reaches apps as a key event (often a vendor key code or KEYCODE_UNKNOWN with a scan code) or as a broadcast that push-to-talk apps register for; Zello documents com.zello.ptt.down / com.zello.ptt.up and com.zello.intent.buttonExtra1.down / .up for handset makers (support.zello.com, hardware partner integration). OksaActivity.dispatchKeyEvent passes today's keys on (the Ulanzi dial's only while it is on) and logs none. Plan, logging only, nothing keyed: (a) every key event the app does not use logged once per press (key code and its name, scan code, the device's name and vendor / product, down / up, repeat), the first 40 of a run, then a count; (b) a receiver registered while the app runs (RECEIVER_EXPORTED on Android 13+) for Zello's actions and the handset actions in common use (to be listed from the vendors' and the PoC apps' public documents before coding), logging the action and its extras. Rob then presses the key once and sends Log › Send to the developer; the PTT itself is the next step (41 (a)'s learned *PTT key*, off by default, under Transmit from the controller). Tests: the render tool cannot run the Java side and no Android device is attached here, so on the user's OnePlus with the beta: keys the app does not use (the volume keys while the dial is off, a Bluetooth keyboard's F-keys if one is at hand) must show their lines in the devlink log, and Space PTT and the Ulanzi dial work as before; the receiver's registration logged at start. iOS: unchanged. Size: small. *Done in the dev build (d8013beeb)*: OksaActivity logs keys from devices other than a full keyboard and keys Android cannot name (down / up, key code and name, scan code, the device's name, vendor:product, built-in or external) and a receiver (RECEIVER_EXPORTED on Android 13+) for com.zello.ptt.down / up, com.zello.intent.buttonExtra1.down / up, android.intent.action.PTT.down / up (RugGear and MediaTek handsets), com.sonim.intent.action.PTT_KEY_DOWN / UP and com.symbol.button.L2, the first 40 lines of a run, into the app's log through a native registered from C++ (android/KeyLog.cpp; until then logcat); nothing keyed. Java compile-checked against android.jar (API 36) with a QtActivity stub; the desktop build passes. On the emulator (Android 15, x86_64 with arm64 translation) with the preview's CI APK (Android run 136): "key log: on ... listening for 9 push-to-talk broadcasts" at start, all nine actions sent with adb am broadcast logged with their extras (source=test), KEYCODE_UNKNOWN logged, Space from the virtual keyboard not logged; but the emulator's own volume key was not, as its hardware keys come from "AT Translated Set 2 keyboard", a full keyboard (type 2), which the first rule skipped wholesale. A handset may report its buttons the same way, so the rule is now: a full keyboard's typing keys (letters, digits, punctuation, Space, Tab, Enter, arrows, editing keys, modifiers, F1-F12, Esc) are not logged, every other key is (volume, media, camera, headset, vendor codes). To retest on the emulator with the next APK, then the OnePlus.

Ideas to analyse before they get a number (2026-11-09: a number only after a pre-analysis):
