- From Ian Gilchrist VK1HF's own build (mail 2026-11-09, his code GPLv3, offered as a branch): two SmartLink accounts with favourite radios that connect in one tap (now 62); Sign out as one tap with a confirmation (our two-tap "Sign out?" is easy to miss on a phone); the CW repeat's pause timed from the radio unkeying (cwx sent= at the batch's end, then TX off for 800 ms) rather than from the queue emptying; an RX jitter buffer over SmartLink (Off / 100 / 200 / 400 ms, upstream discussion #6537). His build b5 (mail 2026-11-10): the Connect page out of shape with Android's font size above the default ("OksaSDR" and the version overlapping, a radio card's email line through its border; fixed here in build 163, see there; his own fix also let the system size only shrink the phone layout's text and gave View › Text size 90 %, not taken); hiding the demo radio: now 56.
- A KiwiSDR receiver as a receive antenna (Wayne Roth WA2N, mail 2026-11-08/09: "Scrape KiwiSDR as antenna from AetherSDR? Can Claude do that?"). Numbered 50 overnight without a pre-analysis, made an idea again by the user 2026-11-10 ("Kiwisdr numeroimattomaksi"); 50 is not reused. *Pre-analysis 2026-11-09*: possible but large. The engine code is in our tree (src/core KiwiSdrClient, KiwiSdrProtocol, KiwiSdrManager, KiwiPublicDirectory, KiwiSdrCredentialStore), but the desktop's glue is big (MainWindow_KiwiSdr.cpp 2427 lines, KiwiSdrApplet 356, KiwiPublicReceiverPicker 279) and the app would need its own: Kiwi receivers in the RX antenna chooser, a slice whose audio and waterfall come from the Kiwi (12 kHz audio, its own waterfall beside the radio's pan), the band and frequency following the slice. The public receiver list comes only from upstream's mirror (KiwiPublicDirectory kDirectoryUrl https://cdn.aethersdr.com/kiwi.json, made at the KiwiSDR maintainer's request, no fallback to kiwisdr.com), and each operator's ext_api policy applies. Upstream open: RFC #3613, #4993 (buzzing audio on selecting a Kiwi since 26.8.2), #5450 (the directory mirror), #7072, #7073. Verdict: not now; worth a number once upstream's audio issue is fixed and the mirror's terms for other clients are clear.
- RX audio drops for up to 30 s on an iPhone (Barry Cohen N2BJ, mail 2026-11-12, build 168, iPhone 17 Pro Max with iOS 27.2 on his LAN, a FLEX-6600 and a 6400, an SO2R Tuner Genius and a Power Genius): "constant audio drop outs sometimes for as long as 30 seconds and then would come back by themselves"; PTT brings it back (which restarts the app's playback), and switching the RX antenna from ANT1 to ANT2 was hard. No log yet: the reply asks for Log › Send to the developer right after a drop, and what he saw with the antenna.
- SmartLink closed on the mobile network (now 99) (Mohammed Alkhayarin A71AT, mail with his log 2026-11-16, build 178, iPad, iOS 27.0.1, an Aurora AU-510, Qatar: "running perfect ... with a 5G network ... but suddenly it stopped working", the radio listed and signed in, only the home network connects). The log: 17 SmartLink attempts, every one connect_ready and the TLS connection to the radio's public address open, then closed from the far end 30-40 ms after wan validate on the cellular network (Qt's "The remote host closed the connection", also what an injected reset gives); at 17:31 on Wi-Fi the same SmartLink connection worked, at 17:33 back on cellular every attempt closed again. The app does the same on both, so the mobile network (operators in Qatar filter VoIP-like traffic) or the home router's port forward is the likely cause. Asked him (reply 2026-11-16): SmartSDR for iOS over the same 5G, OksaSDR on another Wi-Fi, changes at home, the iPad's own SIM or a hotspot. His answer (2026-11-16): SmartSDR for iOS connects on the same iPad, 5G and account, and so does OksaSDR beta 92 on his Android phone over 5G (his video: MOE on 20 m and 15 m, 19-65 ms); it worked on the iPad over 5G that morning and stopped after a disconnect and reconnect, Wi-Fi at home still works. So it is ours and on the iPad; nothing in the connect path changed between builds 174 and 178. Asked him next: Wi-Fi off in Settings, the iPad on the phone's hotspot, Sign out and in. Item 84's log lines (ports, UPnP, the hole punch port, the end before or after the radio answered) for his next log. His log also had LogControls.qml:1066 "Unable to assign [undefined] to bool" (the POTA dialog's park row before its info came): fixed with !! as the dialog's other rows.
- ~~A demo video of the app in operation~~ *done*: made in the dev build by a ShotPlan script (version 5: the iPad tour, SSB, CW, RADE and RTTY on the air, the phone part) and published in the OksaSDR Users group on 2026-11-13. The idea as it was: (Kent Winrich K9EZ, FB question 2026-11-12: "Has anyone created a video showing the software in operation?"; answered with the manual for now). Two ways: the dev build at iPad or iPhone size against the FLEX, driven by a ShotPlan script (repeatable, redone after big builds), recorded with gpu-screen-recorder or frame grabs, the radio's audio with pw-record, captions (SRT) and tap marks made from the same plan by ffmpeg; or the iPad's own screen recording by the user, partly driven over the developer link (band, mode, tune, zoom, drawer), the file copied to this PC and cut and captioned here. Not a release item; publishing (YouTube, the group) asked first.
- The Ulanzi D100 dial as a control on the iPad and Android, as on the desktop (now 83) (Christoph Steinert DM5HF, mail 2026-11-14, build 169, iPad Pro 13 M4: "would it be possible to add the Ulanzi D100 like in AetherSDR (more convenient use for portable due to the internal accumulator)", for the voice keyer). The desktop reads the dial's key stream (src/core/UlanziChordDecoder: the knob as volume up / down keys, the top row and press as media keys, the side buttons as Ctrl chords) through evdev / IOKit / Windows HID, none of which an iPad app has. To analyse: how the D100 connects (Bluetooth or a 2.4 GHz dongle), which of its keys iPadOS hands to an app at all (volume and media keys are normally the system's) and which Android does (KeyEvent sees volume and media keys); the Ctrl chords would arrive as keyboard keys on both.
- Keyboard keys for TUNE, the ATU, a two-tone test and a PTT that stays keyed (now 85) (Christoph Steinert DM5HF, mail 2026-11-16 evening: his D100H on the Mac through Ulanzi Studio, which turns each button into a key: the knob already tunes by ← / →, F1-F3 play the voice keyer, Space is PTT while held; "If now in combination the buttons could work, that would be perfect", his buttons labelled VOX, PTT, ATU and 2 tone tune, and a photo of the D100H beside his cabled CTR2). The app has keys only for tuning, zoom, frequency entry, Space PTT, F1-F12 / ⌘1-⌘0 and Esc; to analyse: which functions get keys (TUNE, ATU tune, two-tone, PTT toggle with a time limit, VOX when 19 exists), fixed keys or chosen ones (the desktop has Configure Shortcuts), and whether Ulanzi Studio holds a key for the length of a press (Space PTT) or sends a tap. Serves any keyboard and a Stream Deck on a Mac too.
- The DX cluster after a network change (now 86) (the developer's iPad, build 180, 2026-11-16): after each switch between Wi-Fi and the hotspot the cluster (dxc.nc7j.com) closed the new connection right after login, several times ("closed 2 times right after login"), until the session from the old network had timed out on the server: most likely a second login with the same call refused. To analyse: a log line naming it, the retry spaced out, or a different SSID (call-1) while the old session lingers; how other clusters treat a duplicate login.
- The slice kept through a reconnect on a one-station radio (now 87) (Rich Yost N2RY, mail with his log 2026-11-16, build 180, iPhone17,4, iOS 27.0.1, FLEX-6500 over SmartLink: "Was on 18.130ish USB, walked outside and switched from WiFi to 5gUW, app connection dropped for like 5 secs then came back to CW to 14.111"). The log: the network change handled as meant (14:08:57 cellular, a new SmartLink server connection, signed in 1.2 s later, the radio back at 14:09:04.9), but the radio (one station) still held the app's session from Wi-Fi with its slice on 18.130 USB; the slice create on the pan the radio restored was refused (5000002a), the app recreated the pan, evicted the stale session, and its slice recovery made a new slice, which the radio put on its default 14.111 CW. To do: keep the slice's frequency, mode and filter when a session drops, and give them to a slice the app has to make again after a reconnect (only then; a slice the radio kept stays as it is).
- SOTA (Summits On The Air) beside POTA (Kent Winrich K9EZ, FB reply on the build 178 post, 2026-11-16: "one thing that would be fun to add is some SOTA (Summits On The Air) integration. I have a SOTA spotting server I am working on. Its up but needs a bit more work"). To analyse: what POTA has in the app that SOTA would mirror (a summit reference in the Log tab and ADIF MY_SOTA_REF / SOTA_REF, a self-spot to SOTAwatch through its API, the summit list), and Kent's server and its terms once it is ready. Kent again (FB, 2026-11-17 evening): "If there is anyhting I can do to help move the SOTA part along, please let me know ... www.Sotl.as is a great site with a dataabse behind it": SOTLAS (sotl.as), a SOTA summit map over the SOTA database, as a source of summit data to look at in the pre-analysis beside the SOTA programme's own summit list and spots.
- OksaSDR on the desktops (Linux now 91): Linux, Windows and Intel Mac from the same code (the user, 2026-11-17 night: "kyllästynyt desktopilla kun pitää olla monta ohjelmaa"; a study, no build asked). Study in the doc "OksaSDR työpöydälle" (claude.ai/artifact/GsLyWL4td6qyFv4qca9La6): the app already runs on Linux as the dev build; upstream's appimage.yml, macos-dmg.yml (arm64 + x86_64, macOS 14.4) and windows-installer.yml can be pointed at aether-mobile; the work is packaging and signing, a real secret store (qtkeychain), audio device choice and desktop habits (windows, shortcuts); Windows needs the POSIX headers (unistd.h, dlfcn.h) behind QLibrary; Intel Macs only through a native macOS build (iPad apps need Apple silicon; macOS 26 is the last for Intel). Suggested order: Linux for own use first (the user's daily ThinkPad, on Linux, then the cabin radio PC, which runs upstream AetherSDR 26.8.3 today), then a native Mac, Windows last (tested by testers or a Windows VM: the ThinkPad's old Windows partition would take the daily machine away). Open questions in the doc.
- Big buttons: a Log button to a simple log screen, just the callsign and maybe the report (Michael Walker, mail 2026-11-15: "Can you have a button to move to a LOG screen where you just type in the callsign and maybe the signal report?") (now 75). The Log tab has the fields already; to analyse: a big-text overlay or the drawer's Log tab, and the on-screen keyboard's room on a phone.
- Logging by voice: "Hey Siri, log VA3MW" (Michael Walker, the same mail, with a Siri Shortcut sketch: dictate the phonetics, keep each word's first letter; layers 0 and 1 now 79). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15* (the user: "pohdi samalla Michael Walkerin Siri asiaa ja onko se Gemini android puolella", then any AI, Claude's free app among them: which is the most sensible): iOS App Shortcuts need no setup and work locked and in CarPlay, but a phrase cannot carry free text (Apple DTS: no arbitrary string parameters in the phrase; one AppEntity / AppEnum at most), so it is two turns: "Hey Siri, log a contact in OksaSDR", Siri asks the call, the operator spells it; a Swift App Intent inside the Qt / CMake Xcode project needs a spike first; iOS 27's Siri AI tools are not available to TestFlight testers. Android: Google Assistant left phones in September 2026; App Actions are Assistant only; Gemini calls third-party apps through AppFunctions (Android 16+) only in a private preview, so today "Hey Google, open OksaSDR" and the app's own voice entry. Claude (or ChatGPT) as the front end: a remote MCP connector (the free plan has one) is called from the vendor's cloud, so it would need our own relay to the phone, with sign-in, hosting and a transmitter beside a cloud path, and no wake word: not sensible for this. Sensible, tied to no assistant, in layers: (0) our own phonetics parser (NATO and ITU words, digits and niner / fife / tree, slash / stroke / portable) with a read-back, small; (1) a voice-log button in the Log tab and Big buttons with on-device speech recognition (iOS SFSpeechRecognizer / SpeechAnalyzer, Android's on-device recognizer), offline, the receive audio muted while listening and never while transmitting, medium; (2) on (1): the iOS two-turn App Shortcut (medium, after the spike) and an Android link that opens straight into listening (small), AppFunctions when Google opens it. The microphone shared with the radio audio and PTT to test first (iOS's session interruption, Android 10's capture sharing). Sources: Apple's App Shortcuts HIG and developer forum threads 777854 and 832277; developer.android.com/ai/appfunctions; support.claude.com's connectors article.
- Upstream's fix for #6051 (PR #6992, merged 2026-11-15, rfoust) (now 74): RX audio packets put back in order (a 30 ms wait for a missing one) before Opus / PCM decoding, so reordering on LTE / WireGuard / Wi-Fi is not concealed as loss. Ours (PanadapterStream's VitaSequenceTracker) only counts loss net of reordering. To analyse: its VitaRxReorder.h and the PanadapterStream changes against our copy, with Barry Cohen's iPhone drops above in mind.
- From Christoph Steinert DM5HF (mail 2026-11-15, build 170 on a Mac: the DVK on the CTR2, Esc and the DX cluster Spot work; (a), (c) and (b) now 76, 77 and 78): (a) a clear button in the Log tab's callsign field; (b) editing a contact already logged (a tap or double click on it in the list): today a logged QSO cannot be changed in the app; (c) a Name field beside Comment, filled with QRZ.com's first name (ADIF NAME is already filled from the lookup; "no prio"); (d) F1-F12 in RADE play the voice keyer as analog SSB although RADE is on: *in build 171*: the radio's voice keyer is held off in RADE (it plays through the radio's analog chain); the app's own recordings already go through the RADE encoder; (e) the arrow keys do not move the cursor in the FreeDV tab's FreeDV Reporter message field (on a Mac's keyboard); *in build 171*: AppKit's function-key characters mapped and navigation keys logged, to be confirmed by his log.
- The CTR2's buttons for the Tuner Genius XL's Tune and a PTT that lets go (Hanno Vogels DK3HV, mail 2026-11-17, build 182, iPad Air 5, iOS 27.2.1, a CTR2-MIDI over Bluetooth, a Tuner Genius XL; thanks for the tuner on top of the Amp tab): (a) "tune" on button 3 "is like MOX ... as long as I push the button, it is transmitting", while the screen's Tune "ask[s] for TGXL and start[s] the short tune process". The MIDI functions TUNE and ATU are the radio's (TUNE keys the radio's tune carrier on and off as the TUNE button on the TX controls; ATU runs the radio's own ATU, nothing without one); the tuner's Tune on the Amp tab (Engine.tgxlTune) has no MIDI function. To weigh: a MIDI function "Tuner Genius XL Tune" (the same call, without the screen's question, under Transmit from the controller), or TUNE / ATU going to the TGXL when the radio has none. (b) PTT on button 4 "is not a push to talk; it has a hanger and PTT keeps pushed". PTT (hold) follows the note on and off (Mohammed A71AT's CTR2 lets go every time in his log of 2026-11-17: "PTT released: MIDI released"); a PTT that stays keyed fits MOX (on / off) learned on the button, or a button that sends only on a press. To ask: which function the button has on the MIDI tab (PTT (hold) or MOX), and what Last message shows on a press and on letting go.
- Mohammed Alkhayarin A71AT's log of 2026-11-17 (build 182, iPad mini A17 Pro, iOS 27.0.1, the AU-510 over SmartLink, MIDI PTT on a CTR2 at up to 520 W; no words in the mail): (a) SmartLink connected every time, once on the second try (84 f, 00:29). (b) With the app in the background an audio interruption began (10:13:50, a call or another app's audio); the radio dropped the session at 10:14:22, the reconnect in the background (84 j) made it again in 7 s, but the receive audio could not start ("failed to open RX audio sink", the play-and-record session not allowed to start in the background: "Session activation failed"), so iOS suspended the app again and the radio dropped that session too; brought to the front at 10:15:11 it was back in 7 s. To weigh: after an interruption the reconnect waits for the front, or the audio starts again as playback only in the background. (c) The 3D view was switched off as "too slow on this GPU" in both runs, each time just after a global profile was loaded (10:02:26; 00:30:18, a second after the app went inactive): on an A17 Pro that is the busy GUI thread (the profile's status burst) or the inactive app, not the GPU; the five late frames should not count then (a warm-up after a profile load and while inactive). (d) The "held ... 2147483647 s" line again (in the build 183 list).
- The View tab's display sliders moved by a finger that only meant to scroll (now 101) (Rich Yost N2RY, mail with his log 2026-11-17, build 182, iPhone17,4, FLEX-6500 on his LAN: "suddenly the waterfall partially disappeared ... Tried to restart app but didn't come back"; the screenshot: the 3D view above, the waterfall black). The log: 23 FFT frames and 1 waterfall row in the first five seconds against 103 and 44 in the run before, as FFT frames/s at 5 and Waterfall speed at its slow end would give; both are kept in the radio, so a restart did not help. A Slider takes the value at the point pressed and is sent on release (DisplayControls.qml PanSetting), so a touch on its track while scrolling the drawer sets it. To weigh: a slider in a scrolling tab moved only by a horizontal drag past a threshold (not by a press on the track), or by its handle; the same for every slider in the drawer. Also in his log: the KPA1500's TCP control link timed out every 13 s while its UDP readings answered at times. Rich's answer (2026-11-17): "the waterfall speed was like 1, after resetting it its was fine" (confirmed); port 1500 was taken by his Node-RED flow for the UDP side, Tailscale next.
- Two crashes on build 181 from Scott Stanford W5SLS (now 100) (TestFlight feedback 2026-11-17 21:48 and 21:56, iPhone 16 Pro Max, iOS 27.2, mobile data): "When I touch the top of the screen that shows “xxxMB” it crashes" (the top row's network badge with RAM ... MB, which opens Network & performance) and "I was just about to disconnect and program froze"; no crash log from the API. The panel opened and refreshed on the dev build at 440x956 against the FLEX (2026-11-18) without a fault. He has build 184 installed since. *Reproduced 2026-11-18 on the developer's iPad* (build 184, iOS 27.2.1, a hardware keyboard, the FLEX over the VPN): a tap on the top row froze everything and iOS ended the app, twice (08:21, 08:22); the next start logged "previous run: GUI thread stalled 32181 ms ... last frame 32059 ms before", nothing logged after the tap. The same panel opens fine in the dev build on Linux (440x956 with the FLEX, 1376x1032 and 1032x1376 without), so it is iOS-only and the GUI thread hangs (a loop or a lock), not a crash in itself. Wanted: the iPad's crash report (.ips, the watchdog's main thread stack); build 184's testflight-symbols artifact holds only the stripped binary (no dSYM: the archive's dSYMs folder was empty), so our own frames will not symbolize: the CI copy of the dSYM to fix with it.
- Build 184 heard from (2026-11-18): Hanno Vogels DK3HV: the Tuner Genius XL Tune from the MIDI button "works perfect after replacing the function" (93), with SmartSDR 4.3.19 on his radio; Michael Walker: voice logging "worked great today from the car" (mail and the FB post); Mohammed A71AT: the microphone handed to another app and back "very fast and effective".
- Hanno Vogels DK3HV, mails 2026-11-18 09:06 and 09:16 (build 184, iPad Air 5, a CTR2-MIDI): (a) the PTT that stayed keyed is solved: "The tx hold works perfect fine now when allocated to the button 3", his six buttons band down, band up, PTT on / off or hold, Tuner Genius XL Tune, step smaller, step wider: "All works perfect" (closes the PTT question of the CTR2 item above). (b) "this new knob from the Belgium guys. It is somehow working but not stable", a 14 s video: an M5Stack Dial (round touch display, its own frequency and a PTT / TX screen) beside the iPad on 15 m; the knob's frequency ran ahead of the app's for a moment (21.30x on the knob, 21.298 on the iPad at 4.5 s), its PTT keyed the radio (TX on the knob at 9 s), both on 21.284 at the end. Probably the CTR2-Dial (the CTR2-MIDI's code on an M5Dial, Bluetooth LE or USB MIDI to SmartSDR for iOS / macOS: https://community.flexradio.com/discussion/8031116/introducing-ctr2-dial-a-midi-controller-for-smartsdr-for-ios-macos). Asked him: which device and firmware, how it connects (MIDI to the iPad, and the radio by Wi-Fi?), what is not stable, and a log right after it happens.
- The Tuner Genius XL's own amplifier keying in the app: its *PTT OUT when tuning* (setup `tuneptt1` / `tuneptt2`) and the PTT IN source (`flexradio ... source=LAN|RCA`), read and set over its port 9010 link (4O3A's TG XL API Commands, upstream discussion #2595) (the user, 2026-11-17, on SmartSDR 4.3.19's Tune TX Suppression with another maker's amplifier before a TGXL: "Tarkista"). What was found: the radio's own suppression (the per-band switch in Radio Setup › Transmit › TX band settings since build 158) holds the RCA TX1-3 outputs off only while the radio itself is in TUNE; the TGXL's autotune keys the radio over its hardware interlock line, so the radio sees an ordinary hardware PTT, not TUNE (upstream #6458), and the suppression may not cover it. With an amplifier keyed through the TGXL (radio RCA TX -> TGXL PTT IN, source RCA; TGXL PTT OUT -> amplifier), the TGXL's *PTT OUT when tuning* off keeps the amplifier in bypass while it tunes; an amplifier keyed straight from the radio's RCA TX is keyed during a TGXL autotune whatever the radio's switch. To verify with a tester's TGXL (Hanno Vogels DK3HV): the radio's status during an autotune (`transmit tune=` vs `interlock ... source=`), and the TGXL's `setup read` values. The developer's station has no amplifier or tuner. Not pre-analysed yet.
- ~~The KPA1500's OPERATE not read back in 3 s once~~ *its log lines in build 195* (Rich Yost N2RY, his build 190 log 2026-11-19: at 11:19 and 11:25 OPERATE after a tune showed in 0.04-0.17 s; at 12:02, 4 s after a tune and 40 s after the control link came up again, "has not shown operate in 3 s" with the UDP readings still answering; Rich, mail 2026-11-19 21:04: "I did not press anything on the amp itself", the tune fine). The log cannot tell whether it came later or not at all: to log the OS value last read when the wait ends, and "shows operate after N s" when it comes late.

## Planned: the Digi tab, keyboard modes as one (asked 2026-11-02)

The user: PSK31 too (bpsk31.com), and every keyboard digital mode thought
of as one whole, since they all need the same ways to answer, log and so
on: one Digi button in Controls, the mode chosen under it, each mode with
ready operating macros. FTx stays its own (its traffic runs by itself);
PSK and RTTY follow the same message norms. The user chose step 1 first
and the RTTY / DIGL / DIGU tuning fix to wait for it (no build 146 alone).

Conventions read first: fldigi runs RTTY, PSK31/63 and the rest from one
window with one macro set (its new-install defaults: CQ "CQ CQ CQ de
<MYCALL> x3 pse k", ANS "<CALL> <CALL> de <MYCALL> x3 kn", QSO "<CALL> de
<MYCALL>", KN "btu <NAME> <CALL> de <MYCALL> k", SK "tnx fer QSO <NAME>,
73 ... <CALL> de <MYCALL> sk", Me/Qth, Brag, Log QSO); a callsign clicked
in the received text goes to the log. bpsk31.com: PSK31 on USB, 14.070
and the band list, the same QSO shape ("CQ CQ CQ de CALL CALL CALL K",
"CALL de MYCALL pse K", BTU, SK), 595 instead of an automatic 599 to a
dirty signal, ALC at zero. SmartSDR's RTTY mode transmits the AFSK tones
sent to it (the mark set in its DSP settings), so RTTY goes out as audio
from this app in the RTTY mode, DIGL and DIGU alike, through the path
FT8 / FT4 use (PTT for digital audio, the TX slice, PROC off, the TX
level).

1. Step 1, RTTY (build 146 / beta 59): the RTTY tab becomes Digi; the
   received text with the calls heard (after DE, or twice) as buttons
   that set the call being worked (the Log tab's, as on the CW tab);
   macros like the CW memories (tap sends, press and hold edits; {CALL},
   {HIS}, {NAME} from the lookup, {RST}) with fldigi's set as defaults;
   a field to type and send; Stop; Log QSO (RTTY on the mark). Transmit:
   Baudot with letters / figures shifts and unshift on space, 45.45 baud
   CPFSK at the way's tones (mark the higher radio frequency), a short
   mark lead and tail. Tested: the encoder against the decoder on the
   desktop, every macro and the field by taps, then a live low-power
   transmission heard back (a second receiver or an RTTY skimmer).
2. Step 2, PSK31 / PSK63 (build 147 / beta 60): the mode row on the Digi
   tab; BPSK at 31.25 / 62.5 baud, Varicode, receive with AFC and a tap
   on the waterfall, transmit with the same macros; ADIF PSK / PSK31.
   Tested with test signals, then live on 14.070.
3. Later: contest macros (serial numbers), more modes (Olivia, MFSK...).

## Planned: logging (step 1 and the ADIF share in build 91)

QSO logging from the app to any logbook, through the standard ADIF routes
(analysed 2026-10-18):

1. **Quick log and callsign lookup.** Call and RST typed (or the call
   from a tapped spot); frequency, band, mode, UTC time, power and the own
   station from the radio. Name, QTH, grid and LoTW / eQSL / paper-QSL
   from QRZ.com through the engine's existing `CallsignLookupService`
   (cache, photos, distance and bearing; country, continent and CQ zone
   from cty.dat without a QRZ subscription). The QRZ password goes in the
   platform secure store (the desktop uses QtKeychain). POTA / WWFF
   fields (SIG / SIG_INFO, MY_SIG).
2. **A log on the device and destinations**, any of:
   - WSJT-X UDP "Logged ADIF" (port 2237): MacLoggerDX, Log4OM
     (ADIF_MESSAGE), HRD Logbook (QSO forwarding), GridTracker, JTAlert;
   - plain ADIF over UDP (port 2333, WSJT-X's secondary-server format):
     Log4OM, HRD; N1MM+ to confirm on a real install (ADIF import works);
   - Wavelog / Cloudlog HTTP API (`/api/qso`, a read+write key, the
     station profile from `/api/station_info`), also over mobile data;
   - an ADIF file shared to anything (mail, Files, AirDrop), e.g. LoTW
     through TQSL.
   A queue resends to HTTP destinations when the network returns; UDP
   (no acknowledgement) keeps the device log and a resend. UDP needs the
   logbook's computer on the same network or a VPN.
3. **Later:** Wavelog's `/api/radio` (frequency/mode to Wavelog's own log
   form); spots marked by LoTW users and worked-before
   (`/api/logbook_check_callsign`, upstream idea #2809); the CW decoder's
   text offering the call; QRZ.com logbook upload.

Not planned: LoTW upload from the app (needs TQSL signing with the
operator's certificate; the logbook or Wavelog does it); QSY from Wavelog
to the radio (needs a listening server on the phone, not possible in the
iOS background).

## Planned: two-way logbook sync (asked 2026-11-01)

The user: Wavelog is the main QSO database for many, so the app should
also read from it, and from every logbook it can reach when it starts
(cloud or self-hosted); for desktop programs, a shared cloud folder. The
app would pull the log at start (and after that only what is new), merge it
into its own log without duplicates (Wavelog's own rule: call, band and
mode within 15 minutes, FT4 as MFSK / FT4), so worked-before, New DXCC and
the FTx colours know everything; each QSO logged here keeps going out as
now. What each service offers (checked in their source or docs):
1. **Wavelog** (first): `POST /index.php/api/get_contacts_adif` with the
   existing key (a read-only key works), the station profile from
   `api/station_info/<key>`, and `fetchfromid` for only the new QSOs
   (Wavelog 1.8 and newer; `limit`, JSON with chosen fields from 2.4.1).
   Wavelog 3.1's API v2 (`api/v2/qso`, a `wl2_` token with `qso:read`)
   pages 5000 at a time.
2. **QRZ.com Logbook**: `ACTION=FETCH` with the logbook's key, 250 at a time
   after a log id (needs a QRZ subscription; upload with `ACTION=INSERT`).
3. **LoTW**: `lotwreport.adi` with the LoTW user name and password, QSOs and
   confirmations since the last fetch (the password in the secure store).
4. **A shared ADIF file** in iCloud Drive, Google Drive, Dropbox, OneDrive
   or Nextcloud, picked once: read at start, each new QSO appended, for
   desktop loggers (Log4OM can watch such a file; N1MM, DXKeeper and
   MacLoggerDX have no internet API).
5. **Club Log**: the whole log only (`getadif.php`, email and an app
   password, an API key from Club Log).
6. **Cloudlog**: no download API; worked-before one call at a time
   (`logbook_worked_status`).
Not possible: eQSL (others' cards only), HRDLog (upload only).
Decided (the user, 2026-11-01): Wavelog first, the pulled QSOs a separate
list. Step 1 is built (local, for the next build): Log tab › Wavelog ›
Worked before from the logbook (off by default) reads every station
location's QSOs (station_info, then get_contacts_adif 5000 at a time with
fetchfromid; at each start only the new ones, Read now at once) into
KnownLog (logbook-known.tsv in the app's data folder: call, band, mode,
submode, date, time, CQ zone). They count for worked before and dupes in
every mode, New call / DXCC, the FTx colours and Answer CQ, never go into
the ADIF log nor out again; a QSO the app sent there counts once (call,
band, mode group within 15 minutes). Tested against a mock Wavelog
(paging, two stations, the dedupe, an incremental read, an old Wavelog's
404, an unreachable server) and the user's own Wavelog (19 362 QSOs read
in about 25 s, 650 kB on disk). Steps 2 and 4 too (the user, 2026-11-01:
"kaksisuuntainen qrz lisätään", and a shared folder where a logger has no
cloud sync): QRZ.com Logbook both ways (Log tab: the logbook's API key;
each QSO with ACTION=INSERT, queued, a duplicate counted as sent; Worked
before from the logbook with ACTION=FETCH, 250 a page after the highest
app_qrzlog_logid, the HTML-escaped ADIF; qrzlog-known.tsv), and a shared
ADIF file (Choose… in the system picker: iOS keeps Qt's security-scoped
bookmark, Android takes a lasting read and write grant and appends through
the ContentResolver with "wa", rewriting where a provider cannot append;
read at start and on Read now, each QSO appended, never a new file in its
place; sharedfile-known.tsv keeps its last read). Each contact counts once
across the app's log and all three. Tested against a QRZ.com Logbook
stand-in (a wrong key, paging 250 + 250 + 103, INSERT and a duplicate, an
empty FETCH, Wavelog's and QRZ's copies of one QSO counted once) and on
desktop files (a Log4OM-style file with CRLF and no final newline, an
empty file, a file gone and back); to test on devices: a real QRZ
logbook key, and files in iCloud Drive, Google Drive and OneDrive on an
iPhone, an iPad, a Mac and Android. Next: LoTW confirmations, Club Log.

## Planned: usability (review of 2026-10-29)

From a review of every panel and drawer tab in 20 window sizes (phones
both ways, the iPhone Duo's and OnePlus Open's both screens, iPads, Mac,
split windows) and a look at the other FlexRadio clients. The goal: the
app feels like the phone's own apps. Which build each goes into is
decided one at a time; the ones marked *to discuss* need a closer talk
first.

Touch and feel:
- **Bigger touch areas** for the most used controls: the info row's items
  (slice, TX, speaker, lock, REC, PLAY, NB / NR) are about 22x33 px and
  STEP, ANT and AGC about 24 px tall, where Apple asks for 44 pt and Google
  48 dp. The touch area grows to 44 px; what shows can stay the size it is.
- **One panel base** for every panel: title, Close in the same place
  (top right, as in the AGC, volume, filter and meter panels now; TNF and
  Network still have it at the bottom) and scrolling where the window is
  low.
- **Haptics:** a light tap felt on PTT press and release, the step buttons
  and − / + *in build 137*; a slider's drag and the View › Felt taps
  switch *in build 157*.

Text and large screens:
- **A text scale that follows the system text size** (iOS Dynamic Type,
  Android font scale): sizes are fixed pixels now, about 80 texts are
  11–13 px and the phone's info row about 10 px. One scale in the theme,
  13 px at the smallest. *To discuss:* how far it may grow before rows
  wrap or move to a second line, and checking every layout at the large
  sizes.
- **Tablets and Mac:** the scale stops at 1.15, so a 13" iPad shows
  12–16 px text; the connect page stretches over the full width (a
  maximum width, centred). On a MacBook the window's short side is about
  950–1100 px, so the scale stays near 1.0: phone-sized text, seen from
  twice the distance, and a stretched phone layout; an external monitor
  more so. A Mac window can be any size, so every size in between must
  work too, and a right click acts as the long press. *To discuss:* views
  side by side on a large screen (FTx and the log, two panadapters)
  instead of a larger phone layout.

Finding things:
- ~~A long press on the spectrum opens a menu~~: no menu (2026-10-30).
  Gone through with the user, only the notch and a memory "here" were left
  (a slice, a tap tune, FTx's bars, CW Autotune and zoom have their own
  controls; Mem's Save keeps the frequency being heard). The long press
  instead opens the notch panel for a new notch, set and confirmed with
  Add, as it added notches too easily by accident: *done in build 139*.
  The hidden gestures (the hold for a notch, the dBm scale drag, the
  divider's double tap, the SPLIT and memory holds, a tap that resets a
  value) go into the first-run hints.
- **First-run hints:** four short cards after the first connect *in
  build 157* (View › Hints shows them again).

Screen reader:
- **VoiceOver and TalkBack:** names for every control *in build 139*
  (290 on the FLEX, none without; 18 before), a double tap acting, a
  swipe stepping a slider. Tuning with the screen reader (the
  frequency an adjustable control, a swipe up or down = one step, read
  out) was in build 156 and is taken out again: the user's device test
  found dialogs that trap the reader (see Later: Screen readers).
  *To discuss:* with a tester who uses a screen reader.

Small ones: done in build 138 ("In use by" on SmartLink cards, 4.5:1
tags, scroll bars wherever a page scrolls).

Later, *to discuss:* a connection doctor (what blocks a connection:
the local network permission, the route, SmartLink, the radio's client
slots, in plain words).

Proposed order (a proposal, each build still decided on its own):
1. **Build A**, small but felt: the info row's touch areas, one panel
   base, haptics on PTT and the steps. *In build 137* (the header about
   30 px taller: 36 / 40 px rows, not a full 44).
2. **Build B:** the text scale with the system text size, the tablet
   scale, the connect page's maximum width, "In use by". *The connect
   page's width and "In use by" in build 138.*
3. **Build C:** first-run hints (the hidden gestures), tuning with the
   screen reader (its names, and the notch confirmation instead of the
   long-press menu, in build 139).
4. **Later:** views side by side on a tablet, the connection doctor.

## Released

**Build 142** (2026-11-01), iPhone and iPad, and Android beta 56:
- Backspace on a Mac (Pentti Pärnänen OH6PP, build 140: typing worked, Backspace did nothing). With the input method off, Qt's UIKit key mapping sends Backspace as key 8 with the text "\b", which a text field ignores; an application filter (AppleControlKeys) sends key 8 and 127 on as Qt::Key_Backspace, which also gives an iPad keyboard's Backspace to the frequency keypad. Checked with a new render tool command (keys, with \b sent the way UIKit sends it): "OH6PPX" became "OH6P", and stayed "OH6PPX" without the filter.
- The Mac text box no longer opens on a double click (Pentti): the second click's release did not always reach the field, so the hold timer ran on; any release anywhere and a double click stop it, and a press soon after a release arms nothing. Only a Mac shows the box, so not testable here.
- QRZ.com not answering (Sloan Davis, iPad Pro, build 140: "Socket timing out"): the account was saved, only the check after Save timed out (the user's Android phone timed out in the same hour; no other tester reported it). A network failure now shows in amber that the account is saved and lookups will try again; a refusal stays red ("did not accept the sign-in").
- The manual's Operating by mode (the user: a picture of every mode and the steps for every feature by mode): SSB, CW, AM and SAM, FM with repeaters, FT8 / FT4, RTTY, RADE and what works in every mode, eight new pictures rendered against the FLEX by update-manual-screenshots.sh (its new "modes" plan).
- Checked against build 141's sweep: 27 sizes, no layout decision changed (layout-diff clean; the info panel cut on phones on their side as before); the pictures differ only in the band's signals. Build 142's sweep is the new baseline.

**Build 141** (2026-11-01), iPhone and iPad, and Android beta 55:
- RTTY the right way round: the radio's RTTY mode is LSB (mark 2125, space 2295 Hz in the audio), and build 140 decoded nothing until Reverse. A tap on a signal, an RTTY spot or the panel's Autotune tunes onto the mark (SpectrumItem::rttyMarkNear: two peaks a shift apart; a spot retries for 8 s while the display fills), and an RTTY spot sets the radio's RTTY mode (it was DIGL). Squelch (on by default) holds back text below the decoder's confidence. Checked live in a Sunday RTTY contest (R8UT, UN8PT, RA6LIS, SP4NKJ decoded; a spot and Autotune from the space tone landed on the mark).
- No burst of band noise at the start of a transmission (Jeff Stinger, Pixel 7; the user heard it on Android beta 54 too): the microphone opens about 100 ms after the press and the radio keys 150-400 ms after it, and until then a phone's speaker played receive into the microphone. Receive now goes silent at the press (AudioEngine::setRxDucked; Qt 6.12 applies the volume as AAudio pulls from its buffer, so what is queued goes silent too) and comes back at the release, or with MON on 250 ms after the radio keys. Checked on the FLEX with a silent USB PTT, MON off and on; not yet with a real speaker and microphone.
- USB-C headsets on Android (Jeff Stinger): Qt 6.12.0's QtAudioDeviceManager does not know TYPE_USB_HEADSET, so Qt opened the speaker and the built-in microphone. A patched copy (mobile/android/qt-patches) is compiled into Qt's Qt6AndroidMultimedia.jar when the Android build is configured; plugging a headset in or out reopens receive.
- ADIF import on Android (Kurt DL5EAG): Qt turned the *.adi filter into application/octet-stream, so an ADIF file stored as text (Drive) was greyed out; no filter now.
- Transmit lock (Larry Averbeck): Controls › TX › Lock, off by default. In standby (SB) every way to transmit is refused (PTT, the CW keys and paddles, CW text and memories, TUNE, the ATU, voice macros, the radio's voice keyer, CQ, a tapped decode); a second's hold on PTT, KEY, Space, TUNE or CQ arms it (OA m:ss) for five minutes after the last transmission. Checked on the FLEX without transmitting, on the iPad and a phone both ways.
- FTx Answer CQ (Kurt DL5EAG): the app answers CQs by itself while no QSO or CQ of ours runs: a CQ on the TX slice that Show passes, not worked on this band and mode, the rarest first; five calls a station, then the next (a station called is left alone for 30 minutes); directed CQs only where we are them, contest CQs never. The presence limit (1-60 min, the user: each operator sets it) stops Answer CQ and Keep CQ after that long without a touch on the app. Live on 20 m at 10 W: OM7AMA, EX7CQ and HA7NB worked and logged with nobody touching anything; two stations got five calls and the next was taken; a 1-minute limit switched it off.
- FT8 / FT4: a QSO ended when the transmission under way ended, even if that was still the previous message, so a reply decoded after our slot had begun lost its RR73 or 73; it ends after the closing message itself now.
- Checked against build 139's sweep (no build 140 sweep was kept; build 141's is now the baseline): 27 sizes, no layout decision changed by the code. The differences came from the radio's state: the slice shown was not the TX slice this time, so its TX tag (and 27-37 px of the info panel) was gone and the panel's scale rose a little. The FTx CQ row with Answer CQ checked at 360, 390 and 844 px wide and in the iPad's panel, with More too.

**Build 136** (2026-10-28), iPhone and iPad, and Android beta 50:
- Android: the connection dropped in the background (beta 49, 22:26, the user's OnePlus Open in the middle of an FT8 QSO; the "GUI thread stalled (app not in front)" open since beta 42). The log showed Inactive, the surface gone, then "called createSurface(), waiting for Surface to be ready..." and the GUI thread stalled 29 s; the radio closed the connection after 15 s without pings. Qt 6.12's Android window, on Suspended, destroys the surface and then sends an unexposing expose and waits for the render thread; a frame that thread was drawing at that moment found the surface marked not created, asked for a new one and waited for it, which only came when the app returned. The app now unexposes its windows synchronously in an event filter on the application state change event, which Qt sends before the signal its window acts on: the render thread finishes its frame (the old surface still marked created, so it fails fast) and stops before the surface goes. Compiled against the desktop Qt 6.12 (the filter fires before the signal); on the device it is still to be seen.
- The tablet page keeps the info panel beside the frequency, as in landscape (the user: upright, the OnePlus inner screen's top was "miten sattuu"): where the row is short of room, the frequency and the panel's type shrink to fit (676 of 700 upright on the OnePlus, 97 %; the iPhone Duo upright 68 %), measured from two hidden probes at 100 % and 70 % so the scale does not feed back into its own measure; under the frequency only below 66 %. The panel's paddings shrink with its type. Rendered at 698x668, 751x616, 626x812, 890x606, 1024x768 (pinned), 600x834, 640x700, 1366x1024 and the phones 390x844, 406x903, 844x390, 533x505.
- FTx: the time line under PSK Reporter always shows (the user: on iOS it never did, the clock being a few ms off): the server and the device clock's offset in ms, amber from 0.3 s; before the first answer, that FT8 / FT4 run on the device's clock.
